________________


The Weight of Wings
They told me I was born to fly,
But they never warned me about the weight of wings.
How they stretch wide,
Casting shadows over the ground I long to touch.
How they ache with the memory of every fall,
Every time I leapt and the sky refused to catch me.
I have carried these wings like a burden,
Their feathers heavy with the dust of dreams deferred.
I have tried to fold them,
To hide them beneath the armor of my doubt,
But they always unfurl,
Reaching for a freedom I am too afraid to claim.
What they don’t tell you about flying
Is that it’s not the wind that lifts you 
It’s the courage to leap,
To trust the air to hold you,
Even when the ground feels safer.
And so, I leap again,
Not because I am fearless,
But because I am tired of the weight.
Because I have learned that the only way
To lighten the burden of wings
Is to use them.
Reflection:
 This poem speaks to the tension between fear and freedom, the struggle to embrace our potential despite the weight of past failures. It reminds me that courage isn’t the absence of fear it’s the decision to leap anyway. The wings we carry may feel heavy, but they are also the tools that will set us free.





Home and Belonging
Introduction:
 What does it mean to belong? Where do we find home? This section explores the search for a place physical or spiritual where one feels truly accepted. These poems journey through the landscapes of longing, the places we've never been but somehow recognize, and the possibility that home might not be a destination but a feeling we carry within. They speak to the universal human desire to find where we truly belong.


Where the Wind Knows My Name"
I’ve walked through rooms that don’t echo
Like I’m a shadow, cast too far from the light.
My hands have touched the walls,
But they’ve never felt like mine.
I’ve slept in beds made of strangers' sheets,
Hoping the fabric would whisper where I belong.
My heart beats like a traveler’s compass 
Pointing in every direction,
But never landing.
The world tells me I am “lost,”
But I don’t feel that way.
I feel like I am simply
Moving through rooms,
Moving through time,
Moving toward the scent of something familiar
I’ve never known.
Maybe home isn’t a place,
Maybe it’s the sound of my name
Carried by the wind,
Where I don’t have to ask if I’m wanted
Because the air already knows.
It’s waiting to wrap me in its arms,
And say, “You are mine.”










________________


"The Places We Are Not"
Sometimes I dream of places I’ve never been,
And they feel more like home than the places I’ve known.
A street lit by lanterns where I never feel alone,
A kitchen where the smell of bread rises
And settles over me like warmth from a mother’s touch.
But those places are only shadows in my mind,
The ones that whisper in the quiet of the night.
In the morning, they fade like dew on grass,
Leaving only the cold of reality behind.
I search for them in crowded cities,
Where noise drowns the quiet kind of peace I’m chasing.
I search for them in faces that never see me
And places that don’t know my story.
It’s not the walls or roads that make a home 
It’s the knowing.
The knowing that when you arrive,
You’re not a visitor,
Not a stranger passing through,
But someone whose absence has been felt,
Whose return is welcomed with open arms.
And until then, I’ll keep chasing
The places that hold me even when I’m not there,
The ones that make me feel like I’ve always belonged.
Even when I’m still finding my way.


________________


"What If Home Was Inside Us All Along?"
I’ve been chasing maps,
Seeking the edges of the earth,
Thinking that home is out there somewhere,
Just beyond the horizon.
But every road I’ve traveled
Leads back to the mirror,
Where I see myself 
The same lost soul,
Hoping to find something that would make me whole.
What if home isn’t a city?
What if it isn’t a house with four walls?
What if it’s just the silence between thoughts,
The quiet within me,
The space where I don’t need to prove anything?
I close my eyes and feel the air of the world,
The way it moves through me,
Like I’m both the wind and the place it flows.
Maybe home isn’t the place I want to arrive at,
But the place I already am.
Maybe home is the acceptance
That I don’t need to run anymore.
I open my eyes and realize
That I have been home all along.
It was never out there.
It was always inside.
Reflection
I’ve been searching for a place where I fit, where I don’t have to carve out space for myself because it’s already there, waiting. I feel like home is somewhere just outside of reach, somewhere not defined by walls but by the way it feels when the world stops asking me to prove I belong. Maybe that’s what it’s all about the moment the universe stops questioning you and just accepts you, like the wind knows your name before you do.
________________


The Door to the Future"
I walked through the door of now,
knowing it would never look the same again.
The past, with all its weight,
clung to my ankles like sand,
but I kicked it off,
one step at a time.
I do not need to know what lies ahead
to walk into it with open eyes.
The future has no face
until I give it one.
And so, I take the next step,
not to outrun fear,
but to embrace it as a companion
along the road of becoming.
Reflection:
These poems, rooted in the themes of power, truth, and personal evolution, mirror the delicate balance between silence and action, vulnerability and strength. In each piece, I explore the tension between holding space and asserting dominance, choosing when to reveal and when to conceal. The journey within these verses reflects the intricacies of navigating both internal and external worlds recognizing the power of words, the influence of silence, and the beauty in the pause before a decision is made. The path is not always clear, nor is it linear, but in the reflection of each poem, I find the truth that we shape our future through the choices we make today, the restraint we practice, and the awareness we cultivate. Power lies not in domination, but in the wisdom to know when to speak and when to be still.
________________
Stories of Pain and Redemption
Introduction:
 This final section delves into the darker landscapes of the human experience pain, trauma, violence, and the possibility of redemption. Through metaphor and vivid imagery, these poems explore the shadows within and around us, the weight of consequence, and the complex journey toward healing. They remind us that even in our darkest moments, there remains the possibility of light, of understanding, and of transformation.
The Silence Between Breaths
The Mind of a Killer
The air thickens before the strike,
A thousand thoughts swim like blood in my veins,
Each breath is a drum, pounding,
Not for mercy, but for meaning.
I wear silence like a crown,
A stillness born of pain.
The heartbeat of my past echoes in my chest,
A reminder of the lives I’ve touched and lost.
But am I the sculptor, or the tool?
Do I shape them, or do they shape me?
I cut away the world’s noise,
Leaving only the sharp edge of truth.
In their eyes, I see reflections,
Whispers of my own fractures,
In their scream, I hear the voice of my own undoing.
Every movement is deliberate,
A story told with the precision of a blade.
Every life, a page turned,
Every death, a sentence written.
Yet, I am not the author.
The book is already written,
Bound in the blood of a thousand forgotten souls.
They are the ones who left their mark on me,
Who whispered their secrets as they bled.
I am the keeper of stories 
I do not choose the ending.
The moment of impact is where truth resides,
For in death, all things are revealed.
The only question remains 
Do I remember their names?


The Hands of Time
The Clock Ticks on the Battlefield of Memory
I remember every hand I've touched,
Not in love, but in the grip of fate.
The same hands that pull the trigger
Once cradled innocence,
But innocence is fragile,
A butterfly crushed by the weight of inevitability.
Time is a thief that robs me of my youth,
But I never was a child, was I?
Born in shadow,
Raised in the deafening hum of war,
Each tick of the clock a call to arms.
I’ve learned to ignore the hands that point at me.
Their accusations don’t sting,
Because I know their story 
It’s the same one I carry.


________________


 The Veil of Shadows
The Faces in the Dark
There are faces that haunt me,
Not in my dreams, but in the waking world.
Faces I cannot place,
Yet I know their eyes,
Their blood, their fears.
They are the ones I’ve swallowed whole,
Their spirits twisting inside me
Like vines around my soul.
I wear their faces like a mask,
Shadows whispering their truths to me.
They show me what I’ve done,
And ask if I can forgive myself.
They know I am nothing but a reflection
Of a world too cold to care.
They see the truth 
I am only a ghost,
Haunting my own heart.


________________


 The River of Red
The Weight of Consequence
A river flows through my veins,
Carrying the red of those I’ve taken,
A current of guilt and power,
Churning beneath my skin.
The water is thick,
It clings to me,
It stains me,
But it is mine 
Mine to drink,
Mine to drown in. I thought it would cleanse me.
I thought I could wash away
The stains of my past.
But the more I try to cleanse,
The more it pulls me under.
The river does not forgive.
It carves its way forward,
Dragging everything in its path.
And I am the river,
And I am the stone sinking at its bottom.
________________


The Burial Ground of Broken Souls
The Final Rest
In the soil, they sleep 
The ones whose names have never been spoken,
Whose lives were taken before they could begin.
I remember each of them,
Not as victims, but as echoes.
For the ground does not forgive 
It holds us in a chokehold,
In its silence,
Until we are forgotten. But I cannot forget.
Each grave is a mirror,
Reflecting the damage I’ve done,
The lives I’ve torn from the earth,
And buried beneath the weight of my guilt.
I walk the cemetery,
Not in sorrow,
But in acceptance.
For I am not the keeper of the dead 
I am one of them,
Only waiting for the day
I am buried too.
________________
The Truth We Bury
They told us the truth was a monument,
Carved in stone, unshakable, eternal.
But I have walked among the ruins,
And I have seen the cracks.
They told us the truth was a light,
Blinding and pure,
But I have stood in its glare,
And found shadows hiding behind it.
They told us the truth was a weapon,
Sharp enough to cut through lies,
But I have held it in my hands,
And felt it slice through me instead.
This generation was born into a world of mirrors,
Where every reflection is distorted,
Where every voice is an echo of another,
And the truth is buried beneath the noise.
But listen 
The truth is not in the monuments,
Not in the light,
Not in the weapon.
The truth is in the quiet places,
The spaces between breaths,
The moments when the world stops screaming
And you can finally hear your own heart.
The truth is not what they give you,
It is what you find when everything else is stripped away.
It is the courage to ask the questions
They told you not to ask.
It is the strength to stand in the silence
And face what you’ve been running from.
The truth is not a thing to be held,
It is a thing to be lived.
It is the way you choose to love
When hate is easier.
It is the way you choose to hope
When despair feels safer.
It is the way you choose to rise
When the world tells you to kneel.
We are the generation of seekers,
Of builders,
Of breakers.
We will not find the truth in their monuments,
But in the rubble we sift through,
In the stories we tell,
In the lives we live.
The truth is not a destination.
It is the journey we take together,
The light we carry in the dark,
The fire we pass from hand to hand
Until the lies burn away
And only the truth remains.
Reflection:
 This poem is a call to a generation searching for meaning in a world of contradictions. It reminds us that truth is not something handed down it is something we must uncover for ourselves. It is not static or simple, but alive, dynamic, and deeply personal. In a time of overwhelming noise, this poem invites us to listen, to question, and to live with integrity. It is a reminder that the truth is not found in the world’s monuments, but in the quiet courage of our own hearts.








Epilogue: The Boy Becomes
I have written myself into existence,
letter by letter, line by line,
leaving pieces of my soul between these pages 
unfolding, unbecoming, becoming.
I have learned that silence can be survival,
but so can the sound of my own voice.
That I am more than the echoes of my ancestors’ sorrow,
more than the fears the world projected onto me.
I have seen my reflection splinter in the weight of expectation,
but I have also seen it rise, whole,
no longer a question, no longer an apology 
but a statement, a presence, a name that refuses to be forgotten.
I do not write for permission.
I do not exist for approval.
I am the dream and the dreamer,
the poem and the poet,
the truth they tried to rewrite,
still standing, still speaking.
And if the world asks who I am,
let these words be my answer:
I am here. I am whole. I am free.






                                                                                                     The End


















About the Author
Kiminou is a poet and storyteller whose work explores the depths of Black identity, resilience, and emotional truth. With a distinct voice that blends lyrical beauty with raw honesty, Kiminou crafts narratives that challenge, heal, and inspire. Their writing is deeply rooted in cultural history, personal experience, and the unspoken stories of the Black community.
Black Boy Poems is a reflection of that commitment an evocative collection that captures the complexities of Black boyhood, manhood, and the spaces in between. Through powerful imagery and poignant storytelling, Kiminou sheds light on the struggles, triumphs, and enduring strength found within Black life.
Their work serves as both a mirror and a call to action, encouraging readers to embrace their truths and engage with the world in deeper, more meaningful ways.

 Our Father
                                           


  


Kiminou Knox


How Can You Be My Shepherd When I Keep Walking Off Cliffs?
I've felt Your staff against my skin,
The curved wood promising protection,
Yet here I stand at the precipice again,
Wind howling through the hollow of my chest,
Stones crumbling beneath uncertain feet,
The valley below dark with possibilities,
My shadow stretches toward the fall.
They told me You watch Your flock,
That not one lamb escapes Your gaze,
But my wool is matted with thorns and mud,
And I've wandered paths You never cleared,
Following the scent of something wild,
Something that calls louder than Your voice 
Did You let me go, or did I break free?
The earth gives way like promises,
Each step forward a negotiation with gravity,
While You remain somewhere distant,
Perhaps watching, perhaps turned away,
Your eyes on more obedient sheep,
Who don't question the narrowness of Your path,
Who doesn't yearn for what lies beyond the fence.
I've tasted the salt of a thousand tears,
Watched the sun rise on mornings after falling,
My bones broken then mended crooked,
Each scar a map of where You weren't 
Or perhaps where You were testing,
The limits of faith against the pull of earth,
The strength of belief against the weight of doubt.
Shepherd, if that's truly what You are,
Why do I keep finding these edges?
These dangerous perimeters of existence
Where the ground no longer holds,
Where Your green pastures give way
To barren rock and empty air 
Is this freedom or abandonment?
The wind speaks in tongues I almost understand,
Carrying whispers of other fallen sheep,
Who walked off cliffs with eyes wide open,
Not in despair but in terrible curiosity,
Wondering if You would catch them,
Or if the falling itself might be
The answer they'd been seeking all along.
If You are my Shepherd,
why does the void mouth my name
like an old hymn?
Why do my feet ache for the cliff’s edge
when a whisper could have held me still?
Did You carve this hunger into my bones,
or did I teach myself to starve


________________


Why Can't I Hear You When I Need You Most?
In the dark cathedral of midnight panic,
When my lungs forget their holy purpose,
And my heart drums against my chest
Like it's trying to break free from prison,
I call Your name into the void,
But my voice echoes back alone,
A mockery of prayer, hollow and unanswered.
I've pressed my ear to the ground,
Listening for Your footsteps approaching.
I've climbed to mountain peaks,
Where they say heaven touches earth.
I've knelt until my knees bled devotion,
Yet Your voice remains a memory,
Or worse a story someone else told me.
The prophets claim You spoke in fire,
In whirlwinds, in the splitting of seas,
That You thundered commandments from clouds,
That You whispered in still, small voices.
But where is that voice when darkness swallows me?
When grief carves canyons through my chest?
When doubt becomes my only companion?
They say You're a father who gives good gifts,
But what child cries out without response?
What parent stands silent behind a locked door
While their child screams with nightmares?
Is this some divine test of endurance?
Or have You simply turned Your face away,
Attending prayers more worthy than mine?
I built altars from the ruins of my expectations,
Sacrificing certainty on faith’s pyre 
Burning every bridge to comfort,
Just to hear one syllable from You.
Yet the heavens stay sealed,
A bronze sky reflecting only my own face,
While my questions circle like starving birds.
The silence between stars stretches vast,
The space between prayer and answer wider still,
And I wonder if You're testing the tensile strength
Of a faith pulled taut to breaking point,
Or if this is just how god's love from distance,
Watching us struggle to interpret silence,
Finding meaning in cosmic indifference.
When I needed You most, where were Your words?
When my world collapsed into ash and bone,
When betrayal made a home in my heart,
When loneliness became my only language 
Was Your silence meant to teach or punish?
Or is the terrible truth much simpler:
You were speaking, but not to me?
________________


Why Does It Feel Like I'm Talking to Myself?
My prayers rise like smoke from a dying fire,
Curling toward a ceiling of indifference,
Dissipating before they reach Your ears 
If You have ears at all, if You're there at all.
Each word feels heavier than the last,
Dragged from the depths of dwindling hope,
Landing with a thud at my own feet.
I've crafted beautiful cathedrals of language,
Soaring arches of praise and supplication,
Stained glass windows of confession,
Intricate mosaics of gratitude and need.
Yet when I step back to marvel at my creation,
I find myself alone in the sanctuary,
The only witness to my own devotion.
They say prayer changes things 
But what if the only change is in me?
What if I'm reshaping myself around the silence,
Learning to mistake echo for answer,
Interpreting coincidence as intervention,
Finding patterns in randomness,
Convincing myself I'm not alone in this room?
The walls of my skull enclose these thoughts,
Bouncing back every question I hurl at You,
Until I can no longer distinguish
Between Your voice and my own conscience,
Between divine wisdom and human rationalization,
Between heavenly comfort and self soothing lies 
Have I created You in my image instead?
My knees bear the marks of devotion,
My throat raw from whispered pleas,
My journal filled with one sided conversations,
Yet I cannot shake this growing suspicion
That prayer is simply the mind talking to itself,
The heart comforting its own wounds,
The soul pretending it's not ultimately alone.
Sometimes in the depths of night,
When even the stars seem to have turned away,
I catch myself listening for a response
That part of me knows it will never come.
Is faith just the stubborn refusal to accept
That the universe isn't listening, doesn't care,
That we're shouting into our own cupped hands?
Still I speak Your name into the darkness,
Still I pour out my heart like water,
Still I wait for the moment when the monologue
Might suddenly become a conversation.
But until then, I wonder with each prayer:
Am I communing with the divine,
Or just getting better at talking to myself?
________________
________________
If You Hold My Future, Why Can't You Show Me A Glimpse?
The road ahead disappears into fog,
Each step a negotiation with the unseen,
While You supposedly stand at the end,
Holding a map I'm not allowed to read.
I move forward on trembling legs,
Faith a blindfold I'm told to wear proudly,
Uncertainty disguised as spiritual virtue.
They say You've counted every hair on my head,
That You knew me before I was formed,
That my days were written in Your book
Before one of them came to be.
If so, then why this cruel suspense?
This cosmic game of blindman's bluff,
Where do I grope forward while You watch?
I've seen fortune tellers with crystal balls,
Weather forecasters with satellite views,
Doctors with machines that peer inside the body,
Scientists who predict the paths of stars 
Yet the God who shaped the cosmos
Can't spare a flashlight beam of foresight
For a child lost in the woods of indecision?
Is it kindness that keeps You silent,
Some divine wisdom that knows
I couldn't bear the weight of foreknowledge?
Or do You enjoy the spectacle of our stumbling,
Our desperate attempts to discern Your will
From tea leaves, coincidences, and the wind,
Our meaning making from meaningless signs?
The prophets claimed to see what's coming,
Spoke of lions lying with lambs,
Of swords beaten into plowshares,
Of tears wiped away forever.
But what about my small, mortal future?
The choices that keep me awake at night?
The crossroads where I stand paralyzed?
I'm not asking for the full revelation,
Just a glimpse to know I'm not lost,
A whispered hint of what awaits,
A shadow of purpose cast on my path.
Instead, You offer parables about birds,
About lilies that neither toil nor spin,
About treasures in heaven I cannot see.
If my future rests secure in Your hands,
If every step is divinely orchestrated,
Then why does each choice feel like a gamble?
Why does regret follow me like a shadow?
Why does anxiety gnaw at the edges of faith,
Unless the terrible truth is simpler than they say:
You know as little about tomorrow as I do?
________________


Did You Make Me Too Weak To Follow You?
My spirit willing but this flesh so frail,
Bones like brittle branches in winter,
Heart a wild creature I cannot tame,
Mind a labyrinth I get lost within.
Did You fashion me from inferior clay?
Mix too much dust with too little breath?
Make me a vessel certain to crack?
I watch others walk Your path with ease,
Their faith seemingly untroubled by doubt,
Their morality uncomplicated by desire,
Their service is untainted by selfish motives.
Did You give them stronger materials?
Better instructions? Clearer vision?
Or am I looking at masks, not faces?
The weight of Your commandments
Presses down like stones on my chest.
I try to lift them, to carry them forward,
But my arms shake, my back bends,
My knees buckle under the holy burden.
Did You design me to fail these tests?
Create me too weak to earn Your love?
I've fallen so many times I've lost count,
Each resolution shattered by sunrise,
Each promise broken before the day ends,
Each attempt at righteousness stained.
Is this the destiny You wrote for me?
This constant cycle of try and fail,
Of reach and fall, of hope and despair?
The saints speak of transformation,
Of putting off the old self like a garment,
Of being renewed, remade, reborn.
But what if my fabric is inherently flawed?
What if the pattern You cut me from
Was destined for beauty but marked for breaking,
For falling short of glory by design?
You ask me to be perfect as You are perfect,
To be holy as You are holy,
To love as You have loved.
But You made me limited, finite, human 
With eyes that cannot see past horizons,
A heart that bleeds when barely scratched,
And a will that bends like grass in the wind.
Did You create me too weak to follow,
Or is my weakness itself the path?
Is it in breaking that I become whole?
In falling I learn to rise?
In failing that I find Your grace?
Or is that just another story I tell myself
To make sense of a design beyond understanding?
________________
If You're The Answer, Why Do I Have So Many Questions?
They hand me Your name like a skeleton key,
Promising it will unlock every door,
Solve every puzzle, heal every wound,
End every argument, calm every fear.
"God is the answer," they say with certainty,
As if Your name is the period
At the end of a perfect sentence.
But each answer You provide
Spawns a hundred new questions,
Each revelation casts shadows
I hadn't noticed before.
Each scripture opens trapdoors
Beneath what I thought was solid ground 
If You're the answer,
Why am I drowning in questions?
The more I learn of Your ways,
The less I understand Your nature.
You command love but permit suffering,
Demand justice but extend mercy,
Create beauty but allow destruction,
Promise presence but feel absent,
Reveal truth wrapped in riddles.
Each prayer answered leaves me wondering
About a thousand prayers were ignored.
Each miracle witnessed makes me question
Why does such power stay so hidden?
Each glimpse of Your goodness forces me to ask
About evil that thrives unchecked 
Are You the answer, or the greatest question of all?
The faithful tell me to stop asking,
To accept mystery as a gift,
To embrace paradox as divine,
To see questions as a lack of faith.
But if You made me in Your image,
Didn't You include this restless mind,
This hunger to understand what lies beyond?
Scientists dissect the cosmos,
Philosophers probe existence,
Artists capture glimpses of transcendence,
All seeking answers to ancient questions.
Did You plant this curiosity in our souls,
Then command us not to use it?
Create a lock but forbid the key?
If You truly are the answer,
The omega to every alpha,
The destination of every journey,
The fulfillment of every longing 
Then why does finding You
Only multiply my questions?
Why does Your light reveal more darkness?
________________


Are You Lost Too?
Sometimes I wonder, in the quietest moments,
When the world sleeps and doubts awaken,
If You're searching for something too.
If beyond the throne and the glory
And the angels' songs,
There's a restlessness in Your infinity,
A question mark in Your omniscience,
A loneliness even creation couldn't fill.
What if Your silence isn't strategic or punitive,
But the sound of Your own uncertain breathing?
What if Your hiddenness isn't a test of our faith,
But evidence of Your own cosmic wandering?
What if the distance we feel isn't discipline,
But the space You need to figure things out,
To come to terms with what You've made?
They say You never change, never waver,
That Your course was set before time began,
That every sparrow's fall is choreographed,
Every galaxy's spiral is predetermined.
But what a terrible burden that would be 
To never discover, never wonder, never grow,
To have all answers but ask no questions.
If You're lost too, somehow,
In the infinite possibilities of existence,
In the labyrinth of free will You bestowed,
In the mystery of love that even God must learn,
Then perhaps our wandering isn't failure.
Perhaps it's inheritance, perhaps it's design,
Perhaps it's how we're most like You.
________________


Why Does Every Blessing Feel Like A Curse?
You give me love wrapped in barbed wire,
Joy that bleeds at its edges,
Honey with a bitter aftertaste.
Every gift You place in my hands
Carries the weight of what it will cost me.
The child You blessed me with
Taught me to fear death in new ways.
The success You granted
Revealed the hollow in my chest
Where contentment should have lived.
You offer water to the thirsty
Then drown them in the flood.
You provide shelter from the storm
Then tear the roof away
Just as thunder cracks the sky.
Is this Your divine comedy 
To watch us clutch Your blessings
Only to discover they're disguised afflictions?
Your gifts arrive as Trojan horses,
Beautiful shells concealing their true nature.
What kind of love gives with one hand
While the other prepares to take?
What lesson hides in this twisted symmetry
Where every height reached
Promises a greater fall to come?
I hold Your blessings like fragile birds,
Afraid to love them fully,
Knowing how Your generosity works 
That everything given contains its opposite,
A curse waiting to be revealed.
Who taught You this terrible mathematics
Where joy plus time always equals loss?
Did You design us to never rest in goodness,
To always search the horizon for the coming storm,
Even as we stand in the warm light of Your grace?
________________


How Much Pain Is Enough?
I've swallowed oceans of suffering,
Felt my bones crack under the weight of Your lessons,
Watched my skin grow thick with scars 
Your fingerprints left where You've touched me.
How much more must I endure
Before You're satisfied?
You speak of refinement by fire,
But the flames have consumed me entirely.
What remains to be purified
When I am nothing but ash
Scattered across the wasteland of my faith?
Is there a ledger where You mark my pain,
A cosmic threshold I must reach
Before You finally say, "Enough"?
Or is suffering itself the point 
The endless circle You've designed for me?
They say gold is tested in fire,
But gold doesn't feel the burning.
I am not metal but flesh and spirit,
Crying out beneath Your hammer,
Begging for the shaping to cease.
I've seen children broken beyond repair,
Saints who died questioning Your name,
Faithful servants consumed by doubt.
Does their agony satisfy Your appetite,
Or do You still hunger for more?
Each time I think I've reached the limit,
The ground beneath me gives way
To deeper chambers of suffering.
Is this Your idea of growth 
This endless descent into pain?
When will You look upon what remains of me
And declare the work complete?
Or is completion itself a myth,
Another cruel promise to keep us hoping
As the suffering stretches into eternity?


________________
Why Did You Give Me Dreams If You Planned To Crush Them?
You planted seeds of vision in my heart,
Whispered possibilities in my eager ears,
Showed me glimpses of what could be.
Then I watched You systematically destroy
Each tender shoot that dared emerge.
Was this always Your intention 
To make me architect of castles in the air,
Only to position me here on solid ground
Where I can watch them collapse,
Too distant to save, too close to ignore?
I followed stars You hung specifically for me,
Trusted the map You placed in my hands,
Only to find myself in unmarked wilderness,
The path behind me erased,
The stars suddenly extinguished.
What divine purpose exists
In this elaborate game of creation and destruction?
Do You delight in watching hope bloom
Only to witness the precise moment
When it withers under reality's harsh sun?
I've buried so many dreams now
I could start a cemetery.
Each grave marked with Your name and mine 
Cocreators of visions
Neither of us intended to preserve.
My hands, once open to receive Your promises,
Now curl into fists against my chest,
Protecting what little remains.
Is this what You wanted all along 
To teach me to stop dreaming entirely?
Or perhaps this is Your twisted kindness 
Showing me that dreams themselves are the problem,
That wanting is the true sin,
That the only safe path through Your world
Is to desire nothing at all.


________________
Why Do You Break What You Claim To Love?
Your love is a hurricane that claims to shelter me
While tearing my foundations apart.
You call Yourself Father while orphaning my hopes,
Healer while inflicting fresh wounds,
Savior while watching me drown.
I've studied the mathematics of Your affection 
How it multiplies my pain under the guise of growth,
Divides me from myself in the name of wholeness,
Subtracts my joy while promising abundance,
Adds burden after burden while whispering,
"My yoke is easy."
The potter shouldn't resent the clay for breaking
When it's the potter's hands that apply the pressure.
You shape me with force that cracks my essence,
Then seem surprised when I cannot hold water,
When I leak from the places You've damaged.
Is this love this constant reconstruction?
This endless cycle of breaking and remaking,
As if I am never quite right,
Never finished enough to deserve gentleness,
Never whole enough to escape Your improving touch?
The mother eagle pushes her young from the nest, they say,
But she doesn't break their wings first.
The shepherd leaves ninetynine to find one,
But doesn't drive the sheep away himself.
What kind of love deliberately wounds what it cherishes?
I wonder sometimes if You understand love at all,
Or if Your divine perspective has forgotten
How fragile human hearts can be,
How we crack under the weight of Your attention,
How Your loving touch leaves bruises we carry for years.
Do You break us because You love us,
Or do You love us because we break?
Is our fragility what attracts Your gaze,
Our tendency to shatter what draws Your hand,
Is our brokenness the only thing You truly cherish?


________________
Am I Being Refined Or Am I Being Destroyed?
The line between purification and annihilation
Grows thinner with each passing day.
I feel myself dissolving in the acid bath of Your lessons,
Watching pieces of who I was float away,
Wondering if anything will remain when You're finished.
The blacksmith knows precisely how much heat
The metal can withstand before it's ruined.
Do You possess such knowledge about my soul,
Or are You experimenting as You go,
Curious to see my breaking point?
I was told this pain would refine me,
That suffering would burn away impurities,
Leaving something precious behind.
But what if there's nothing left but the burning?
What if I am fuel, not gold?
Those who speak of spiritual refinement
Have never felt their very self being consumed.
They imagine gentle remodeling,
Not this violent unmaking
That leaves me unrecognizable even to myself.
Friends tell me to trust the process,
To surrender to Your divine metallurgy.
But they cannot see inside the furnace,
Cannot feel the core of me melting away,
Cannot tell if this is transformation or obliteration.
Sometimes in the depths of night,
I sense You watching the flames,
Your face is illuminated by my burning.
Are You the master craftsman at work,
Or a child fascinated by destruction?
I want to believe this crucible has purpose,
That something valuable will emerge from this agony.
But the question haunts me as I dissolve:
Am I being refined into something better,
Or simply destroyed for Your mysterious pleasure?


________________
If Suffering Brings Me Closer To You, Why Do I Want To Run?
They told me pain would draw me to Your arms,
That suffering would be the bridge between us.
Yet with each fresh wound You allow,
My instinct is not to draw near but to flee,
To hide from the God who permits such torment.
If trials are meant to be the thread
That binds my heart more tightly to Yours,
Why does every affliction feel like scissors,
Cutting whatever connection remained,
Leaving me more isolated than before?
The preacher speaks of breaking horses,
How struggle leads to submission.
Is that Your model of divine love 
That I must be broken to be Yours,
That rebellion must be crushed before intimacy can begin?
I've sat in churches where they celebrate suffering,
Praising the God who tests His children.
But their voices sound like hostages,
Convincing themselves their captor loves them,
Finding meaning in chains to avoid confronting their bondage.
If pain is Your love language,
We are speaking past each other.
For when I hurt, I don't see Your face more clearly 
It becomes obscured, distant, foreign,
A blurry portrait I struggle to recognize.
Perhaps the greatest mystery of faith
Is this perverse expectation
That we should run toward what harms us,
That we should thank the hand that strikes us,
That agony should produce not flight but devotion.
Suffering doesn't bring me closer;
It builds walls of confusion and doubt.
If this is Your divine strategy for intimacy,
You've misunderstood something fundamental
About the hearts You claim to have created.


________________
Do You Hurt When We Hurt?
When the child screams beneath collapsed rubble,
When the mother cradles her starving infant,
When the innocent hang from crosses of injustice 
Does Your divine heart constrict with pain,
Or do You observe with the detachment of eternity?
They say You wept at Lazarus' tomb,
That human suffering moved You to tears.
But what good are divine tears
That fall but nothing?
What use is cosmic empathy without intervention?
If You truly felt the agony of Your creation,
Wouldn't this world be different?
Wouldn't Your love compel You to act,
To end the suffering You allegedly feel,
To finally become the God we need?
Do You compartmentalize our pain,
File it away as necessary for some greater good?
Or does it pierce Your essence,
Make You question Your own methods,
Wonder if the cost of free will is too high?
I need to know that somewhere in the universe,
Someone carries this burden with me.
That when my heart shatters in my chest,
Something in You breaks as well,
That I am not alone in this ocean of suffering.
The God who hurts when we hurt
Would be a God I could follow.
The God who remains unmoved by our agony
Is not a God worth worshipping,
Just a distant tyrant with a cosmic plan.
So tell me, Creator of all that is:
When Your children suffer beneath Your watchful eye,
When we cry out for relief that never comes,
When we break under burdens we cannot carry 
Do You hurt too, or are You simply watching the show?
________________
Did You Ever Speak to the Gods We Deny?
Before we carved Your name in stone,
When other deities walked the earth 
Did You exchange words with Osiris?
Share wisdom with Vishnu?
Argue with Zeus over territory and tribute?
I wonder about the celestial conversations
That must have echoed across the cosmos,
Divine voices debating which humans to claim,
Which lands to mark as sacred,
Which prophets to whisper mysteries to.
Did You send messengers to Their temples,
Ambassadors to negotiate divine boundaries?
Or did You simply declare Them fiction,
Phantoms invented by misguided minds,
While claiming exclusive rights to truth?
The ancients heard Their voices as clearly as we hear Yours.
They built civilizations on Their commands,
Recorded miracles that matched Your own,
Received revelations that contradicted Yours,
Died believing They were just as real as You.
When the Indigenous elder prays to Grandfather Sky,
When the Hindu prostrates before Shiva,
When the ancient Greek poured libations to Athena 
Did You lean close to listen?
Or did You turn away, jealous and dismissive?
Perhaps the great cosmic secret
Is that You've been speaking to each other all along,
Planning which peoples to guide,
Which truths to reveal gradually,
Which aspects of divinity each culture could bear.
Or is loneliness the true burden of godhood 
That You are indeed the only voice in the void,
Watching Your creation fragment Your image
Into a thousand divine reflections,
Each one catching just a sliver of Your true face?


________________
Are You the Same God in Every Religion, or Are They Talking to Someone Else?
Muslims bow toward Mecca five times daily,
Jews wrap themselves in ancient prayers,
Hindus immerse in sacred rivers,
Christians consume their God in bread and wine,
Indigenous peoples dance to honor ancestral spirits.
Are they all reaching toward You
Through different doors in the same house?
Or are some knocking on empty rooms,
Calling names that echo unanswered,
Praying to the cosmic equivalent of wrong numbers?
How terrifying to consider
That some faiths might have the right address
While others send their devotions
Into an unresponsive void,
Their spiritual GPS fatally miscalibrated.
More terrifying still:
What if they're all connecting to something real,
But not all to You?
What if the universe holds multiple divine consciousnesses,
Each one claiming exclusive truth?
The woman bowing before Kali,
The man communing with Allah,
The child saying bedtime prayers to Jesus 
Are they speaking different languages to the same ear,
Or addressing entirely different beings across the cosmos?
Prophets across time have claimed direct connection,
Each returning with contradictory revelations.
Were some of them speaking to imposters?
Cosmic pretenders wearing divine masks?
Or fragments of Yourself in different guises?
The answer reshapes everything 
Whether we're all climbing the same mountain on different paths,
Or whether some of us are scaling entirely different peaks,
Some leading to You, others to alternative divinities,
And some, perhaps, to nothing at all.


________________
Why Did You Let Other Beliefs Thrive If They Were Lies?
If Yours is the only true voice,
Why allow the cacophony of competing faiths?
Each one certain of its exclusive truth,
Each one claiming divine authority,
Each one ready to die for its particular god.
A loving father would not let his children
Follow strangers promising candy.
A truthful teacher would correct
Students repeating dangerous falsehoods.
Yet You've watched billions worship "false" gods for millennia.
Hinduism flourished for thousands of years
Before Your Bible was assembled.
Buddhism spread its compassionate message
While Your prophets spoke of vengeance.
Were these merely elaborate divine tests?
If salvation depends on correct belief,
Your communication strategy seems deliberately cruel 
To birth souls into cultures with established faiths,
Then condemn them for following
The only spiritual path they've ever known.
Some say You plant truth in every tradition,
That all religions contain partial revelations.
But this contradicts Your demands for exclusive worship,
Your commandments against other gods,
Your promises that there is only one narrow path.
The simplest explanation is the most disturbing:
Either You don't care what we believe,
Or You're not the only voice speaking,
Or perhaps most terrifying of all 
You're testing which humans can discern true divinity from false.
What kind of God allows billions of souls
To live and die in sincere devotion to the "wrong" faith?
One whose plans transcend our understanding,
One who values free will above correct belief,
Or one who isn't what any religion claims Him to be?


________________
If The Truth Is One, Why Did You Let It Be Splintered?
You spoke Your truth, we're told,
In clear, unambiguous terms.
Yet that singular revelation
Fractured like light through a prism
Into countless contradicting interpretations.
Catholics and Protestants killed each other
Over the meaning of Your words.
Sunni and Shia spill blood to this day,
Each claiming authentic understanding.
Different Buddhists follow divergent paths to enlightenment.
If Your message was crystal clear,
Why has it generated endless division?
Did You deliberately speak in riddles
To watch us struggle with meaning,
To test which humans could discern Your true intent?
Christians alone have splintered into thousands of denominations,
Each claiming the proper reading of the same text.
Same book, same God, wildly different conclusions 
All convinced they alone have correctly
Decoded the divine communiqué.
The truth should unite, not divide.
Clarity should bring consensus, not conflict.
Your fractured revelation has caused more bloodshed
Then perhaps any other force in human history.
Was this Your plan all along?
Perhaps You never intended a single truth at all,
But scattered different facets of wisdom
Across diverse cultures and ages,
Like a cosmic scavenger hunt
Requiring all of humanity to complete the puzzle.
Or perhaps the splintering itself
Is proof that no single voice spoke,
That humans have been projecting
Their own values, fears, and hopes
Onto the silent canvas of an uncommunicative cosmos.


________________
Is the Devil Just a God Who Lost?
History is written by the victors, they say.
Does this apply to divine history as well?
Is Satan simply a deity who lost the cosmic war,
His worship criminalized,
His story rewritten by the conquering God?
Every villain was once someone’s hero.
Every demon was once someone’s god.
The horned deities of ancient faiths
Became the devils of newer religions 
Divine rebranding after a celestial coup.
The serpent in Eden offered knowledge,
The morning star brought illumination,
The adversary demanded justice
When Job was cruelly tested.
Are these the actions of evil,
Or of a different kind of good?
How convenient for a jealous God
To paint all competing deities as demons,
To reframe divine rivalry as cosmic evil,
To cast the other gods in the role of adversary,
Converting religious pluralism into spiritual warfare.
Perhaps Lucifer was the first heretic,
The first to question divine authority,
The first to suggest alternative interpretations,
The first to wonder if the ruling God
Was indeed as benevolent as claimed.
In ancient pantheons, gods competed openly,
Each with their domains and followers.
Only monotheism needed devils 
Theological repositories for the gods they displaced,
Supernatural villains to explain competing divine voices.
I wonder if, in some parallel reality,
The cosmic battle ended differently,
And what we call the Devil sits on heaven’s throne,
While today’s God is taught as the great deceiver,
The definitions of good and evil are perfectly reversed.


________________
Are There Others Like You?
Does the universe host a pantheon of beings
Similar to Yourself but distinct 
Cosmic entities watching different galaxies,
Divine consciousnesses evolving along separate paths,
Creator gods with their own realms and rules?
Are You truly alone in Your godhood,
A solitary being without peer or companion?
Or do You belong to some celestial community,
A society of similar beings
Who shares the burden of existence?
The concept of divine loneliness is staggering 
To be the only consciousness of Your kind,
With no equal to challenge or understand You,
No peer to share eternity with,
No companion who knows the weight of creating worlds.
Perhaps this explains Your obsession with humanity 
Not that we’re so extraordinary,
But that we’re the closest thing You have to company,
Pale reflections of Yourself,
Better than the crushing solitude of absolute uniqueness.
Or maybe the cosmos teems with godlike beings
Just beyond our perception,
Each tending their own universal gardens,
Occasionally crossing paths at the edges of creation,
Sharing notes on the curious experiment of conscious life.
Do You envy them their domains,
Compete with them for cosmic resources,
Learn from their alternate methods,
Compare the faithful of Your realm to theirs,
Take pride in the particular devotion of Your creation?
The vastness of existence suggests
That anything possible exists somewhere.
If God is possible, then surely many gods are equally so 
Unless the nature of divinity itself
Requires absolute singularity in an otherwise infinite universe.


________________
If We Called You By Another Name, Would You Still Answer?
If I called You Allah, would You turn Your ear?
If I addressed You as Brahma, would You recognize Yourself?
If I prayed to the Great Spirit, would You accept the invitation?
Or are You bound strictly to the particular syllables
Recorded in one tradition’s holy book?
A rose by any other name, they say 
But does divinity follow the same rule?
Are You the substance behind all sacred names,
Or are You particular in Your identity,
Responding only to certain holy words?
Some believers insist the divine name has power,
That certain syllables unlock cosmic forces,
That precision in spiritual language is essential.
But this implies a God extraordinarily small 
One trapped by human phonetics and semantics.
A mother answers to "Mom," "Mama," "Mother,"
Recognizing the intent behind various forms of address.
Would the Creator of language itself be less flexible?
More concerned with terminology than with the heart
Of the one reaching out across the divine human divide?
I have heard prayers in a hundred languages,
Witnessed devotion to differently named divinities,
Observed sincerity that transcends terminology.
Does the cosmic switchboard sort these calls by name,
Routing them to different divine recipients?
Perhaps the greatest test of faith is this:
To believe that divine love transcends nomenclature,
That the God of a thousand names recognizes them all,
That the heart’s intention outweighs the tongue’s precision,
That no sincere prayer is ever misdirected.
Or perhaps the ultimate truth is more disturbing still:
That You answer to one name in exclusivity,
That billions pray sincerely but to the wrong address,
That divine connection depends on correct terminology,
That salvation hangs on a phonetic lottery we never chose to enter.


________________
Why Do I Keep Failing You?
Did You Make Me a Sinner Just to Watch Me Fall?
My son feels embedded in my marrow,
A birthright I never asked to inherit.
Was I designed with this fatal flaw,
This crack that runs from crown to sole?
Sometimes I wonder if my bent toward breaking
Is not a glitch but a feature of Your design 
A cosmic experiment in predestined failure.
You sculpted me from dust and dreams,
Breathed life into clay that now crumbles daily.
Did Your fingers tremble as they shaped me,
Knowingly planting the seeds of my undoing?
Or did You smile at the perfect imperfection,
At ribs too weak to cage the wild heart,
At a mind wired to want what destroys it?
The first sin echoes through my veins
Like a song I’ve known since before language.
I feel the fruit’s sweetness on my tongue
Though I’ve never walked in Eden’s shade.
Did You place the tree there expecting the worst,
Or does omniscience mean You always knew
I would be born already fallen, already lost?
I imagine You watching from beyond the stars,
Tracking each stumble, each shameful night,
Each moment I choose shadow over light.
Are these my failures or Your successes?
Is my struggle the very theater You created,
A stage where my weakness serves as entertainment
For eyes that never close, never look away?
The pattern feels too perfect to be chance 
This endless cycle of sin, shame, redemption, repeat.
Did You make salvation so sweet
Because You knew the fall would be inevitable?
Is grace merely the counterweight to a burden
You intentionally made it too heavy to bear?
What kind of love designs its beloved to break?
Sometimes in darkness I hear laughter
And cannot tell if it’s Yours or the serpent’s.
Am I the punchline in some divine comedy,
The clay pot formed specifically to shatter?
If You are perfect, why make me so flawed?
If You are in love, why seed me with self destruction?
If You are Father, why set Your child up to fall?
Yet here I stand in my beautiful brokenness,
Wondering if my cracks are not failures
But windows through which Your light was meant to shine.
Perhaps my sin was never the point at all,
But rather what You would do with it 
How grace appears most radiant
Against the darkest backdrop of human failing.


________________
Why Does Trying to Be Good Feel Impossible?
I wake with resolute heart, steelspined intentions,
Promises fresh as morning dew on my lips.
By noon they’ve evaporated in the heat of living,
My will bent back toward familiar sins
Like a compass needle drawn inescapably north.
Who designed this broken moral compass?
Was it You, or is this corruption all my own?
The good I want to do hangs just beyond reach,
A fruit on a branch that bends away from my fingers.
I strain toward virtue, muscles trembling with effort,
Yet find myself empty handed by day’s end,
Cradling only the weight of what I failed to become.
Why does evil come as natural as breathing
While goodness requires the struggle of drowning?
I’ve crafted a thousand paper boats of intention,
Set them sailing on waters of determination,
Only to watch them dissolve in the current,
Their fragile hulls are no match for desire’s undertow.
Did You make the pull of wrong so strong
To test the strength of my feeble righteousness,
Or to prove I cannot stand without Your grace?
Your commandments clean, precise as mathematics 
Somehow blur when applied to flesh and blood.
I know the formula but fail the solution;
My soul is an equation that never balances.
Why build a house that cannot stand,
Plant a garden destined to wither,
Create a heart that resists its own salvation?
The weight of trying crushes more than failing.
It’s not the sin that breaks me but the trying 
The endless reaching for a bar set at heaven’s height
While my feet remain planted in earthly soil.
Did You intend this cosmic frustration,
This perpetual striving with no arrival,
This race where the finish line recedes with every step?
Sometimes I glimpse it the person I should be 
Standing just ahead on the path, waiting,
Face turned away but posture unmistakable:
Shoulders straight with integrity’s confidence,
Hands open in genuine generosity,
Steps aligned with justice and mercy.
Why can I see but never become this self?
Perhaps the impossibility is the point 
The space between who I am and who I should be
Is where You’ve always intended to dwell.
Maybe goodness was never meant to be achieved
But eternally pursued, like stars that guide sailors
Without ever being destinations themselves.
Is the beautiful agony of trying Your real gift?


________________
Why Do I Keep Repeating My Mistakes?
The familiar cliff edge I know its contours
By heart now, the exact moment earth gives way
To empty air.
Yet here I stand again, toes curled over the precipice,
Staring down at the same abyss that has claimed me before.
Why does my mind rediscover old dangers
With the enthusiasm of new love?
I’ve mapped every quicksand pit of my weakness,
Created detailed warnings in my memory:
"Here be dragons," written in scar tissue.
Still, my feet find these paths in darkness,
Unerring as water flowing downhill.
Is this Your grand cosmic joke 
To remember everything but wisdom?
The taste of regret is so familiar now
It feels like home cooking, bitter comfort.
I know its flavor before I’ve even lifted the spoon,
Can anticipate the exact moment of disgust
That follows indulgence like night follows day.
Yet knowledge doesn’t disrupt the cycle 
Is this the curse of being both god and animal?
Sometimes I catch myself mid fall,
Watching my hands reach for what will burn them,
As if my body belongs to another master.
I hear my voice making the same excuses,
See my heart opening the same rusted doors.
Did You plant this glitch in human programming 
This compulsion to circle back to our worst selves?
My mistakes wear grooves in my life
Like water carving canyons through stone 
Patient, persistent, deepening with each pass
Until the path of least resistance
Is the very one that leads to destruction.
What divine purpose is served by this repetition?
What lesson am I missing in the thousandth fall?
The faces change but the script remains 
The same words, different mouths;
The same wounds, different weapons;
The same sin, different circumstances.
I am trapped in this recursive nightmare,
A spiritual Groundhog Day of failure.
Did You design this loop as punishment or education?
Perhaps the spiral isn’t truly a circle 
Each iteration slightly higher than the last,
Each failure containing seeds of future victory,
Each repetition teaching me something new
About the bottomless depths of Your grace.
Maybe my mistake is seeing only the pattern
And missing how the fabric slowly transforms.


________________


Does My Guilt Please You?
I've turned shame into an art form,
Sculpted it into cathedrals of self loathing
Where I worship daily at the altar of my failures.
My prayers smell of smoke and remorse,
Each word is an ash covered offering.
Does this please You this ritual flagellation,
This endless catalogue of my insufficiency?
I wear my guilt like a hair shirt,
The constant chafing a reminder
Of every promise broken, every standard failed.
It seems the proper punishment,
The necessary weight for balance 
But am I serving justice, or feeding pride
In the elaborate religiosity of my regret?
Some nights I wonder if my guilt
Is more beloved to me than Your forgiveness 
If I've made self condemnation my true religion,
Finding perverse comfort in familiar pain
Rather than the disorienting freedom of grace.
Have You been waiting all this time
For me to stop worshipping at my own shrine?
The mathematics of my guilt make perfect sense:
Sin plus remorse equals restored order.
Yet You disrupt the equation with grace 
This wild, illogical erasure that leaves
No remainder, no logical conclusion.
Does my insistence on carrying what You've lifted
Suggest I trust my judgment more than Yours?
My brokenness defines me 
A strange refuge in guilt.
Without these scars, who am I,
And what fills the void of shame
Perhaps that's what terrifies me most 
The emptiness that forgiveness would create,
The person I'd have to become without my chains.
Sometimes I imagine You watching
My elaborate self punishment with sadness,
Waiting for me to accept the key
You've already placed it in the lock.
Is my guilt an offense rather than an offering?
Do I mock Your sacrifice by insisting
On paying a debt already settled?
The irony cuts deep as revelation 
That guilt itself may be my greatest sin,
The final idol I refuse to surrender,
The last piece of control I won't relinquish.
Perhaps true repentance isn't feeling worse,
But accepting the costly gift already given 
The scandalous freedom of being truly, fully loved.


________________


If You Love Me, Why Do I Hate Myself?
Your love is an ocean I cannot fathom 
Boundless, they say, deeper than the cosmos.
Yet here I stand on its shore, parched,
Unable to cup enough in my hands
To quench the desert of self hatred within.
How can Your perfect love exist alongside
The perfect contempt I've cultivated for myself?
I study my reflection in mirror shards,
Each fragment showing a different failure,
A comprehensive mosaic of inadequacy.
They tell me I bear Your image,
But all I see are the ways I've defaced it 
The divine fingerprint smudged beyond recognition.
Is this distortion my vision or Your creation?
I've heard the sermons, memorized the verses:
"Fearfully and wonderfully made," they insist,
While my mind catalogs evidence to the contrary.
Did You know, when You knit me together,
That I would spend a lifetime trying to unravel
The very fabric of who You made me to be?
What kind of love allows such self destruction?
Your voice calls me beloved,
But mine whispers impostor.
Your hand offers acceptance,
But mine forms a fist against my chest.
Your eyes see beauty,
But mine fixate on flaws.
Can both realities exist in the same universe?
Sometimes I wonder if self hatred is itself
A twisted form of pride the arrogance
Of believing my judgment trumps Yours,
Of trusting my perception over divine insight.
Have I made an idol of my unworthiness,
Worshipping at the shrine of my inadequacy
Rather than accepting the love You offer?
The math doesn't add up 
Your perfect love
Plus my persistent self hatred
Should not equal this fraught existence.
One of us must be wrong about who I am.
Logic says it's me, yet emotion disagrees,
Clinging to familiar self condemnation
Like a prisoner who fears his own pardon.
Perhaps self hate isn't the opposite of Your love
But the inevitable shadow it casts
In hearts too small to contain it.
Maybe my hatred isn't failure but growing pain 
The necessary friction as my soul expands
To hold a love too vast for human comprehension,
The birth pangs of becoming who You see.


________________


Am I Even Redeemable?
There must be souls beyond salvaging,
Some cosmic triage that even You perform 
Hearts too black for cleansing,
Minds too twisted to straighten,
Souls too shattered for restoration.
I fear I've crossed that invisible line
Where even divine mercy must admit defeat.
I've counted my sins like stars
Until the night sky of my conscience
Holds no space for light between darkness.
The weight of them bends me toward earth,
A spiritual gravity I cannot escape.
Is there a mathematical limit to grace,
A point where the equation of redemption fails?
How many second chances can exist
Before "again" becomes mockery?
How many broken promises constitute
A heart fundamentally opposed to goodness?
I've constructed a theology of my unworthiness,
Complex arguments for my exception to grace.
Did You create certain souls knowing they're disposable?
The stories speak of You leaving ninety nine
To find the one but what if that one
Has wandered off a hundred times before?
Surely divine patience has its breaking point,
Some final straw that even You won't overlook.
I've built my identity on being irreparable 
What if that's the one belief You won't contradict?
Sometimes I wonder if this fear itself
Is the final barrier to redemption 
Not my sins, but my certainty they define me,
Not my failures, but my faith in their finality.
Have I made an idol of my brokenness,
Worshipping at the altar of what cannot be fixed
Rather than surrendering to what can be transformed?
The question echoes in the chambers of my heart:
If I believe myself beyond redemption,
Am I calling You impotent or unloving?
What hubris lurks in my despair 
As if my sin could outreach Your grace,
As if I alone among creation
Could it prove too difficult for the Creator to restore?
Perhaps being redeemable has nothing to do
With the magnitude of darkness I contain,
But with the nature of the Light itself 
Not a matter of my worthiness
But of Your relentless, resourceful love
That finds ways to use even broken things,
It specializes in making masterpieces from ruins.


________________


How Many Times Will You Forgive Me Before You Give Up?
I've counted my returns to You like tide marks 
Each one stained with shame a different shade,
Each apology worn thinner than the last,
The words fraying from overuse.
How many times can the same lips say "sorry"
Before the sound loses meaning entirely,
Before even divine ears grow deaf to repetition?
I keep a mental tally of my failures,
Notches on the bedpost of my conscience 
Surely You must keep a similar account.
Is there a threshold I approach unconsciously,
Some cosmic limit to divine patience
Where even Your endless grace must acknowledge
The futility of forgiving what never truly changes?
Your disciples once asked about forgiveness 
"Seven times?" they ventured, generous to a fault.
"Seventy times seven," You replied, casual
As if suggesting an afternoon stroll.
But I've done the math: four hundred ninety.
I passed that milestone years ago.
What happens at four hundred ninety one?
Sometimes I picture You with an abacus,
Celestial fingers sliding beads across eternity,
Each click marking another chance extended,
Another fresh start that I'll inevitably squander.
Does Your hand ever hesitate before resetting the count?
Does divine mercy ever consider retirement,
Worn out from my relentless consumption of grace?
The fear lives in me like a second heartbeat 
That I am testing even infinite patience,
That someday I'll approach Your throne
Only to find the door finally, irrevocably locked.
Not from cruelty, but from wisdom 
The recognition that some vessels are determined
To remain sieves, forever leaking what fills them.
What terrifies me most isn't Your anger
But the possibility of Your resignation 
Not the thunderous voice of judgment
But the quiet sigh of giving up.
Would You release me with regret but resolution,
Like a bird that refuses to heal it's broken wing,
Like a child who will not stop playing with fire?
Yet here's the miracle that stops my breath:
Each time I return, tail tucked between my legs,
You're watching the horizon as if for the first time,
Eyes shaded against the sun, scanning for my silhouette.
Each forgiveness feels brand new, freshly minted,
As if You haven't spent eternity
Perfecting the art of welcoming me home.

Did You Ever Think About Starting Over?
Did You ever stand at the edge of creation,
Looking at the chaos You had unleashed,
And wonder if the spark was worth the fire?
Did You ever hold the blueprint of humanity
And feel the weight of what You’d made 
A species destined to break itself
Before it could ever understand love?
Did You ever think about wiping the slate clean,
Erasing the stars You hung with trembling hands?
Did You imagine a world without us,
A silence where our cries never echoed,
A void where no prayers rose to meet You,
Where no one cursed Your name
For the pain You allowed to exist?
When the first murder stained the earth,
Did You hesitate, hand hovering over the reset button?
Did You see Cain’s shadow stretch across time,
A lineage of blood and betrayal,
And wonder if the cost of free will
Was it too high for you to bear?
Did You regret the breath You gave us?
When the floodwaters rose,
Did You feel relief or sorrow?
Did the screams of the drowning
Reach Your ears, or Did You turn away?
Did You cry for the world You destroyed,
Or was it easier to let it go,
To pretend it was never there at all?
Did You ever think about starting over,
Not with water, but with fire this time?
Did You imagine a world without sin,
Without the weight of our endless failures?
Or did You know, even then,
That perfection would be a prison,
And love cannot exist without choice?
Did You ever think about starting over,
Or did You love us too much to let us go?
Did You see something in our brokenness
That even You couldn’t explain?
Did You hope that one day,
We might surprise You,
That we might finally be worth the pain?

 Was There A Moment You Considered Wiping Us Out Completely?
Was there a moment, God,
When You looked at the mess we’d made
And thought, “Enough”?
When the wars piled bodies like mountains,
When the earth groaned under the weight of our greed,
When the prayers of the innocent
Were drowned out by the laughter of the wicked 
Did You consider ending it all?
Did You ever stand at the edge of time,
Watching the centuries unravel like thread,
And wonder if the tapestry was worth it?
Did You see the faces of the unborn,
The ones who would suffer for sins not their own,
And feel the urge to stop the clock,
To let the universe collapse into silence?
When the Holocaust burned,
When slavery chained Your children,
When famine hollowed out entire nations,
Did You think, even for a second,
That we were beyond saving?
Did You weigh the scales of justice and mercy
And find us wanting,
Find us unworthy of the breath You gave?
Was there a moment,
In the quiet of eternity,
When You whispered to Yourself,
“This is not what I intended”?
Did You feel the ache of disappointment
In the hearts You had shaped,
The hands You had formed to create,
Now destroying everything You called good?
Did You ever consider wiping us out completely,
Not with a flood, but with a thought?
Did You imagine a world without us,
A creation that didn’t rebel,
That didn’t spit in the face of its Maker?
Or did You know, even then,
That love cannot exist without pain?
Was there a moment You considered giving up,
Or did You hold on,
Not because we deserved it,
But because You couldn’t bear to let us go?
________________
 When You Flooded The World, Did You Cry?
When the rain began to fall,
Did You feel the weight of every drop?
Did You hear the screams of the drowning,
The pounding fists on the ark’s closed door?
Did You cry, God,
As the waters rose,
As the world You had spoken into being
Was swallowed by the silence of the deep?
Did You mourn the children,
The ones who never had a chance to sin?
Did You grieve for the mothers clutching their babies,
For the fathers who couldn’t save them?
Did You feel the loss of every life,
Or was it easier to focus on Noah,
To pretend the rest didn’t matter?
When the animals floated lifeless,
When the trees were uprooted,
When the mountains disappeared beneath the waves,
Did You hesitate, even for a moment?
Did You wonder if the punishment
Was too great for the crime,
If the flood was justice,
Or just another mistake?
Did You cry, God,
When the last voice was silenced,
When the earth was nothing but water and regret?
Did You look at the rainbow You placed in the sky
And feel the weight of Your promise,
The vow never to destroy us again,
Even though You knew we would deserve it?
When You flooded the world,
Did You cry for what was lost,
Or did You cry for what remained?
Did You weep for the ones who perished,
Or for the ones who would carry the burden
Of rebuilding a broken world?
Did You cry, God,
Or did You turn away,
Unable to face the destruction
Of what You had once called good?

Are We A Failure Or A Lesson?
Are we Your greatest failure,
Or Your most painful lesson?
Did You create us to reflect Your glory,
Only to see us shatter the mirror?
Did You hope we would rise above,
Or did You always know
We would fall?
Are we the experiment that went wrong,
The garden that grew weeds instead of flowers?
Did You plant us in the soil of free will,
Only to watch us choke on our own choices?
Did You see the potential for beauty,
Or were we always destined
To be a cautionary tale?
Are we a failure,
A mistake You regret but cannot undo?
Or are we the lesson You needed to learn,
The reminder that even perfection
Cannot force love?
Did You create us to teach Yourself
That even a God can be surprised?
Do You look at us with disappointment,
Or with hope?
Do You see the cracks in our souls
And think, “This is not what I intended”?
Or do You see the light that still shines through,
The glimmers of goodness
That refuses to be extinguished?
Are we a failure,
Or are we the lesson
That love worth the risk?
That even in our brokenness,
Are we capable of something divine?
Do You regret making us,
Or do You hold on,
Hoping that one day,
We might finally get it right?

Do You Ever Feel Trapped By Your Own Promises?
Do You ever feel the weight of Your own words,
The chains of the promises You made?
When You swore never to flood the earth again,
Did You feel relief,
Or did You feel trapped?
Did You wonder if mercy
Would one day cost You more than justice?
Do You ever regret the covenant You made,
The rainbow You hung in the sky?
Do You look at the world we’ve created
And wish You could take it back,
That You could start over
Without the burden of Your own faithfulness?
When You promised Abraham descendants,
Did You foresee the wars they would fight,
The blood they would spill in Your name?
When You gave us free will,
Did You know it would lead to this 
A world where Your promises
Feel like a noose around Your neck?
Do You ever feel trapped, God,
By the vows You cannot break?
Do You look at us and think,
“This is not what I wanted,
But I cannot go back?”
Do You feel the weight of Your own love,
The cost of a grace
That refuses to let go?
Do You ever wish You could undo it all,
Not because You don’t love us,
But because love has become
The heaviest burden of all?

________________
 If You Could Undo Us, Would You?
If You could go back to the beginning,
To the moment before You said, “Let there be light,”
Would You speak those words again?
Would You breathe life into the dust,
Knowing it would one day curse You?
Would You create us,
Knowing we would break Your heart?
If You could undo us,
Would You?
Would You erase the wars,
The hatred, the suffering,
The endless cycle of sin and redemption?
Would You choose silence over chaos,
Void over creation,
Peace over love?
Do You ever look at us and think,
“This was not worth it”?
Do You see the pain we cause,
The destruction we bring,
And wish You could take it all back?
Or do You hold on,
Not because we deserve it,
But because You cannot bear to let us go?
If You could undo us,
Would You choose a world without us,
A world without the weight of our failures?
Or would You choose us again,
Not because we are good,
But because we are Yours?
________________
Or Do You Love Us Too Much To Let Go?
Do You love us, God,
Even when we are unlovable?
Do You see the mess we’ve made,
The pain we’ve caused,
And still call us Your children?
Do You hold us in Your hands,
Even when we try to slip through Your fingers?
Do You love us too much to let go,
Even when we curse Your name?
Even when we turn our backs on You,
When we choose darkness over light,
When do we break every promise we’ve made?
Do You still see something in us
That makes it all worth it?
Do You love us,
Not because we are good,
But because You are?
Do You hold on,
Not because we deserve it,
But because You cannot bear to lose us?
Do You love us too much to let go,
Even when we fail You,
Even when we fail ourselves?
Do You see the spark of divinity
In the ashes of our humanity?
Do You believe in us,
Even when we no longer believe in You?
Do You love us, God,
Not because of who we are,
But because of who You are?
Do You hold on,
Not because we are worthy,
But because love cannot let go?
