Do I Keep Asking For Answers I Refuse To Accept?
I lay my questions down like bricks,
stacking them into walls too high to see over.
I say I want truth,
but only the kind that doesn’t break me.
I beg for wisdom,
but only the kind that agrees with what I already know.
I pretend I’m waiting for Your voice,
but I only listen for the answers I like.
You whisper in winds,
but I demand a storm.
You place signs at my feet,
but I walk past them, looking for neon.
You close doors that will destroy me,
but I keep banging my fists, demanding they open.
I ask why the pain lingers,
but I won’t let go of the wound.
I say I want healing,
but only if it doesn’t require change.
I say I trust You,
but I still make my own plans in secret.
I say You are good,
but only when life feels easy.
I call You silent,
but maybe I’ve just mastered the art of selective hearing.
I demand an answer,
But what if You’ve already given it?
What if I buried it beneath my pride?
What if I ignored it because it asked too much of me?
What if I’m not waiting on You 
What if You’re waiting on me to admit I already know?
Because deep down, I do.
I always have.
And maybe that’s the real reason I keep asking.
Maybe I don’t want the answer.
Maybe I just want permission to keep pretending.


________________
Am I Too Impatient For Your Timing?
I treat time like a finish line,
sprinting toward a future I haven’t earned.
I tap my foot at eternity’s door,
like a child throwing a tantrum.
I ask You why things take so long,
but I never question why I rush.
I want mountains to move,
but I refuse to climb.
I want the blessing,
but not the breaking that builds it.
I want the crown,
but not the cross that comes first.
I call it faith,
but it’s just control wrapped in scripture.
I claim to trust You,
but I keep checking my watch.
I act like You work on my schedule,
as if You owe me a deadline.
But You don’t rush seasons
just because I’m impatient.
The earth turns without my permission.
The sun rises without my consent.
So who am I to demand urgency
from a God who paints time in galaxies?
Maybe I don’t need faster answers.
Maybe I need slower faith.
Maybe patience isn’t punishment 
Maybe it’s protection.
Maybe the waiting isn’t empty 
Maybe it’s building me in ways I don’t yet understand.
Maybe I don’t need to know when.
Maybe I just need to know You.
Because time has never been my enemy.
My doubt has.


________________
Do You Stay Silent Because I Already Know The Answer?
I act like I’m lost,
but the road has always been beneath my feet.
I pretend I don’t understand,
but the truth has been stitched into my bones.
I call You silent,
but maybe You’re just done repeating Yourself.
Maybe You don’t waste words
on lessons already carved into my soul.
Maybe You stopped answering
because I stopped listening.
Because I only hear what I want.
Because I only obey what’s convenient.
You told me to let go,
but I held tighter.
You told me to move forward,
but I stood still, waiting for an easier path.
You told me to trust You,
but I demanded proof.
You told me the truth,
but I called it too hard.
You gave me direction,
but I kept looking for detours.
And now I sit in silence,
wondering why You won’t speak.
But maybe You already did.




________________
Or Are You Just Done Talking?
The silence hangs heavy, like a curtain drawn in a storm.
I whisper your name into the void, searching for a ripple.
Every unanswered prayer feels like a door closed forever.
I wonder if your voice has been lost in the echoes of my faults.
Perhaps you’ve tired of my constant clamor, my desperate pleas.
I trace memories of past conversations, each word a fragile relic.
Was it the arrogance of my demands that drove you away?
Or the relentless noise of my unsatisfied heart?
In the quiet, I hear the ghost of your once thundering promise.
I question if your silence is the end of love, or its transformation.
Every pause in our dialogue is a chasm of unspoken grief.
The weight of your absence bears down like dusk on broken dreams.
I search for signs in the falling leaves and distant thunder.
Maybe the stillness is not an ending, but a test of my resolve.
I recall the warmth of your words, soft as the morning dew.
Now they are memories, fading into the cold reality of emptiness.
My doubts multiply like shadows in a room with no light.
I ask if I pushed you away with my constant need for certainty.
Or if I misunderstood the quiet as your final goodbye.
In this silence, my own voice echoes back with painful honesty.
I try to decipher if your withdrawal is sorrow, pride, or despair.
The unanswered calls and silent nights aske the hardest question.
Is this absence a punishment for my stubborn disbelief?
Or is it the start of a dialogue I’m too afraid to embrace?
In the end, I stand alone, waiting for the one sign that proves you still care.


________________
Did I Lose You, Or Did I Just Never Know You?
In the mirror of my soul, I search for your reflection.
Was your face ever there, or am I haunted by a myth?
I built altars of hope and carved your name in every prayer.
Yet every offering fell to ashes, leaving only whispers.
I wonder if our connection was ever real or just a phantom dream.
I recall moments when I felt you near, only to be left cold.
Perhaps I mistook solitude for communion, pain for presence.
I question whether I ever truly saw you or only the longing for love.
My heart clings to memories like leaves in a furious storm.
Each recollection poses a question without an answer.
I wander through the ruins of faith, desperately searching for you.
The echoes of my doubts challenge every sign that once felt true.
I strain to hear a voice, a hint of the divine in the mundane.
But the silence roars, a testament to what might have been.
I mourn the loss of something I never fully held.
I weep for the connection that remains an unclaimed promise.
Was it a friendship, a bond, or simply a cruel illusion?
The emptiness between us forms a canyon of unanswered prayers.
I question whether I built you up or merely wished for your presence.
My soul trembles in the space where you should have been.
I face the truth: perhaps you were never meant to be known.
The quest for you mirrors the void inside me.
I surrender to the silence, accepting the unknown.
In letting go, I wonder if losing you meant losing a part of myself.
And so I stand, haunted by the possibility that I never truly knew you.


________________
If You’re Still Here, Give Me A Sign Or Have You Given Up?
The midnight sky trembles under the weight of my pleas.
I stand at the crossroads of despair and hope, heart in trembling hands.
If You are near, let the heavens shatter in gentle fury.
Let a comet blazing across the night is a burning testimony.
Speak in the rustle of leaves, in the murmurs of the ancient earth.
I beg for a whisper, a subtle cue to mend my fractured faith.
Each heartbeat echoes a silent, desperate question.
Is Your presence still a promise, or have You turned away?
I search for a sign in every falling star, every ripple in time.
My soul aches for the comfort of a divine touch.
Let the wind carry words that my eyes have longed to decipher.
Let the rain trace messages on the parched ground of my doubt.
I need a signal to shatter the oppressive quiet of my uncertainty.
Perhaps a flower will bloom in the dead of winter.
Or a tear will fall that mirrors the depth of my longing.
Every moment stretches a canvas waiting for Your art.
I stand naked before the universe, unguarded and raw.
Every fiber of my being cries out for connection.
I am desperate for the warmth of Your eternal gaze.
Is this silence a test of my perseverance or a final farewell?
My faith flickers like a candle, yearning for a spark.
I implore You to break this stillness, to offer me a sign.
Without Your whisper, I drift in a sea of uncertainty.
The silence roars with the echo of my unanswered calls.
So, if You’re still here, speak now if not, let this quiet seal my fate.


________________
Will You Abandon Me If I Stop Praying?
My prayers were lifelines cast into an endless void.
Every whispered plea was a desperate bid for connection.
I knelt in brokenness, hoping for a miracle to mend me.
Yet now I wonder if my devotion was ever enough.
What if I let go if I allow my voice to fall silent?
Would You still be there, steadfast in the quiet aftermath?
Is love measured by the cadence of our pleas?
Or does it exist as an unwavering presence beyond my doubts?
I fear that in my silence, I might lose the gentle touch of grace.
The quiet may roar louder than any prayer I’ve ever offered.
My soul trembles at the thought of being left alone in that void.
Every unanswered plea adds weight to my fear of abandonment.
Yet still, I cling to a hope that defies logic and reason.
Would You reach for me if I chose to fall silent?
Could Your love span the chasm left by my missing words?
I question whether my need to pray was merely a mask for despair.
If I stop, is that surrender and acceptance of isolation?
Must my faith be measured only by the sound of my pleas?
In the quiet absence of my voice, do You remain open?
I dream of a love that endures without constant affirmation.
A love as vast and unyielding as the silent stars overhead.
Even if my prayers fade into distant echoes,
I long to believe You remain steadfast, true, and eternal.
So tell me, if I stop praying, will You still hold me close?
Can love endure in the silence of a heart unvoiced?


________________
Can I Be Atheist And Still Be Yours?
I stand at the edge of belief, questioning every sacred story.
My heart beats with rhythms that defy ancient dogma.
Scriptures echo like distant memories of a past life.
Yet a hidden part of me yearns for the divine touch.
Can love transcend the rigid boundaries of faith?
Is there room for doubt within the sanctuary of your embrace?
I wander through barren landscapes of disbelief,
Searching for a spark that defies the cold logic of reason.
Must I renounce my doubts to claim Your love?
Can I be both skeptic and beloved, rebellious and cherished?
I wear my uncertainty like armor, yet I remain achingly vulnerable.
In the silence of my questioning, I sense Your steady heartbeat.
Love should not be a transaction dictated by belief.
It ought to be an enduring promise beyond the confines of creed.
My mind questions while my soul longs for a deeper connection.
I crave a love that embraces my fractured certainty.
Must I recite ritual prayers to prove my worthiness?
I challenge the notion that unwavering belief is the only key.
Can our souls meet where reason and wonder intertwine?
Is it possible to be both the architect of doubt and the recipient of grace?
I long for a love that accepts me in my raw, unfiltered form.
Even as I question, I feel the tender pulse of Your care.
A love that murmurs softly, “I am yours,” even in the void.
So tell me can I be an atheist and still call You mine?


________________
If I Leave You, Will You Chase Me?
I stand at the precipice of abandonment, trembling with fear.
The thought of walking away haunts each uncertain step.
My heart is a restless wanderer, desperate for solace.
Yet what if I choose to leave, casting off my familiar chains?
Would You pursue me, reaching out through the dark unknown?
Or would I vanish into a shadow too elusive to capture?
My doubts scream in the cavernous silence of solitude.
I question if love is a relentless, tender pursuit.
Could Your footsteps echo behind mine in the deep night?
Would Your heart beat faster in the silence of my departure?
The possibility of loss is a weight too crushing to bear.
Still, a daring part of me craves to test the limits of grace.
Is love defined by the distance bridged in our reunion?
Or is it a bond that endures even as paths diverge?
I envision a scene where I dissolve into the horizon’s edge.
And You, unwavering, chase the echoes of my fleeing soul.
My heart dares to wonder if breaking free is salvation or ruin.
Will the love we built shatter, or will it soar beyond the void?
Could the chase itself testify to the strength of our bond?
A love so fierce it defies even the silence of separation.
I ask the night: will Your love be the beacon that follows me?
A light that guides even when I stray far from home.
Or will I be left to drift, forever lost in my own leaving?
The possibility terrifies and thrills me in equal measure.
So if I leave, will You chase me, or must I wander alone?


________________
Or Will You Just Watch Me Go?
In the hidden corners of my heart, I sense a looming farewell.
I wonder if my departure will be met with quiet resignation.
Will You, in all Your vast mystery, simply observe my retreat?
Or will Your eyes blaze with the fierce light of unyielding love?
I fear that love might sometimes reduce itself to a distant spectator.
That devotion can slip into indifference, a cold, detached witness.
In the theater of my sorrow, am I merely a solitary actor?
A fading ghost in a scene that has long lost its color?
I imagine a world where my leaving is rendered in silent film.
Each moment a still frame, void of sound or vibrant life.
Would You, in Your infinite grace, watch as I dissolve into memory?
Or would You surge forth like a storm, breaking the stillness?
The coldness of inaction is a terror too sharp to endure.
I fear that if You merely watch, I become a tragedy unredeemed.
Love should not be passive, a mere observer of my pain.
It must be the force that draws me back from the brink.
In my darkest hour, I yearn for more than just a watching eye.
I need the rescue, the touch that shatters my isolation.
Without it, I remain stranded in a void where hope withers.
I challenge this silence, pleading for a love that actively fights.
One that refuses to let me fade into the twilight.
I cry out for intervention in the face of impending loss.
So tell me, will You just watch me go, or will You fight to hold me?


If I Come Back, Will You Pretend I Never Left?
I wander through corridors of lost time, steeped in uncertainty.
Each step echoes with the heaviness of my long absence.
If I return, will You greet me as though I had never vanished?
Or will the scars of my leaving brand me as a stranger?
My heart aches with the desire to be reawakened by Your care.
To feel the warmth of a love that forgives all transgressions.
I question whether my departure carved a wound too deep to heal.
Will You erase the memory of my flight from grace?
Or will You keep it as a silent lesson, a permanent mark?
The allure of a clean slate is both soothing and terrifying.
I fear that my absence has irreversibly altered our bond.
Can love be reset, or must it forever carry the weight of regret?
I imagine coming home to arms grown cold by solitude.
Or to smiles that hide the pain of a long forgotten farewell.
The desire for redemption burns fiercely within my soul.
I long to return to a love untainted by my wanderings.
Yet I tremble at the prospect of being a ghost among you.
An echo of a self I no longer recognize.
Would You celebrate the return of the prodigal,
Or silently mark the void where I once belonged?
I wonder if forgiveness can gently erase the past,
Or if every memory lingers like a shadow in the light.
In my longing, I dare to believe in new beginnings,
A love that offers renewal without clinging to old scars.
So if I come back, will You pretend I never left,
Or will You embrace every scar as a part of our shared truth?


________________
Will You Let Me Choose Hell?
I stand at the crossroads of faith and defiance,
fire licking at the hem of my hesitation.
The freedom to choose burns untamed, unyielding.
In the hush of self reckoning, I ask:
Can love be unconditional if it permits my ruin?
Does grace have a breaking point?
My soul is a battlefield,
caught between salvation’s whisper
and the fierce gospel of self determination.
Maybe my rejection is not rebellion 
but the last, desperate reach for something real.
The notion of hell is both curse and liberation.
Can Your love follow me
into the wreckage of my own making?
If I push past mercy, will You pull me back,
or honor my choice to be lost?
I test the edges of grace with every defiant step,
watching to see if the abyss blinks first.
Yet, even as I wander toward ruin,
a phantom hope lingers in my shadow.
Must devotion require surrender?
Or can love still hold me,
even when I refuse to be held?
I demand the right to be wrong.
To fall without a hand reaching to break it.
To taste the fire and decide for myself
if it is suffering or salvation in another name.
Will You let me choose hell,
and still call me beloved?
Or does love end at the threshold
of my undoing?








Epilogue
This book was never meant to offer answers. It was meant to be a conversation one sided, raw, and unfiltered. It is the voice of someone standing at the edge of faith, staring into the abyss, screaming at a God they cannot deny yet refuse to embrace. It is the mind of a deconstructing believer unraveling, condemning, pleading, and daring to judge the Almighty. It is the struggle of a soul that both hates God and aches for Him, trapped in the tension between certainty and rebellion.
I am a devout Christian. I believe in the presence of God, in His love, in His sovereignty. But I am also deeply fascinated by those who once believed and now feel abandoned. What happens when a heart that was once worshiped turns bitter? When prayers turn to accusations? When the silence of God is no longer seen as mystery but as neglect? This book is an attempt to sit in that space not to justify it, not to explain it away, but to feel it.
There is no table of contents or sections, because there is no map for this kind of journey. I wanted the reader to step directly into the chaos, to navigate this wilderness the way one truly does without a guide, without direction, without certainty. This is not a book for those seeking easy resolutions. It is for those who have found themselves screaming at the heavens, wondering if God is listening at all.
Maybe you are here because you have felt this pain. Maybe you are here because you fear it. Or maybe, like me, you simply want to understand. Whatever brought you here, I hope you walked through these pages not just as a reader, but as a witness to the rawest parts of faith; the ones we are often too afraid to speak aloud.




The Spirit Of Solomon
        A Collection Of Poems








                                                   




































              Kiminou Knox

For those who loved me at my worst, who saw the man beneath the crown, and for those I lost along the way your absence taught me presence.                                                                                                          
“The heart has its reasons which reason knows nothing of.”
                                        
  Blaise Pascal

________________


                                                      Introduction
                                        
In the ancient text, King Solomon was blessed with wisdom beyond measure, yet even he fell prey to the temptations of flesh and power. His story is one of glory and folly, of profound insight and profound blindness                                                        
This collection traces a modern Solomon’s journey a man who crowned himself king of desire, who built his kingdom on conquest rather than connection, who mistook power for worth and possession for love.                                        
These poems chart the rise and fall of that kingdom, the cracks in that crown, and ultimately, the wisdom that can only come from wounds. They speak to the universal struggle between what we want and what we need, between who we pretend to be and who we truly are.
In the tradition of the Song of Solomon, these verses explore the sacred and profane nature of love its capacity to elevate and destroy, to heal and to harm. They ask what it means to be a man in a world that rewards performance over presence, and what it costs to maintain that performance.                                        
This is not just a collection of poems about relationships; it is an excavation of the self, a reckoning with the ghosts of what could have been, and ultimately, a testament to the possibility of rebuilding from ruins.                                        
The journey begins in desire and ends in wisdom. What lies between is the story of a man learning, finally, that the true measure of a king is not how many he rules, but how deeply he can surrender to the most powerful force of all love. 
                                
                        
                
                                                                   
I. The Kingdom of Desire
The Idea of Love
The idea of love.
It’s one that’s followed me my whole life,
but I still ask myself
was that really what I was seeking?
Love.
What even is that?
Some made-up fairy tale
where the prince and princess get together in the end?
No. It’s more.
It’s always been more.
I’ve sent many women packing,
left them with their hopes and dreams still unpacked.
Yet somehow, I can still feel them.
Still hear them.
Still see their wanting eyes, even when I let them go.
Is that love?
No.
Love is more than that.
What I’m describing is connection
one I think I never had
with the ones who said they loved me.
Loved me so much, I started believing
pain was love.
That suffering made it stronger.
My love ain’t peaceful.
It ain’t kind.
My love is heartache, breakups,
and finding your big brother in bed with your wife.
But before you start casting stones,
let me ask you
what is your idea of love?
The one you hold so dear?
See, mine makes a winner.
Yours puts you in a crowd that cheers.
I’ll get the ring and leave before the kiss.
You’ll never see the white in your own gown.
And that
that is my solemn vow.
________________
God Ain’t Watching
I move like smoke in a dim-lit room,
cool hands, slow grin, voice like velvet sin.
Ain’t no thunder cracking the sky,
no ghosts whispering warnings in my ear.
If I was meant to pay for this,
shouldn’t the bill be here by now?
They say every touch leaves a scar,
but my hands stay clean,
my sheets stay warm,
and my phone still hums with names
that don’t ask for much 
just a taste, just a night,
just enough to make them wonder
what loving me might feel like.
I collect them like secrets,
soft bodies pressed into memory,
lips parted in surrender,
hearts left on my nightstand
like spare change I never spend.
I pray but not for forgiveness.
Not for peace.
I pray for more.
More skin, more sweat,
more nights where the only sound
is my name tangled in their breath.
And if I wake up untouched,
if my luck ain’t run out yet, then maybe, just maybe,
God ain’t watching at all.
________________
The Art of Rotation
It’s a delicate thing 
like jazz, like chess, like the tide knowing
just how far to reach before it pulls back.
Too much, and they drown in you.
Too little, and they start looking for land.
See, a real player don’t chase.
A real player moves like gravity 
silent, invisible, but always felt.
He don’t beg for time;
he lets absence make them curious.
Lets them wonder where he at,
who he with,
why he ain’t called.
The trick?
Make every woman feel like she the only one
without ever saying she is.
A well-placed “I miss you,”
a lingering glance,
a slow kiss that says everything
and nothing at all.
Text her just enough
to keep her warm but never hot.
Love her just enough
to keep her close but never locked.
Give her just enough
to make her think she winning,
when really, she just playing your game.
And when one gets too close 
when she starts whispering words like forever 
pull back.
Disappear just long enough for doubt to creep in,
for her to miss the way your hands fit her waist,
for her to remember how quiet her bed feels
without you in it.
Because the art of rotation
ain’t just about keeping them in line 
it’s about keeping them wanting.
About making them think leaving
was never an option.


________________
Love? That Ain’t for Me
Love is a heavy coat in the dead of summer,
too warm, too weighty, too much to bear.
I prefer the breeze, the light touch of passing hands,
the warmth that don’t linger past sunrise.
See, love is a contract,
inked in sacrifice, bound in blood,
a debt that always comes due.
And me? I deal in moments,
in lips that forget my name by morning,
in hearts that never get close enough to break.
I give just enough to taste,
never enough to swallow.
Just enough to make her wonder 
but not enough to make her stay.
They call it game,
but it’s really just knowing the rules.
Knowing that a man who don’t fall
can’t be caught.
She asks what we are,
and I kiss her slow,
let my silence press against her skin.
Some questions ain’t meant for answers.
Some hands ain’t meant to be held.
Because love?
Love is a locked door,
and I’ve never been the type
to beg for keys.
________________
They Say I’m Just Like Him
They say I’m just like him, the king of the game,
a man too slick, too sharp to be tamed.
Silver-tongued prophet with hands laced in sin,
I whisper a prayer, then do it again.
I move like a shadow, too quick to be caught,
love ain’t a lesson I ever got taught.
I take what I want, then I leave it behind,
never a trace, never a sign.
She calls me the devil, but still, she stays,
wrapped in the spell of my halfway gaze.
She wants forever I laugh at the thought,
ain’t nothing I build that don’t end up rot.
But they still follow, still reach for my touch,
swearing I’m different, swearing I’m much.
Like I ain't the echo of men from before,
writing their names, then locking the door.
They say I’m just like him I take it in stride,
a king with a kingdom, a throne built on pride.
And maybe one day, I’ll answer the cost,
but until then?
I never get lost.


________________
Ain’t No Kings Without Queens
What is a king without women to kneel?
A throne with no warmth, a crown that ain’t real.
Gold means nothing when hands stay cold,
when nights stretch long, and the bed feels old.
See, power ain’t power if no one obeys,
if no one is waiting, if no one will stay.
So I keep them close, but not too tight,
a whispered promise, a stolen night.
I touch like worship, I move like prayer,
a prophet of pleasure with time to spare.
She thinks she’s special, the only one,
but she’s just a verse in a song I’ve spun.
One tells me she loves me I let her believe,
watch as she builds, while I plan to leave.
One wants forever I smile, stay mute,
ain’t no kingdom that don’t bear fruit.
Because what is a king if he stands alone?
A fool in gold, a man unthroned.
So I let them love, I let them dream,
but I never let them reign with me.
________________
The Gospel of Game
I never tell lies 
I just let them believe.
A well-placed pause, a touch on the sleeve,
a look that lingers, a smirk, a tease 
they write their own stories, I just plant the seed.
See, love ain’t a language, it’s a trick of the tongue,
a song well-sung, a web well-spun.
Give her just enough to keep her near,
never too much, never too clear.
A compliment wrapped in something unsaid,
a whisper that lingers long after bed.
A hand on her thigh, then pulling away 
leave her wanting, make her stay.
They call it game, but it’s more like faith,
belief in the things I never say.
A touch, a glance, a half-meant vow 
she’ll paint the picture, I just show her how.
Because truth is a mirror, and I learned too young,
most hearts break long before they’re strung.
And me? I ain’t cruel, I just let them dream.
Ain’t my fault they want to believe in me.
________________
The Last Time I Prayed
I used to kneel with both hands tight,
whispering hopes into dead-of-night.
Asking for mercy, asking for grace,
before I learned how to lie to His face.
See, once upon a time, I played it straight,
thought love was fate, thought hearts were safe.
But lessons come quick when the world turns cold 
prayers go quiet when trust gets sold.
The first time I fell, I fell too deep,
gave what was real, lost what was me.
And when she left? I learned the trade 
how to make a woman stay.
Now my prayers are lips, my hands are hymns,
I baptize bodies, then leave like wind.
No need for blessings, no need for light,
I make my own heaven, one touch at a time.
But sometimes just sometimes I hear that voice,
deep in the silence, stripping my choice.
And maybe one night, when the games all fade,
I’ll remember the last time I truly prayed.
















II. Conquest and Crown        
Her Lips, My Sin
Her lips were scripture,
 written in red,
a gospel of hunger,
 a prayer left unsaid.
Soft as deceit, 
sweet as a lie,
she kissed like a promise
, then left me to die.
She tasted like midnight, like whispered regret,
a sin I ain’t touched,
 but still won’t forget.
She was fire, she was smoke,
 she was death wrapped in lace,
but I still let her pull me to that dangerous place.
My hands on her hips, 
my name on her tongue,
she laughed when I told her she might be the one.
Not for forever,
 not for a life,
but for nights built on heat, for pleasure,
 not wife.
I knew what she was a ghost draped in silk,
poison in perfume, honey in filth.
But I drank anyway, let her pull me too deep,
now her lips are a wound, 
and I pray in my sleep.


________________
She Called Me Beautiful
She had that honey-gold skin, kissed by dusk,
curls wild like secrets the wind wouldn’t trust.
Eyes that held fire, the kind that devours,
a look that could break men in less than an hour.
She ran the night like she owned the streets,
soft hands, sharp tongue, gold anklets on her feet.
Her scent? Like warm vanilla left out in the sun,
a sweetness that stayed long after she was gone.
I was the wolf at least, I thought,
’til she grabbed the wheel and left me caught.
One hand on my thigh, the other in gear,
she smiled, slow like she already knew my fears.
"You're beautiful," she whispered, voice thick as sin,
and damn if I ain't felt it deep in my skin.
A man don’t blush, a man don’t shake 
but she had me breathless, made my body betray.
She traced my jaw, laughed when I sighed,
like she loved to unravel whatever I tried.
I thought I was hunter, I thought I had game,
but in her hands, I was prey just the same.


________________
Red Dress, Black Heart
She walked in, and the whole room leaned.
Scarlet silk, slit high as sin,
curls wild down her back, soft as a hymn,
hips swaying like a song I’d die to sing.
She smelled like burnt sugar and bad decisions,
like midnight wrapped in jasmine and sweat.
Every man watched, but she looked at me 
a slow drag of her gaze, undressing my regret.
I felt her before she even touched me,
heat licking up my skin,
fingertips ghosting my wrist,
nails tracing the veins like she could read my sin.
“Miss me?” she whispered, lips just shy of mine.
And God help me, I did.
Because she was famine, she was feast,
she was the hunger I never could quit.
My hands found her waist, greedy, unholy,
her breath in my ear, a wicked little prayer.
She laughed low, knowing 
and pressed closer, til all I could taste was her air.
But I knew better.
I knew that dress was warpaint,
those lips were a blade,
her love was a grave I had already laid.
Still, I followed her out,
like a man with no past, no shame, no cost.
Because she had a black heart,
but damn, it matched mine.
The First Woman I Ever Worshipped
She let me think I had the reins,
smirked when I gripped her hips like I owned her,
like she wasn’t the one guiding my hands,
wasn’t the one pulling the strings under my skin.
Her body was silk and sin,
warmth spilling over me in waves,
hips rolling like a whispered command,
breath hot against my neck, laced with praise.
She sighed my name like it meant something,
like I was more than another conquest in her bed,
like I wasn’t just the next fool chasing
the ghost of her touch, the taste of her breath.
I traced fire down her spine,
dragged my lips across every inch of her,
thinking I was marking her, branding her 
but she only laughed,
tilted her head,
pressed me deeper into her world,
and let me drown.
She let me lead, let me whisper,
let me play the part of the man in control,
but when the morning light kissed her skin,
when she stretched like a queen in silk sheets,
I knew 
I had never been in charge at all.
________________
I Let Her Ruin Me
She had me. Completely. No fight, no pride, no name.
Just skin on skin, breath on breath,
me slipping deeper into the space between her thighs,
where time slowed, and reason drowned.
I was a man before her sharp, calculated, untouchable.
But in her hands, I unraveled,
bones turning to smoke,
control bleeding out in moans I swore I’d never make.
She rode me like she owned me,
like she knew my undoing was stitched in the way she moved,
hips rolling, nails dragging, mouth curved in something wicked,
a slow, torturous rhythm that made me beg 
and I swore I never begged.
"You're mine," she whispered, tongue tracing the shell of my ear,
and I nodded helpless, breathless, already lost.
My fingers dug into her skin, desperate to hold on,
but she was slipping, shifting, leading me deeper,
until there was no edge, no floor, no escape.
I wanted to stop just for a second,
just long enough to remember who I was before her,
but she wouldn’t let me.
Her body tightened, her lips found my pulse,
and just like that, I shattered 
a man broken open, devoured whole.
And when it was over, when she lay back,
satisfied, smug, victorious,
I realized 
I had never stood a chance
________________
Love Ain’t The Word
Could I love a woman made of smoke and sin?
A woman who moved like temptation itself,
who kissed me deep enough to make me forget my name,
then left my sheets cold by morning?
I told myself no 
that I was just lost in the spell of her skin,
that it was lust, not love,
that I wasn’t one of those fools who thought
a whore could be a home.
But then she smiled,
and something in me cracked.
She had me questioning 
was I chasing her because I wanted her?
Or because I needed to prove
that she could be mine alone?
But love ain’t the word for a woman
who never belonged to one man,
who let me hold her close,
whisper promises she never intended to keep,
then slipped away like a thief in the dark.
She swore she was mine,
but she moaned another man’s name in the same breath,
came home smelling like cologne I didn’t wear,
with lips swollen from kisses I didn’t give her.
And still, I stayed.
Still, I let her crawl back into my bed,
whisper lies into my skin,
make me forget for a night, at least 
that she was never mine to begin with.
Could I love a woman like that?
I looked at her, eyes full of apologies
I couldn’t stomach anymore,
and I finally knew the answer.
No.
I never loved her.
I just hated losing.
________________
I Left, But She Never Did
I told myself this was it.
No more late-night calls, no more lips that lied,
no more pretending she could ever be something
other than a wound I kept pressing just to feel something.
She looked at me, smirk curling like she knew better.
Like she’d heard this before.
"You’ll be back," she whispered, stretching lazy in my sheets,
like she was permanent, like she was home.
But I wasn’t losing.
Not this time.
Because the only place I was losing
was in having her 
in the way her body made me forget
that love wasn’t supposed to feel like war,
that real peace didn’t taste like regret in the morning.
I pulled my shirt over my head,
stepped back as she sat up, hair wild,
eyes still glowing with the ghosts of men
who all thought they’d be the one to tame her.
"I’m not them," I said, more to myself than to her.
I had to believe it.
She just smiled, slow, knowing, stretching like she had all the time in the world.
"You never were," she said.
And that scared me more than losing her.
I walked out.
Didn’t look back.
But even after I was gone,
after her scent faded from my sheets,
after my phone stayed silent at 2 a.m.,
she stayed 
tucked in the cracks of my thoughts,
whispering in the spaces between sleep and waking.
I left her.
But I don’t think she ever really left me.

III. The Cracks in the Throne 
What If I Stayed?
What if I stayed 
let her love settle into the cracks
instead of running before it could reach me?
What if I met her halfway,
instead of watching her walk the whole distance alone?
She was steady hands on my restless skin,
a warmth I only learned to miss in the cold.
She was Sunday morning peace,
but I was Saturday night sin,
too wild, too selfish, too sure I had time.
I told myself she was too good,
too patient, too soft for a man like me.
But the truth?
She wasn’t too much 
I was just too little.
She wanted a builder,
but I only knew how to break.
She prayed for me,
and I laughed at her faith,
never knowing one day I’d kneel,
asking God why He sent me love
before I was man enough to hold it.
And now, some nights,
when my bed is too wide,
when silence hums where her voice used to be,
I wonder 
What if I stayed?
Would I have been enough,
or just learned new ways to lose her?
________________
She Knew My Mother’s Name
She sat across from my mother,
laughing like they shared a secret
that had nothing to do with me.
Like they had history,
like they had plans 
like she wasn’t just passing through.
She knew my mother’s name.
Not just the name the world called her,
but the name she carried before the weight of life
curved her spine and thinned her voice.
She said it soft, like she belonged there.
Like she could love what made me
before I even knew how to love myself.
She folded into my family like she was born for it,
like she had always been meant to be
the one to fix what I never had the hands for.
And I let her. I let her love my mother,
my sisters, my past 
everything but me.
Because love like that is heavy.
Because I didn’t want to owe her anything
when I finally ran.
And when I did 
when I left her standing in that kitchen,
still stirring, still hoping, still waiting 
I told myself it was mercy.
Better to hurt her now
than let her think I could ever be
the man she deserved.
But years later, I saw her again.
She didn’t look at me the same.
She didn’t smile.
She didn’t speak.
And for the first time in my life,
I felt like a stranger in my own story.
She knew my mother’s name.
And now 
I’m the one she forgot.
________________
The One I Could’ve Built With
Man… I be thinking.
Like, really thinking.
Like, sitting in my car staring at the steering wheel type thinking.
I had ‘em all 
the ones that smelled like vanilla and bad decisions,
the ones that only hit my phone after midnight,
the ones that left their earrings on purpose
like they was marking territory.
Had ‘em laughing, had ‘em screaming,
had ‘em swearing I was the best they ever had.
Had ‘em for a night, maybe two.
Had ‘em, then forgot ‘em.
But her?
Man… she different.
She knew my favorite meal without asking.
Knew when I was lying before I said a word.
Knew how to be quiet when I ain’t feel like talking,
and knew how to talk when I ain’t feel like listening.
She knew me.
And I ain’t even know what to do with that.
I fumbled. Hard.
Treated her like all the rest
when she was nothing like them.
And the crazy part?
She never even hated me for it.
Just looked at me one day, real soft, and said,
“You ain’t ready for me, are you?”
And I wasn’t.
So now she gone.
Probably laughing in some other man’s passenger seat,
probably learning his favorite meal,
probably knowing him better than he know himself.
And me?
I got hoes.
But I don’t got her.
________________
She Prayed For Me
She used to hold my hands like they was something holy,
like maybe if she held tight enough,
some of that goodness in her would sink into my skin.
Like she could love me into being a better man.
I remember one night,
I was laid up in her lap, half-listening,
half-tracing circles on her thigh.
She sighed, real soft,
ran her fingers through my hair,
and whispered, "I pray for you, you know?"
I laughed.
Laughed like she just told me some dumb joke.
Like I wasn’t worth praying for.
Like I ain’t need saving.
But she just smiled.
Didn’t argue. Didn’t preach.
Just kissed my forehead and kept loving me anyway.
And that?
That hurt more than if she woulda cursed me out.
More than if she woulda slammed the door in my face.
More than if she woulda told me I wasn’t shit and never would be.
Because deep down,
I knew she saw something in me I couldn’t see in myself.
And I hated her for that.
Hated how she made me feel like I was worth something.
Hated how she looked at me like I was more than the sum of my sins.
Hated how she prayed for a man who had no plans of being saved.
I think about that sometimes.
Wonder if she still prays for me.
Wonder if she prays for him now instead.
Wonder if he deserves it more.
________________
She Deserved a Husband, Not a Hobby
I tried to make her leave.
Gave her every reason, every excuse.
Said the meanest shit I could think of,
watched the words hit her like bricks,
but she just blinked, wiped her face,
and said, "That all you got?"
I ain't never met a woman so stubborn.
She saw the wreckage and still stayed,
like love made her immune to the smoke,
like she ain't mind suffocating
if it meant she could save me.
I came home drunk,
perfume on my shirt that wasn’t hers,
lips swollen from kisses she ain’t give me,
and she still made me coffee in the morning.
Sat across from me, stirring her tea,
not saying a word.
That silence killed me.
Worse than screaming.
Worse than a slap.
Worse than leaving.
Because I knew she was waiting.
Waiting for me to be better.
Waiting for me to catch up to the love
she was so damn ready to give me.
And I?
I was busy setting fire to everything,
just to see if she’d stay in the flames.
She did.
And I hated her for that too.

One Day, She’ll Be Loved
I hope he knows to bring her lilies.
Not roses she always said roses were lazy,
the bare minimum of romance.
But lilies?
She loved how they opened slow,
like they were stretching awake,
like love should patient, unhurried.
I hope he listens when she talks about true crime,
how her favorite killer was the one
who never got caught.
Not ‘cause she liked what he did,
but because she admired a mystery,
the way a mind could be dark and still fascinating.
She used to say, “Even monsters need love.”
 And maybe that’s why she stayed so long.
I hope he calls her at lunch,
just to hear her voice.
Texts her “made it home safe?”
 even when she forgets to check in.
Because she’ll act like she don’t need that,
but she does.
I hope he kisses her wrists.
I never did.
Never thought to.
Didn’t realize that’s where she carried her stress,
where she rubbed when she was thinking,
where her pulse beat quick when she was happy.
I hope he makes her tea,
with just enough honey,
not too much milk.
She likes it warm, not scalding.
I always made it too hot,
made her wait before she could enjoy it.
Maybe that was a metaphor.
I hope he loves her in a way
that don’t feel like work,
like trying,
like forcing himself to be someone he ain’t.
I hope it’s easy for him.
Effortless.
The way it should’ve been for me. I hope she forgets the way I made her feel.
And I hate that I hope that.
But I do.

The Last Time I Saw Her
She stood in the doorway, wearing my favorite color.
Not that she knew that.
Or maybe she did.
She always paid attention when I wasn’t looking.
I wanted to say something,
but words felt like knives in my throat,
sharp with regret, heavy with truth.
So I just stared.
She looked at me like she already knew.
Like she’d read the ending of this story
long before I had the courage to turn the page.
“I love you,” she said, voice steady, like a fact.
Like gravity. Like breath. Like something
I should’ve believed in, but never did.
I closed my eyes.
Because when I looked at her,
I saw everything I could’ve been 
a man worth keeping.
A man who stayed.
A man who wasn’t too afraid
to try, to trust, to be good.
But I ain’t him.
I never was.
So I reached for a lie,
something to make this easier,
something to turn her softness into stone,
to make her hate me enough
to stop loving me.
“You deserve better.”
It was the kindest way I knew how to leave.
She laughed, but it wasn’t funny.
“You think I don’t know that?” she said,
shaking her head like I was the biggest fool alive.
And I was.
Because in that moment,
I realized she had never been the one
who needed convincing.
I was.
I wanted to ask her to wait.
To save me a seat at the table of her love,
to believe in me even when I couldn’t.
But I knew better.
She had spent too many nights
praying for a man who would never arrive.
So I let her go.
Because I knew she’d spend the rest of her life
waiting for a man who believed in himself.
And I wasn’t sure I ever would.

IV. The Fall of Solomon
She Called Me Out
I fed her lines like scripture, smooth as silk and twice as tight,
Told her she was something different, something rare, something right.
But she just laughed low and knowing like she’d heard it all before,
Sipped her drink, crossed her legs, left me stranded at the door.
“You talk too much,” she finally said, eyes like knives against my chest.
“Bet you tell them all they’re special, just to see which one says yes.”
I smirked, like I had answers, but my tongue was tied in chains,
Because she wasn’t just listening she was studying the game.
I leaned in close, played my role, let my fingers brush her sleeve,
But she didn’t melt, didn’t blush, didn’t give me room to breathe.
She just held her ground, too calm, too cool, too unimpressed,
And I felt something strange creep in a quiet kind of stress.
She called me out without a whisper, without even raising hell,
Like a judge who’d read my verdict long before the hammer fell.
Every trick, every tale, every polished little line,
She disassembled me with ease piece by piece, lie by lie.
And as I watched her walk away, hips swaying, heels clicking,
I swore I’d let her go, but something deep kept itching.
Because she was the first to see me, to pull apart my show,
And for the first damn time in my life I didn’t want to go.


________________
No Games, Just Mirrors
She wasn’t a queen, a muse, a prize 
She wasn’t impressed with rehearsed goodbyes.
Didn’t chase, didn’t flinch, didn’t play pretend,
Didn’t smile just to let me win.
She didn’t lean in when I spoke real low,
Didn’t gasp when I threatened to go.
Didn’t beg for my love, didn’t fawn at my name,
Didn’t need my rules she wasn’t in the game.
She held up a mirror, clear and cold,
And I saw myself in the way she told 
The half-truth smiles, the hollow plans,
The man who was boy, with empty hands.
She said, “You’re used to being worshipped, but I ain’t got the time.”
 She said, “You love yourself so much, you never learned to love right.”
 I watched her, stunned, no script, no cue,
No clever escape, no line to use.
I reached for her wrist she slipped away,
Left me standing in my own decay.
No text, no call, no second chance,
No slow unravel, no final glance.
And now I see her in reflections dim,
In car windows, in barroom hymns.
She didn’t stay, she didn’t weep,
Didn’t haunt my nights but she owns my sleep.

Her Eyes Were My Confession
She ain’t have to yell, she ain’t have to beg,
Ain’t throw no glass, ain’t call me fake.
Just sat there, staring, real damn still,
Like she already knew but wanted me to spill.
And I swear to God, I almost did.
Almost cracked, almost slid,
Almost let the weight of every lie
Come crashing out in real time.
But I ain’t built like that.
Nah.
I play it cool.
Play it sharp.
Give a half-smile, shrug, lean back in the dark.
Say some shit like, “You trippin’, girl,”
Try to laugh, try to twist her world.
But she ain’t laugh.
Didn’t even blink.
Just kept looking at me like she could smell the stink 
Of every slick word, every empty vow,
Every time I left and came back somehow.
I felt naked.
And not the good kind.
Not the slow, soft, whispered-moan kind.
The kind where you cold, under bright white lights,
Where somebody bigger than you just read you your rights.
She wasn’t arguing.
She wasn’t mad.
Just disappointed and somehow, that hurt more than that.
And I sat there, stomach tight,
Mind running through every fight,
Every promise I ain’t keep,
Every lie I put to sleep.
I should’ve apologized.
Should’ve grabbed her hand.
Should’ve told her what I was,
Tried to make her understand.
But I ain’t say shit.
Just let the silence stretch and bend,
Until she sighed, shook her head 
And let me lose her like a man.

"You’re Not Solomon"
"Damn, you really think you him, huh?"
She said it real calm, real easy,
like she wasn’t about to cut me open
with a sentence that sharp.
"All that talk about wisdom, about game,
but you ain’t wise, baby.
You just scared."
I laughed 'cause what else was I supposed to do?
She ain’t blink, though.
Ain’t smile. Ain’t flinch.
Just sat there, one leg crossed over the other,
fingers tapping slow on the rim of her glass,
watching me squirm like she had all the time in the world.
"You ever ask yourself why you move like this?
Why you collect women like trophies,
but never keep none long enough to gather dust?"*
I sip my drink, clear my throat,
say some slick shit about how I ain’t meant to be caged,
how kings don’t kneel, how love ain’t in my blood.
She smirks.
Not the kind you wanna see.
Not the soft, flirty kind.
The kind that says, "I feel sorry for you."
"See, Solomon had a kingdom."
She leans forward now, elbows on the table.
"You got a roster."
Ouch.
"Solomon had wisdom."
She tilts her head.
"You got excuses."
Damn.
"Solomon fell 'cause he let his demons in."
She leans back, arms crossed now.
"You already livin' with yours."
I should leave.
Should laugh.
Should brush this shit off,
text one of my girls,
remind myself I still got options.
But my hands are still on the table.
Still empty.
And for the first time in a long time,
I ain’t got a single word to say.

"Why Are You Like This?"
She don’t yell.
She don’t beg.
She don’t even sound mad.
Just tired.
Like she already knew the answer,
like she already knew me better than I ever did.
"You break everything you touch."
She says it soft,
watching me, waiting,
like I got some grand explanation,
some excuse that’ll make it all make sense.
I don’t.
I sip my drink slow,
pretend it don’t sting,
pretend I ain’t shifting in my seat.
"You love what’s easy, what don’t ask for too much."
She leans back, head tilted, studying me.
"You want worship, not love. You want control, not closeness.
And when you get something real?"
She exhales, eyes cutting straight through me.
"You set it on fire, just to watch it burn."
I laugh, but it’s hollow.
Shake my head, try to play it cool.
"Man, you overthinking it."
"Am I?"
She don’t flinch.
She don’t blink.
She don’t move.
And I swear, for the first time,
I feel naked.
Like she peeled me open with her bare hands
and laid every ugly piece of me on the table.
"You ever even ask yourself why?" she says.
"Or is it just easier not to?"
The ice in my glass clinks.
The room feels too quiet.
I swallow hard.
Look away.
Wish I had a lie ready.
Wish I had the nerve to say,
"I don’t know."
But we both know the truth.
I ain’t ready to say it.
And she ain’t waiting for me to figure it out.

"The Woman Who Didn’t Want Me"
She moved like moonlight slipping through fingers,
soft, distant never mine to hold.
She kissed me without sinking,
lips like a whisper, touch like a ghost,
and when she pulled away,
I swear she took something with her.
I been loved, been worshipped,
been the sun they circled ‘round.
But she?
She stayed in her own orbit,
eyes too knowing, smile too sharp,
never once mistaking me for a god.
"You ain't Solomon, baby,"
she hummed,
tracing my jaw with the tip of her nail.
"You just a man who likes the sound of his own name."
She said it smooth,
like it wasn’t a wound she just opened.
Like she ain’t just put a mirror in front of me
and dare me to look.
I tried to unravel her,
press my fingers into her silence,
pull her into the gravity of my hands.
But she never wavered.
Never lost herself.
She let me in without giving me shit to hold onto.
And when she left,
she ain’t cry, ain’t beg, ain’t look back.
She just gathered her perfume,
her red nails, her steady voice 
and vanished like she was never there.
Now, my phone stays dry.
No missed calls, no "I miss you" texts.
And me?
I keep telling myself I ain’t thinking about her.
That she was just another one.
That she didn’t win.
But some nights,
when the air is thick and my bed feels too big,
I wonder 
Was she even mine to lose?
Or was I just another story
she walked away from,
without ever needing to know the ending?

"She’ll Forget Me, But I Won’t Forget Her"
She was the only one who never knelt.
Never folded.
Never gave me that look the one that said,
"You could ruin me, and I'd thank you for it."
No.
She was the storm I couldn’t sail through.
The door I couldn’t kick open.
The silence that swallowed my name whole
and spat it back out like it never mattered.
I should’ve seen it coming.
She studied me like a bad habit
she was never addicted to.
Watched me spin my web,
watched other women get caught,
and just stepped over the bodies.
Unmoved. Unimpressed.
"You want me ‘cause I don’t want you," she said,
arms crossed, weight on one hip,
mouth curled up like she knew something I didn’t.
And maybe she did.
Maybe she saw the boy under the crown,
the cracks in the gold,
the hollowness I kept dressing up in silk and smoke.
I called her a challenge.
She called me a waste of time.
And when she left, she ain't leave a mark.
No lipstick on my mirror,
no earrings under my bed,
no scent in my sheets.
Just a gap in the air,
a space that still hums with her absence.
And me?
I keep reaching for ghosts.
Keep talking to echoes.
Keep looking for her in every woman who stays too long,
waiting for one to be as cruel, as clean,
as devastating.
But I know better.
They don’t make them like her twice.
And she?
She’s somewhere forgetting me 
the way I wish I could forget her.

V. The Ruins of Pride        
We Weren’t Innocent
We weren’t no soft love, no Sunday morning sweetness,
no “good morning, my love” type of shit.
Nah. We was streetlights humming at 3AM,
we was hands around throats just to feel something.
We was "baby, I love you" through clenched teeth,
right before the next war started.
I fucked up first.
Yeah, I did it. Ain’t no excuses.
She cried, but she ain't leave.
Just tucked it in her back pocket like a blade,
waitin’ on the right time to slide it across my throat.
And I? I thought we was good.
Thought we made it through.
Thought the blood dried and the past was buried.
Til she said she needed space.
Some air. Some time.
A fuckin’ break.
And I, dumb as hell,
kissed her forehead, said “yeah, okay.”
Told her I’d wait.
Like a fool. Like a simp.
Like I ain’t know what “a break” really meant.
Two weeks later, she came back,
eyes all swollen, voice all soft.
Told me she “got caught up,”
Told me she “didn’t mean to,”
Told me it “just happened.”
Like he tripped and fell dick-first into her.
And my heart?
Shit, my heart ain’t break 
it cracked,
it snapped,
it crumbled like a house made of all the lies we told each other.
See, I could fuck half the city,
but she was supposed to be different.
She was supposed to cry and claw at my chest,
not spread her legs for the first smooth talkin’ motherfucker
who told her she deserved better.
And now, when I look at her?
All I see is him.
His hands on her hips.
His lips on her collarbone.
His fuckin’ voice laughin’ in the dark 
a ghost in my own goddamn bed.
So I went on a warpath.
Drowned in bodies that didn’t smell like her.
Tried to fuck the taste of her out my mouth.
But no matter how many women I left breathless,
I still woke up choking on her name.
And she?
She still looks at me with those big, wet eyes,
like I was supposed to understand.
Like I was supposed to forgive.
But love ain’t built like that.
Not ours.
Ours was war.
And war don’t end in peace.

“The Night I Left Her Crying” 
Her name popped up.
 New number. Same ghost.
Said she just wanted to talk,
like words could patch a slit throat.
Like I ain’t been bleedin’ out for days,
wonderin’ how she slept so sound
after rippin’ me open.
“I didn’t mean to hurt you.”
 That’s the first thing she said,
like that shit ever mattered.
Like it was the intention,
not the action, that left me
punchin’ holes through my own reflection.
And then she added more.
More details I ain’t ask for.
More reasons to hate her.
More bricks to the chest.
“It wasn’t just once.”
 “We weren’t even drunk.”
 “He kissed me first but, I mean, I let him.”
 “I cried after… does that count for something?”
Man, I sat there, phone burnin’ in my palm,
wonderin’ what the fuck I did in a past life
to deserve this kinda punishment.
I ain’t cheat.
Ain’t even think about it.
But somehow, I woke up blocked on everything 
IG, Twitter, my own goddamn email.
Even the Bible app, bruh.
 Like, she ain’t want me near God neither.
Like, damn, I can’t even read Proverbs in peace?
And now she sittin’ here,
talkin’ all slow, all sorry,
sayin’ she miss me.
Sayin’ she made a mistake.
Sayin’ she been prayin’ on it,
like I’m some test God put in her path
just to see if she could stay faithful.
And I almost caved.
 Almost believed it.
Almost let her weave her way back in,
curl up in the place where my trust used to be.
But then I heard his voice in my head.
The one she swore didn’t mean shit.
The one she let whisper against her neck.
The one she chose
when she said she needed space.
So I inhaled slow,
let the silence stretch long,
then gave her the only truth I had left 
“I hope he was worth it.”
And just like that, I heard it.
That sharp little breath.
That stutter in her throat.
That exact second she realized
she lost something she couldn’t get back.
And me?
I just walked away,
left her sittin’ in the wreckage she built,
tears spillin’ where my love used to be.


________________
“She Said She’d Get Me Back” 
I should’ve known.
Should’ve caught it in the way she smiled,
all teeth, no warmth,
like she was already making a plan,
already setting the stage.
“I’ll get you back.”
 She said it soft,
like a promise,
like a prayer,
like a curse.
And I laughed.
Because what could she do to me
that I ain’t already survived?
I thought I was untouchable.
Unmovable.
Above all that.
But see, vengeance ain’t always loud.
Sometimes, it’s whispered between sheets
that don’t belong to you.
Sometimes, it’s written in lipstick
on a mirror you ain’t ever looked in.
Sometimes, it’s soft moans in a room
where your name ain’t never been spoken.
And that’s how she did it.
Not with fists, not with fire,
but with silence,
with another man’s weight between her thighs,
with a smirk when she finally told me,
like she had been waiting to see my face
crack open like an egg.
And I tried 
Tried to act unbothered,
tried to smirk back,
tried to laugh like it ain’t burn.
But she saw it.
She saw the way my jaw tensed,
the way my hands curled into fists.
She saw the moment I felt it.
And she leaned in,
breath hot against my ear,
whispered, “Now we’re even.”
And I swear to God,
it wasn’t even the cheating that broke me 
it was the way she walked away,
heels clicking against the pavement,
like she had just won a game
I ain’t even know I was playing.


“Was He Better Than Me?” 
I ain’t even mean to ask.
Swear to God, I didn’t.
But that question been crawlin’ up my throat
since the night she confessed. 
Was he better than me?
Did he kiss you like he was starvin’?
Did he press his weight against you,
make you shiver when he whispered,
make you beg when he pulled away?
Did he touch you like I used to,
or did he fumble,
all clumsy hands and dead weight?
Did you call him baby?
Did you call him mine?
Did you say his name
the way you used to say mine 
like a promise,
like a prayer,
like I was the only thing
you ever believed in?
Tell me.
 Did he make you feel safe,
or just satisfied?
Did he make you feel loved,
or just needed?
Did you close your eyes and see me,
or did he carve a whole new home inside you,
one I could never enter again?
And when it was over 
when you laid there in the after,
his breath still heavy on your skin 
did you feel whole?
Or did you feel hollow,
like all he did was stretch out the space
where I used to live?
Was he better than me?
I almost ain’t wanna hear the answer.
Almost ain’t wanna know.
But she looked at me,
eyes glistening,
lips parting,
words forming 
And I saw it.
The hesitation.
The regret.
The guilt sinking in
just a second too late.
And that’s when I knew.
That’s when I laughed.
Because it didn’t even matter, did it?
Better or worse,
she still lost me.








________________


I Tried to Love Her Again
I tried. I swear I did.
Sat across from her at some dimly lit spot,
watching her stir her drink like she was mixing up lies,
watching the candlelight flicker in her eyes,
wondering if it reflected back the man she let inside her.
I sipped slow,
let the burn settle under my tongue
before I opened my mouth and ruined everything.
Tried to see her how I used to 
soft, sweet, something worth staying for.
Tried to forget the weight of his name in her mouth.
But I couldn’t.
All I saw was him.
 Like he left fingerprints on her skin I could never scrub off,
like he rewrote her body in a language I never learned.
She smiled like nothing happened.
Like I ain’t seen her knees bruised from kneeling for another man.
Like I ain’t hear her breath hitch when I pulled her close,
like she had to remind herself that I was him before him.
And I wanted to love her, I really did 
but only in the way a king loves his crown,
only in the way a man loves a secret he never meant to tell.
Because the truth?
I ain’t love her.
I loved having her.
Loved the way she reached for me like I was her god.
Loved how she folded,
how she made me feel like I was the only thing worth breaking for.
And maybe that’s why I kept her around,
why I let her crawl back into my bed,
why I kissed her slow and deep
like I was searching for the part of me she swallowed.
But love?
No, love was never in it.
Just possession. Just habit. Just lust dressed up in borrowed feelings.
Just me playing victim, pretending like I ain't ruin her first.
Pretending like I wasn’t the one who set this whole thing on fire.
She wanted redemption.
I just wanted revenge.
And that’s why, when she whispered, “I miss you.”
I pulled her closer,
kissed her like I meant it,
ran my hands over every inch of her,
and left her there 
naked, breathless, undone 
just so she could feel what it was like
to lose something you thought was yours.




________________
“My Revenge Was Sleeping With Her Ghost” 
I traced her name in the sweat of another woman’s back,
watched it disappear when she rolled over,
just like she did.
I reached for someone new,
but my hands only found echoes,
bodies shaped like distractions,
moaning sounds that never quite matched the melody I remembered.
I kissed lips that weren’t hers,
tasted liquor instead of love,
called them baby because their names felt foreign on my tongue,
because every damn one of them
was just an understudy in a play she wrote.
And they let me.
Let me pretend.
Let me fuck her through them,
 like my body could summon her ghost,
like every thrust was a séance,
like if I went deep enough,
I’d hit the place where she still loved me.
But I never did.
Never could.
Because she wasn’t in the way their fingers traced my ribs,
or the way they arched, or sighed, or begged.
She wasn’t in the moans,
or the morning after,
or the slow drag of a cigarette at 3 AM
when the sheets still smelled like someone I didn’t care about.
She was in the silence.
In the space between one woman leaving
and the next one walking through my door.
In the way I stopped saying I love you
because I could still hear her laughing in my head.
She was in the mirror,
in the guilt stitched into my reflection,
in the way I sat in my own filth,
surrounded by all these bodies 
but somehow still alone.
I thought I could bury her in flesh,
drown her in perfume and sweat,
forget her in the heat of a stranger’s mouth.
But all I did was resurrect her.
Again, and again, and again.
Because no matter how many women I took to bed,
the only one I ever really fucked 
was her ghost.


________________


We Weren’t Supposed To
She smelled like a song I ain’t never heard,
but somehow knew every damn word.
A melody thick with midnight and Moët,
skin caramel-dipped, glowing like sin done blessed her.
She moved slow, like she owned time,
like the world was just a room she was passing through.
Gold hoops swayed like whispers ‘round her neck,
and her dress red, tight,
like a secret too loud to keep.
I told myself I was just looking.
Just admiring God’s audacity.
But then she turned 
eyes dark, heavy-lashed, the kind that tell you
“Come closer,” even when they mean “Run.”
And I came.
She said her name like it wasn’t dangerous,
like it wouldn’t taste like regret in the morning.
Voice rich, dipped in something sweet,
with just enough edge to cut through my caution.
Laughed low, touched my wrist,
leaned in like she had something holy to confess 
or something filthy.
I swear I ain’t hear the DJ switch the track,
‘cause the only rhythm I felt was her breathing,
the way it synced with mine,
like our bodies had history our minds ain’t yet remember.
We danced.
No she danced, and I followed,
hands hovering, then gripping, then lost.
My fingers traced the line between good man and fool,
and by the time she pulled me closer,
I ain’t care where I landed.
“You always this smooth?” she asked,
lips teasing the rim of her glass,
eyes teasing me worse.
“Only when I’m inspired.”
She smirked, slow. Knowing.
Ran her nails down my arm,
sent a shiver straight through my sense.
Told me she liked the way I talked,
the way I looked at her,
like she was the last temptation.
And maybe she was.
I ain’t ask about a man.
Ain’t check for a ring.
Ain’t think past the neon lights and the way her body fit against mine.
I was too lost in the lie we both was telling,
too drunk off the thought that she was mine,
even if just for a song.
But when she kissed me,
pressed lips soft and slow, like she was taking her time,
like she was memorizing me for later 
that’s when I knew.
Not by the way she tasted,
but by the way she trembled,
like guilt had her by the throat.
That’s when I knew 
we weren’t supposed to.
________________


Karma Kissed Me Back
I wake up smelling her perfume in rooms she ain’t never been in.
Vanilla and violets, thick like a whispered warning,
clinging to my sheets, my skin, my sins.
I shower twice but she don’t rinse off 
she lingers, soaks into my marrow like she got a key to my body.
I tell myself it’s nothing.
Just another night.
Just another woman.
But lies don’t hold weight against a soul tie,
and I feel her in places prayers don’t reach.
She left her laugh in my throat.
Left her breath on my neck.
Left her name in my mouth like a prayer I wasn’t supposed to say.
Now, every woman I touch feels like a mistake,
like they borrowing a body that don’t belong to them.
Like I’m renting my skin out to ghosts.
I close my eyes,
but behind the lids, I see her 
bare-shouldered and bitten-lipped,
moaning secrets into my palms,
fingernails digging scripture into my back.
I swear I hear her whisper, "More."
 And I give.
And I give.
And I give.
Then she leaves,
slipping through my fingers like a promise she never meant to keep.
And I swear I can hear the door lock behind her.
But she don’t be gone.
Not really.
She stay in the way I move.
Stay in the way my bed feel too big when I sleep alone.
Stay in the way I flinch when my phone ring,
half-expecting it to be her, half-hoping it ain’t.
Stay in the way my hands still remember her hips,
like they were sculpted just to hold them.
I see her man once.
At a gas station, buying her favorite flowers.
White lilies.
Innocence.
A lie in a bouquet.
I wonder if he smells me on her the way I smell her on me.
Wonder if he kisses her slow,
if she closes her eyes and pictures my lips instead.
I tell myself I ain’t care.
Tell myself she was just a body,
a moment,
a mistake.
But my heart beat different now.
Like it’s been tampered with.
Like somebody else got their hands on the controls.
They say soul ties don’t break easy.
I’m startin’ to think they don’t break at all.
________________
"The Prayer I Can’t Say"
I tried to pray last night.
Sat on the edge of my bed, hands folded,
like maybe if I gripped them tight enough,
I could press the sin out my skin.
But my tongue wouldn’t move.
Wouldn’t form the words.
Wouldn’t dare speak God’s name
with her still on my breath.
I know He saw me.
Not just that night all of them.
Saw me trace fire on skin that wasn’t mine,
saw me press my lips to stolen fruit,
saw me swallow guilt like it was communion wine.
And still come back for more.
I know better.
Mama raised me better.
Grandma prayed over me like I was a house to be blessed,
but I keep kicking the doors open,
inviting ghosts in,
letting demons make themselves comfortable.
And now, my own name feels foreign in my mouth,
like I ain’t even the same man who grew up in Sunday school,
who used to believe in mercy,
who used to believe in himself.
So I sit in the dark.
Hands still locked,
lips still sealed,
with a prayer I can’t say
and a God who already knows.
________________
"The Guilt Feels Like Her Perfume"
It lingers.
Sticks to my skin like sweat after sin,
like cigarette smoke in a Sunday suit,
like the echo of a name I shouldn’t have whispered.
Her perfume jasmine, vanilla, and something dangerous,
something that smelled like promises neither of us could keep.
I swear I still catch it in empty rooms,
like the guilt found a way to haunt me.
I shower three times.
Scrub my hands raw, like I’m trying to wash off the crime,
but the scent won’t leave.
Neither will the weight.
Her laugh sweet and wrong at the same time.
Her touch warm, then burning, then ice.
The way she looked at me like a woman who already knew regret
but didn’t care enough to stop.
I should have stopped.
Should have seen the gold band before I saw her lips.
Should have asked her name before I memorized her body.
Should have left before she whispered,
"No one has to know."
But someone always knows.
Even if it’s just me.
Even if it’s just God.
Even if it’s just the walls in my bedroom,
soaked in the scent of her sins and mine.
And now, every time I breathe in,
it smells like jasmine, vanilla 
and a mistake that won’t fade.


"I Knew Better"
The sheets still smell like thunder.
Like rain-soaked pavement and something electric,
something that crackled between us before the storm ever hit.
I should’ve left when the sky warned me,
when the air got thick,
when the wind told me her name didn’t belong in my mouth.
But I stayed.
Let her hands become lightning 
striking, searing, setting fire to my good sense.
Let her lips taste like a crime scene 
red, wet, begging to be silenced.
Let her body be the altar where I laid my conscience down
and walked away baptized in sweat and secrets.
I knew better.
Knew the way her voice dropped when she said, "Don’t ask me that,"
knew the way she flinched when my phone rang at midnight,
knew the way her nails dug too deep,
like she was trying to hold onto something slipping from her grasp.
I knew better,
but I still traced her back home in the scent of hotel linen.
Still let her name sit heavy in my chest like an unpaid debt.
Still caught myself watching the door,
waiting for karma to walk in wearing her husband’s face.
And now, every room is too small.
Every silence is too loud.
Every touch feels borrowed, stolen, not mine to keep.
I knew better,
but knowing ain’t ever stopped a man from burning
when he’s already too close to the flame.


________________
We Never Spoke Again
I tell myself it was just a night.
Just skin on skin, just hands that shouldn’t have wandered,
just lips that knew better and didn’t care.
But I feel her still.
Like a bad habit, like a song I hate but can’t stop humming.
She left first.
Didn’t fumble for excuses, didn’t linger.
Just slipped her ring back on,
checked her reflection like she was fixing a smudge,
and walked out 
like I was nothing but a moment to be cleaned up.
I stared at the door,
waiting for it to knock, waiting for her to forget something,
waiting for her to come back and say what?
That she wanted me? That she regretted it?
That she felt it too?
She didn’t.
And I didn’t call.
Didn’t text.
Didn’t reach for a name I wasn’t supposed to have.
A week passed. Then two.
Then a month.
And the silence started growing teeth.
I’d hear a laugh that sounded like hers and my stomach would drop.
I’d see a red dress and taste regret.
She became a shadow I couldn’t shake.
A question I couldn’t answer.
A sin that never settled right in my chest.
And we never spoke again.
Not because we didn’t want to 
but because we knew if we did,
we’d do it all over again.
________________
Her Husband Didn’t
He never knew.
Never caught the scent of sin on her skin,
never traced the fingerprints I left like ink.
He held her hand, kissed her cheek,
while I sat in the dark with a mouth full of secrets.
She laid beside him, spine straight,
breathing even, lips stitched tight.
Did she dream of me?
Did she flinch when he whispered her name,
when his fingers grazed where mine had been?
I wonder if he noticed the way she blinked too slow,
the way her smile had a second of delay,
like her heart was still catching up,
like she was pulling herself back from another man’s arms.
I saw them once together.
A picture-perfect frame of vows unshaken.
He laughed, deep and easy,
like he had no reason to fear the storm sitting right beside him.
I almost pitied him.
A king without a crown, a fool with a queen he couldn’t see.
A man who prayed at night while his wife was on her knees for me.
But I wasn’t bold.
I wasn’t brave.
I was just a ghost in their house, a stain on her soul,
a crack in the vows that never quite broke 
but bent, bent, bent.
And her husband?
Her husband didn’t know.
Didn’t see.
Didn’t wake up in the middle of the night
to a body that wasn’t fully his.
Didn’t hear my name in the silence.
Didn’t feel my touch in the spaces between.
Didn’t realize he was sharing a love
that no longer belonged to him.

Her Silence Is My Sentence
You ever been punished without a word?
Not a slam, not a scream, not a "you ain't shit" 
just silence, clean like a cut from a blade too sharp to feel.
She ain't block me outta anger.
She just…
stopped seein’ me.
Ain’t that some cold shit?
I tell myself she doin’ it to teach me a lesson,
so I can pretend I still matter in the story.
Like maybe,
just maybe,
she’ll break the silence when she ready to forgive me.
‘Cause ain't no way she just left,
like I was never somethin' she cared to lose.
Nah.
She gotta be playin’ chess,
treatin’ me like a pawn to prove a point.
I mean,
why else would a woman like her walk away
from a man like me?
...Right?
But I know the truth, deep down where I don’t visit often.
She ain’t punishin’ me.
She just found peace,
and a man like me got no place in it.
And that?
That’s worse than any cuss out,
any slap,
any tear-filled goodbye.
That’s a sentence with no parole.
Ain’t no breakin’ silence
when she don't even hear me no more.

She Loved Me, Then She Left Me
See, baby girl moved like jazz 
smooth, effortless, like she knew the rhythm before I even heard the beat.
She’d laugh, low and smoky,
like she already knew the punchline to a joke I ain’t tell yet.
I thought I had her,
thought my name was carved somewhere deep inside her,
like scripture,
like gospel,
like she’d be singin’ me under her breath for years to come.
But nah.
She was just dancin’ in my arms ‘til the music changed.
And when it did?
She ain't stumble.
She ain't hesitate.
She just let go,
like a breeze slippin’ past an open window.
Gone.
No fight.
No tears.
Not even a scene.
Just a slow blink, a soft smirk,
like she was thankin’ me for the lesson.
And bruh, I ain’t gon’ lie 
I thought I could double back,
slide in smooth like I never left,
hit her with that, “Damn, you still think ‘bout me?”
But when I did?
She just laughed.
Ain’t even the type of laugh you wanna hear 
nah, this was that knowing laugh,
that I’m good, baby laugh,
that I don’t even dream about you no more laugh.
And man, I felt that.
‘Cause I spent all this time thinking I was the storm in her life,
but I ain’t even leave a puddle.
She walked away dry,
left me drowning in my own ego.
And that?
That’s the coldest game I ever got played in.
