I Taught Her How to Forget Me
I gave her the blueprint,
the exit strategy,
the step-by-step manual on how to unlove a man like me.
Didn’t even know I was doin’ it 
thought I was just movin’ the way I always moved,
thought she’d always be waitin’ right there,
soft hands, warm lips, open door.
But nah,
she studied every time I ain’t answer,
took notes when I made her feel small,
highlighted the nights I chose the streets over her sheets.
She put all that shit in bold, underlined it twice,
then turned it into scripture.
I taught her patience,
but only in the way you learn waitin’ for a train that don’t ever come.
Taught her silence,
but only ‘cause she got tired of beggin’ me to listen.
Taught her strength,
but only in the way you build muscle from carryin’ a love that was never meant to be light.
See, I ain’t know I was showin’ her the way out.
Didn’t know every cold shoulder was a lesson in detachment,
every missed call was a practice round for forever.
Didn’t realize I was training her to walk away without lookin’ back.
And when she left?
She ain't hesitate.
Ain’t no long texts, no please reconsider, no last kiss.
She just went.
Like she never been here.
Like I never mattered.
Like she learned the trick of disappearin’
without even leavin’ a shadow behind.
And now, I stand in rooms we used to fill,
say her name just to see if the walls remember.
But they silent.
Just like she is.
Just like I taught her to be.

I Didn’t Deserve Her
She was a sun-drenched morning wrapped in silk,
and I was a long night that overstayed its welcome.
She came in like cinnamon and rosewater,
something you savor slow, something you sip on careful,
and I was rust in a crystal glass 
something that look like wine ‘til you taste it.
She wrote me in soft cursive,
letters pressed deep into the page,
like she thought we’d be read forever.
And I signed my name in vanishing ink,
smudged fingerprints, a story half-told,
a man who loved the idea of love but never the weight of it.
She poured patience in my wounds,
stitched my ego with thread spun from mercy,
kissed my history like she wasn’t scared of ghosts.
And I?
I let her.
Took her softness like a thief with dirty hands,
wore her love like a winter coat in July,
flung it off soon as the heat got too real.
See, I didn’t deserve her.
Didn’t deserve the way she looked at me like I was the answer,
when I was just a riddle with no clean ending.
Didn’t deserve the prayers she whispered with my name in ‘em,
the way she carved space for me in a future I never earned.
Didn’t deserve the way she tried 
God, she tried 
to build a home in a man who only knew how to be a locked door.
And now?
She’s somewhere laughin’ with a man who knows how to stay,
who don’t flinch when love calls his name,
who don’t mistake devotion for a cage.
And me?
I sit in rooms too quiet,
pour drinks too strong,
write poems too late.
But poetry ain’t a time machine,
and apologies don’t hold the weight of a woman who finally learned
that loving me was just another way of losing herself.
________________
I Almost Called Her
The idea of a call sat heavy in my hands,
like a glass I wasn’t sure I wanted to shatter.
Her number still lived in muscle memory,
a ghost beneath my fingertips,
waiting to be summoned 
waiting to remind me that I was once loved
by something softer than regret.
I almost called her.
Almost let the whiskey turn courage,
let the night slip its hands around my throat
and press out an apology too late to matter.
Almost let my voice be a door she thought was locked,
let my breath be the draft that stirred old ashes.
But what could I say?
That the silence between us felt like a sermon?
That I watched the moon some nights,
wondering if she still loved the way it spilled silver
across city streets and sleeping bodies?
That every woman I held after her
was just another way to hold my own mistakes?
I almost called her.
Almost begged for a chance to stand in the wreckage
and pretend I had the hands to rebuild.
Almost fed her my best performance,
the one where I finally know what love is,
the one where I’ve changed,
the one where I never let her down.
But I ain’t a good enough liar for that.
Ain’t delusional enough to pretend
I was anything more than a beautiful mistake
she finally learned to stop making.
So I set the phone down,
let the moment pass,
and sat in the quiet I earned.
________________
I Could Write Her a Thousand Poems
I could write her a thousand poems,
each one dipped in the honey of Solomon’s verses,
lined with the silk of a love too sweet to swallow.
But she don’t read scripture no more,
and my voice ain’t strong enough
to resurrect what died in her hands.
She was my garden,
spiced with longing,
lips like pomegranates split in the heat of July 
but another man walked through her gates,
drank deep from her well,
and left me outside the walls,
begging for a taste of what was mine.
She told me love was patience,
but she never waited.
Told me love was trust,
but she never trusted me.
Told me love was sacrifice,
but I was the only one bleeding.
I could write her a thousand poems,
but they’d only collect dust
like the prayers I stopped saying.
Like the "come back" I swallowed whole.
Like the man I could've been,
if she just believed in me enough to stay.
She was supposed to be my crown,
my Proverbs 31,
the woman wise enough to see the king in me 
but maybe I was just another fool,
sitting in the ruins of a throne
I was never meant to wear.

VI. The Wisdom of Wounds        
I Was Her Temptress
I ain’t never been no saint,
but I ain’t never been no devil neither.
I just held out my hand,
and she put hers in it.
That ain’t a sin, is it?
She say I led her to ruin,
but she was already walkin’ barefoot on glass
long before I came whisperin’ in her ear.
She called me temptation,
like I was somethin’ forbidden,
somethin’ dangerous 
but I was just a mirror,
and she ain’t like what she saw.
Ain’t it funny, how a woman
can make you her weakness,
but blame you for her fall?
Like I ain’t stumble too.
Like I ain’t wake up with her ghost on my lips,
prayin’ for a woman I never learned to deserve.
She tellin’ folks I wrecked her,
left her love-dizzy and ruined,
but she the one who packed up her heart
and put it in another man’s hands.
Now I’m the one watchin’ from the door,
swearin’ I ain’t bitter,
but wonderin’ how my arms went empty
when I was the one she couldn’t get enough of.
If I was her mistake,
why she still say my name
like it taste too good to forget?
________________
The Cost of a King
The throne was never built to last 
gold-leaf promises peeling,
velvet sins unraveling at the seams.
I sat high, above love, above guilt,
above the wreckage of every woman
who ever knelt before me.
I called it a kingdom.
But glass don’t hold weight,
and I was heavy with the ghosts of my own making.
She told me once "Karma don’t care about crowns."
 I laughed,
thought I was untouchable,
thought my name was carved in the marble of memory.
But marble cracks.
And memory fades.
Now my castle is a mausoleum,
my throne a coffin,
my kingdom nothing but dust
blown back into my eyes.
I reach for mirrors that show me nothing.
I speak to echoes that won’t answer.
I beg for women who only remember
how I made them forget themselves.
This is the weight of a king who ain't real.
This is the gold of my life, melting into rust.
________________
Too Wise to Be Saved
I was never the fool. Never the one caught slippin’.
I had a playbook so thick, even karma had to flip through it twice.
Knew how to hold a woman like a prayer
without ever kneeling,
how to make ‘em whisper my name
like scripture they never meant to believe in.
I spoke in riddles, in half-truths wrapped like gold chains,
said just enough to keep ‘em reaching 
never enough to let ‘em catch me.
But knowledge ain’t wisdom.
And wisdom don’t keep you warm at night.
You can master the game and still lose the house.
You can hold the map, trace every road back to her hands,
and still get lost.
I shoulda known that. I shoulda seen it coming.
But I never thought the day would come
where my own reflection wouldn’t look me in the eye.
Where my own voice would feel like a stranger’s.
An open Bible sits on my nightstand 
I ain’t touched it in years,
but somehow, the words keep flipping themselves open
like they waiting for me to listen.
Like they know the weight I carry.
Like they know how heavy regret sits in a man’s bones.
And now, my shadow won’t even stand beside me.
I cut my own strings thinking I was free,
but a puppet without a master
ain’t nothing but limp wood and silence.
I was too wise to be saved,
too proud to be humble,
too empty to be full.
And maybe, just maybe,
too late to be anything else.
________________
When The Music Stopped
I used to walk in a room and feel the bass bow down.
Women turned when I spoke,
men nodded when I moved 
I was the song that got stuck in your head,
the chorus you couldn’t help but sing.
Now?
The record skips.
The track ran out.
And I’m still standing here,
watching the smoke clear like I wasn’t the one that lit the fire.
The club is empty now.
A few tables flipped, glasses half-drunk,
ghosts of last night still clinging to the walls.
Used to be, I was the noise, the reason the night stayed wide-eyed.
Now I’m just silence,
listening to my own heartbeat
like it’s the only rhythm left.
Checked my phone no missed calls.
No where you at?,
no come back, baby.
Just a screen as black as the space I take up.
Once, I had ‘em wrapped around my fingers,
playing ‘em like a DJ with a fresh vinyl,
spinning ‘em into love,
remixing their pain into something they could dance to.
Now?
Ain’t nobody left on the floor but me.
I used to move crowds.
Now I move alone.
The music stopped.
And the only thing left playing
is regret.
________________
I Built My Own Hell
I ain’t fall. Let’s get that straight.
Ain’t no trip, no push, no trick of fate.
I walked in with my chest out,
head high like I was immune to flame,
like the fire was just another woman
I could whisper my way into.
Like heat only burns the weak.
But fire don’t care who you think you are.
And when the smoke cleared,
all that was left was me 
hands covered in soot,
heart blackened like the bed of ashes I made myself lay in.
I used to call this place a kingdom.
Used to think power meant how many loved me,
how many cried my name into their pillows,
how many turned back just once before walking away.
Now my throne is a chair with three legs,
wobbling under the weight of my own doing,
and the echoes in these walls ain’t begging me to stay 
they’re laughing.
Mocking.
Repeating every lie I ever told a woman
like a mixtape on repeat,
reminding me that the devil don’t always come knocking 
sometimes he just waits for you to come home.
I built this.
Brick by brick.
Every game I played, every love I faked,
every time I left and thought the road back would stay open.
I stacked my sins like poker chips,
betting against the house,
thinking luck was loyalty.
Thinking I was the only one who’d never have to pay.
But the thing about hell?
Ain’t no doors.
No exits.
No arms waiting to pull you back.
Just you and the weight of everything you ever did,
piled high like an unmade bed you too ashamed to lay in.
I built my own hell.
And I live here now.
________________
No One Prays for Players
Ain’t nobody light candles for men like me.
Ain’t no hymns sung soft for the ones who had it all
and let it slip between ringed fingers and whispered names.
No tear-streaked cheeks call out for my soul.
No mother on her knees asking God to bring me home safe.
The pews stay empty where my name should be.
I walk past a church, doors bolted shut.
I knock. Ain’t nobody answer.
God don’t talk loud to men who only listen in loss.
Only sound I hear is the wind through the cracks,
like a breath too tired to speak my sins aloud.
I remember women that once called me king,
hands on my chest, praying in ways I didn’t deserve.
Now they don’t speak my name. Not in love.
Not in hate.
Not at all.
The only mouths that mention me
do it with regret heavy on their tongues,
like my name is something bitter they can’t swallow.
"Shoulda known better."
"Shoulda never answered that call."
"Shoulda left when I had the chance."
Shoulda. Shoulda. Shoulda.
I am a lesson wrapped in velvet and cigarette smoke,
a ghost with a body, walking through rooms
where no one looks up anymore.
Who prays for the man who played himself?
Who cries for the fool who ran toward the flood,
dared the waves to take him,
and then screamed for a rope when the tide pulled too strong?
Ain’t no mercy for a man who knew better.
Ain’t no redemption for the one who ran toward the storm
with a smile on his lips,
thinking the rain could never drown him.
________________
I Thought I Had Time
I walked like the sun would never set on me,
like the streetlights would hold their glow just a little longer,
like I could take the long way home
and still find her porch light burning.
I thought love had no expiration date,
that apologies never spoiled,
that regret was just a word
and not a weight with a name.
I thought I had time 
to fix the wreckage,
to unburn the bridges,
to say “I love you”
without it sounding like a eulogy.
But time don’t wait for men who whisper “tomorrow.”
It don’t stop for hands too proud to knock,
for lips too stubborn to speak first.
It don’t care how bad I want to rewind,
how many nights I sit in silence,
waiting for a voice that ain’t coming back.
Now, her name is a prayer I ain’t got the right to say.
Her memory is a door that don’t swing both ways.
And the clock God, that clock 
ain’t got no hands left for me.


________________


God, Are You Still There?
Knees pressed into a floor that never learned my name,
palms folded like they knew what mercy was,
mouth open but only silence comes out 
ain’t that just like me? Always late, always empty.
The ceiling don’t move when I whisper.
The air don’t shift when I breathe Your name.
Ain’t no fire, no thunder, no proof You still listening,
but I ain’t got nowhere else to turn.
I been king of ruin, prince of regret,
carved my throne from women’s ribs,
drank loyalty like it couldn’t run dry 
but it did. Lord, You know it did.
I called love a weakness,
called loss a game,
called faith a fairytale
until I woke up alone.
Her perfume still lingers in the air like a prayer I never answered,
her silence stretches wider than the grave I dug for us.
I wore betrayal like a coat made of wolf fur,
but damn, God, it’s cold now.
I ran with devils who laughed in smoke rings,
who taught me sin like a second language,
who kissed my knuckles and told me power ain’t free.
I paid every price but peace.
The mirrors don’t even hold my face no more,
just a hollow thing shaped like a man,
a shadow that can’t outrun itself.
Ain’t that what hell is?
I ain’t praying for a second chance.
I know what I did, know where I been,
know the blood ain’t dried on my hands yet,
but damn, Lord, I miss being clean.
I miss waking up without the weight of ghosts,
miss love before I poisoned it,
miss mornings that didn’t taste like regret.
I ain’t asking to be saved, just to be seen.
I hear my own voice echo in the stillness,
bouncing off these walls like a sermon with no audience.
The wind moves, just barely,
and for a second, I swear it ain’t mine.
Maybe that’s You. Maybe it ain’t.
Maybe grace is just a debt You don’t collect on time.
Maybe You let men like me kneel in the wreckage
just long enough to remember who built it.
I ain’t asking You to fix it.
I ain’t asking for the past to unburn.
I just wanna know if You still watch men like me,
or if I buried myself too deep to be found.
The silence don’t change.
The sky don’t crack open.
But my hands still shake when I say Your name,
and for the first time, I think that’s enough.
God, are You still there?
I don’t deserve an answer,
but I ask anyway.
                                                   
________________


VII. The Rebuilding                                
The Uncrowned King
I stand among the ruins of my kingdom,
Throne toppled, crown shattered, subjects gone 
Not by enemy’s hand or rival’s sword,
But by my own design, my own undoing.
I built an empire on shifting sands,
On bodies I never held till morning,
On promises whispered but never kept,
On hearts collected then discarded.
Solomon had wisdom, had wealth, had women 
A thousand lovers to warm his bed,
A thousand songs to praise his name,
A thousand prayers to bless his reign.
And I? I chased his shadow,
Mistook conquest for power,
Mistook desire for love,
Mistook fear for strength.
They called me king, and I believed them.
They knelt before me, and I accepted it.
They gave me their bodies, and I took them.
They offered their hearts, and I refused them.
For what is a king who must surrender?
What is a ruler who must be ruled?
What is a god who must be human?
I was too afraid to find out.
But kingdoms fall, and crowns tarnish,
And even Solomon, in all his glory,
Found himself humbled before greater powers 
Time. Truth. Consequence. Love.
These are the real rulers, the true sovereigns,
The forces that bring even kings to their knees,
That strip away pretense and performance,
That reveal the man beneath the myth.
She saw it first the woman who wouldn’t kneel.
She looked at me with eyes too clear, too knowing,
And said, “You’re not Solomon, baby.
You’re just a man who likes the sound of his own name.”
And I hated her for that truth,
For that mirror she held up without mercy,
For seeing the boy hiding in the man’s clothes,
For knowing I was more costume than king.
Then came the one who prayed for me,
Who held my hands like they were something holy,
Who saw past the walls I’d built so high
To the broken foundation underneath.
She loved me not for my performance,
Not for my crown or my conquests,
But for the man she believed I could be
If I just had the courage to try.
And I ran from that too 
Ran from her faith, her hope, her love,
Ran from the possibility of becoming
Something more than my father’s son,
Something more than my fears and failures,
Something more than the sum of my sins.
I ran until there was nowhere left to go,
Until I found myself back where I started.
Alone.
This is the wisdom wounds teach us:
That power without purpose is just noise,
That pleasure without connection is just emptiness,
That pride without humility is just delusion.
This is the lesson Solomon learned too late:
That all the wives in the world can’t fill
The space where love should be,
That all the gold can’t warm a cold heart.
So here I stand in the aftermath,
In the quiet after the storm,
In the stillness after the fall,
In the silence after the music stops.
I have no crown, no throne, no kingdom 
Just these hands that have held too much
And kept too little,
Just this heart that has wanted too much
And given too little.
But maybe that’s where rebuilding begins 
Not with grand gestures or royal decrees,
Not with conquest or collection,
But with the simple act of surrender.
Of saying, “I was wrong.”
Of saying, “I am sorry.”
Of saying, “I am afraid.”
Of saying, “I need help.”
Perhaps the true measure of a king
Is not how many subjects he commands,
But how many times he can fall
And still find the strength to rise.
Not how many hearts he can conquer,
But how many lives he can touch.
Not how many women he can possess,
But how deeply he can love just one.
I am not Solomon. I never was.
I am just a man with scars and stories,
With regrets and possibilities,
With a past I cannot change
And a future I can still shape.
I am just a man learning, finally,
That the greatest wisdom comes not from ruling,
But from serving;
Not from taking,
But from giving;
Not from hardening,
But from opening.
The kingdom I thought I built has fallen,
But from its ashes, something new might rise 
Not an empire of conquest and collection,
But a sanctuary of truth and connection.
Not a throne built on fear and performance,
But a home founded on courage and presence.
Not a crown that separates and elevates,
But a circle that includes and embraces.
This is my uncrowned glory,
My throned humility,
My powerful surrender.
This is my song of Solomon,
Not as he was in legend and lore,
But as he might have been
In his quietest moments,
When the crown was set aside,
When the throne room was empty,
When he was just a man
Facing the same stars we all face,
Asking the same questions we all ask:
Did I love well?
Did I live fully?
Did I learn what matters?
And finding, perhaps,
That the answers were always
Simpler than he thought,
Closer than he knew,
Waiting in the spaces between heartbeats,
In the silence between words,
In the warmth between bodies,
In the trust between souls.
I am the uncrowned king,
The ruler who finally learned to be ruled,
The conqueror who finally learned to surrender,
The man who finally learned to be human.
And maybe that’s enough.
Maybe that’s everything.
                                
                                        








________________




Back Matter
 Author’s Note
When I began writing these poems, I had no intention of creating a collection, let alone one that traced a journey from conquest to contemplation. Each piece                                                        
emerged from a moment sometimes of triumph, sometimes of loss, sometimes of that peculiar emptiness that follows getting exactly what you thought you wanted.                                        
I wrote about love the way I experienced it: as a game, a battlefield, a transaction. I wrote about women as conquests, as mirrors, as ghosts. I wrote about myself as untouchable, unbreakable, unstoppable.                        
But somewhere along the way, the writing itself became a mirror I couldn’t look away from. The words revealed patterns I hadn’t wanted to see, truths I hadn’t been ready to face. The man on these pages arrogant, afraid, always running was me, stripped of pretense and performance.                                        
This collection represents not just a creative journey, but a personal reckoning. The Solomon figure who begins these poems believing himself a king ends them understanding what true power looks like: the courage to be vulnerable, the strength to be honest, the wisdom to be still.                                        
I don’t claim to have completed this journey in my own life. Some days I’m still that man in the first section, believing his own mythology. But in creating this collection, in arranging these pieces into a narrative of fall and potential redemption, I’ve created a map for myself a way forward through the ruins of what I once thought defined me.                                        
My hope is that these poems speak to anyone who has ever mistaken conquest for connection, who has ever hidden behind a crown of their own making, who has ever stood in the wreckage of their own design and wondered if rebuilding is possible.
                                        
It is. That’s the promise these poems make. Not that the rebuilding will be easy or that the new structure will resemble the old, but that from ruins, something authentic can emerge something not built on fear, but on truth.
                                        
Thank you for walking this path with me.
                                        
Acknowledgments                                        
To the women who appear in these pages some composites, some all too real thank you for the lessons, even the painful ones. Especially the painful ones.                                        
To my mother, who never stopped believing there was a better man inside me, even when I gave her every reason to doubt your faith was a light I ran from until I was ready to be found.                                        
To the mentors who pushed me to dig deeper, to write truer, to face the page with honesty even when honesty hurt your challenges made this collection what it is.                                         
To the readers who find themselves in these words whether in the swagger of the early poems or the humility of the later ones I see you. I was you. In some ways, I am still you. We’re all just trying to figure out how to be human in a world that often rewards us for being anything but.                                        
And finally, to myself the boy I was, the boy I am, the man I’m trying to become grace. Always grace. The journey continues.                                 
                                                                                                                                                          About the Author
Kiminou Knox is a writer, athlete, and creative force emerging from East Palo Alto, California. A poet and storyteller with a voice both sharp and introspective, he crafts narratives that explore
the weight of history, the search for identity, and the deep- rooted spiritual and cultural legacies
that shape Black existence. At just 18, he has already made his mark with Poems from a Black
Boy, weaving words that pulse with emotion, truth, and an unflinching gaze at
the realities of life. With Spirit of Solomon his Sophomore TItle, Kiminou delves into themes of legacy, resilience, and the unseen forces that bind us to our past. His work bridges the poetic and the profound, drawing readers into layered stories that challenge, heal, and ignite something raw within the soul. Beyond writing, he carries the discipline of an athlete, the vision of an entrepreneur, and the spirit of a storyteller determined to leave an impact. As he moves toward a future literature and sports journalism, his voice continues to grow one that demands to be heard, one that refuses to be                                                                              forgotten.                                
                        
                                        
                


    Hopeless Romantic
    A Collection Of Poems








                                           




                                                


Kiminou Knox        
                                
                                        
                                  
                  Copyright © 2025 All rights reserved.                                                        
                                                        












                         Dedication


For those who have loved and died internally from it but to always understand that there is always hope in love                                         






“The emotion that can break your heart is sometimes the very one that heals it.”
  Nicholas Sparks


“What is to give light must endure burning.”
  Viktor E. Frankl


“Hope deferred makes the heart sick,
but a longing fulfilled is a tree of life.”
  Proverbs 13:12
        
        


Table of Contents
★ Act I – The Offering
Come Heal Here-Love Is a Soft Place to Fall-Why Won’t You Love Me-So God… There’s This Girl-I Don’t Need Much (Just You)- Do You Believe in Soulmates?- Let Me Love You in Peace
✦ Act II – The Cracks
How Did We Get Here- Say It Back- Maybe I’m Just Imagining It-Talking to Your Silence-One-Sided Prayers- Delivered Again- You Didn’t Have to Pretend-If I Disappear, Will You Notice?
❖ Act III – The Spiral
I Swear You Felt It Too-This Song Sounds Like Us-I Dreamed You Said It-Still, Sometimes-53 Days-Even If You Don’t Want Me-I’ll Wait-That Almost That Killed Me
 ♱ Act IV – The Break
FUCK YOU BITCH- You Played Me-Time I’ll Never Get Back -You Wasn’t Even My Baddest Bitch-Could’ve Done Better-I Should’ve Let You Drown-You Made Me Soft Just to Break Me- I Don’t Write About You No More-Was I Ever Enough?
♱ Act V – The Release
I Can Love Us Both-I Don’t Need You to Choose Me- Stay As Long As You Need-I Was There When You Fell Apart-This Isn’t Your Fault-Loving You Isn’t a Mistake- I’d Still Pick You- My Hopeless Romantic Homegirl- If You Called, I’d Still Answer- You Looked Back Once-One Day We’ll Laugh About This-Hopeless Romantic
Introduction
I wasn’t supposed to still feel it.
Not this long after. Not this deep.
But here I am still saving a seat for someone who left without saying goodbye. This isn’t a poetry book. Not really.
It’s a memory spiral. A fever dream.
A collection of every time I should’ve let go but didn’t.
Of every “wyd?” I answered when I knew better.
Every ghost. Every maybe. Every goddamn almost.
I didn’t write these poems in order. I didn’t live them that way either.
Some days I was healed.
Other days I still flinched at their name.
Sometimes I wanted to pray for them.
Sometimes I wanted to scream into their voicemail.
I did both. You ever reread your own messages and feel like a stranger?
Yeah. That.This is about the love that never fully landed.
The kind that lingers in your throat,
makes you feel stupid for being soft,
but even worse for pretending you’re not.
These poems are for the nights you lied and said you were fine.
The mornings you made excuses for why they disappeared.
For the phone that lights up and doesn’t say their name.
And for the times it does and ruins your whole week.It’s all in here.
The devotion. The delusion. The death of hope.
The way you keep setting the table for someone who never shows up.
This is what it looks like when you love out loud and it echoes.
This is what happens when your heart doesn’t get the memo.
This is what “moving on” really means when your soul’s still checking the door.You’re not crazy.
You’re not too much.
You’re just a hopeless romantic in a world that keeps pretending it doesn’t care.
So here.
Take what breaks you.
Keep what heals you.
Leave what hurts too bad to hold. I’ll still be here.
Writing about the silence like it ever loved me back.
 Kiminou




Come Heal Here
Come heal here.
Not out of rebellion.
Not to prove a point.
But because I see the parts of you
he never bothered to learn.
You don’t have to explain
why you stayed.
I know what it’s like
to mistake consistency for love
and survival for joy.
But this ain’t that.
This is different.
I won’t love you with rules.
I’ll love you in languages
you forgot you knew.
Gentle.
Present.
Patient.
I won’t ask you to be strong
when you’re tired.
Or soft
when you’re shaking.
I’ll just be here.
Still.
Close.
Warm.
Let him keep his ego.
Let him keep the version of you
he broke into shape.
Bring me the woman you hide in mirrors.
The one who flinches
when the door slams too hard.
The one who cries without making a sound.
The one who used to laugh
with her whole body
before he taught her to hold it in.
Come heal here.
I’ll run the bath.
Pour the wine.
Light every candle that says:
you are allowed to rest.
 you are allowed to be loved without performance.
You don’t have to beg for space.
Or earn touch.
Or apologize for wanting more
than routine kisses
and cold apologies
that come too late.
He called you dramatic.
I call you divine.
He loved the mask.
I want the face underneath.
He memorized your curves.
I want your courage.
Your chaos.
Your truth.
Come heal here.
Not because I want to fix you
but because I know what it feels like
to ache in places nobody can see
and still keep showing up
like nothing’s bleeding.
Let me hold what he dropped.
Let me water what he withered.
Let me love you
like home never had walls.
I am not a maybe.
I am not a threat.
I am the hands that don’t shake
when you fall apart.
Come heal here.
You don’t have to stay
where your light goes unnoticed.  Come where you are not
too much.

Love Is a Soft Place to Fall
You don’t know how to fall.
Not gently.
Not without checking for knives beneath the cushion.
Not without apology stitched into your landing.
You don’t know how to land
because you were never taught there could be arms waiting.
Only impact.
Only concrete.
Only silence that bites back
and doors that slam louder
than any goodbye.
You don’t cry pretty.
You don’t break cute.
You shatter in private.
Gasp behind closed doors.
Bleed behind lipstick.
Smile with a mouth full of glass
because nobody ever stayed long enough
to help you pick the pieces out.
You don’t rest.
You recover.
You rebuild yourself faster than the people who broke you.
You call that strength.
They call it intimidating.
I call it tragic.
Necessary.
Unfair.
You deserve more
than the strength that was forced on you.
You deserve softness
that doesn’t ask you to earn it.
So let me be that.
Let me be the couch you collapse on.
Let me be the night light
that doesn’t ask why you’re still scared of the dark.
Let me be the pillow
that smells like peace
and doesn’t keep score.
I don’t want your best self.
I want your real one.
The one with mascara streaked down her cheek
and dreams you’re scared to say out loud
because everyone keeps telling you to be realistic.
F*ck that.
Give me the version of you
that still wants fairytales
even if she won’t admit it.
Give me the girl who wanted to be chosen first
and stopped asking to be picked at all.
Let me be the place
where you put your guard down,
your bag down,
your mask down,
and nobody leaves.
I won’t fix you.
I’ll just hold you
until the shaking slows.
Until the need to explain fades.
Until the tightness in your chest unclenches
without permission.
Let love be that.
Not the fire.
Not the test.
Not the punishment for wanting too much.
Let love be the bath after the battle.
The quiet hand on your thigh
that doesn’t move.
Just rests.
Says, “I’m still here.”
Let love be a warm hoodie.
A couch nap.
A ride home.
A Sunday morning without pain.
Let love be the bed you fall into.
The breath you didn’t know you were holding
finally leaving your lungs without a fight.
Let me be the mattress.
Let me be the sheets.
Let me be the soft place
you collapse into
and finally
don’t have to leave.
I don’t need to be the love of your life.
Just the one who didn’t let you fall alone.
Why Won’t You Love Me
Why won’t you love me?
Not like a maybe.
Not like a memory you’re afraid to name.
But like a yes.
A stay.
A don’t-move-I’m-still-holding-you kind of love.
I gave you the softest parts of me
before I even knew how to keep them.
And you
you looked me in the face
like you were still deciding
if I was real enough to risk it.
Do you know
what that does to a heart?
To be looked at
like a question
when all you ever wanted
was to be an answer?
I shrank so you could breathe easier.
I quieted my joy so yours could be louder.
I bent myself
into the shape of everything
you said you missed.
And still
you would not love me.
I asked you for honesty
and you handed me silence.
I reached for you with both hands,
and you gave me shoulder.
Cold.
Careless.
Gone.
You never said goodbye.
You just stopped making room for me.
Like I was a phase.
Like I was a fever you were waiting to sweat out.
And God
I waited.
I stayed sick for you.
Why won’t you love me?
When I would’ve cracked myself open
just to keep you warm?
When I would’ve made a home
out of every apology
you never gave?
I carried your absence like inheritance.
Folded it into my walk.
My wanting.
My worth.
And now
when I see your name?
I still flinch like it means something.
Because it did.
Because I did.
I would’ve met you in the middle.
In the ruins.
In the dark.
With a flashlight
and forgiveness.
I would’ve loved you
even if it broke me.
Even if it meant I had to rebuild myself
without your hands.
So tell me
why won’t you love me?
When I was already yours
in every way that counts.
When I begged God to make me
easier to hold.
And quieter to need.
Why won’t you love me?
I am still
and I am shattered
both.
And I would’ve stayed.
I swear to you
I would’ve stayed.


So God… There’s This Girl
So God…
There’s this girl.
And she got me twisted.
Not ‘cause she fine 
even though she is 
but because her silence says more
than most people’s mouths ever could.
She walks past me like I’m invisible,
but I swear her spirit be screaming my name.
She won’t look too long.
Won’t laugh too loud.
Won’t let me in 
‘cause she’s been through men
who only loved her when the lights were off
and the clothes were gone.
She thinks I’m like them.
She thinks I’ll take her heart,
fold it like a note,
and leave it somewhere to rot.
But God, I swear to You 
I only ever wanted to hold it.
Not fix it.
Not own it.
Just honor it.
She been through hell
and still got the nerve to look like heaven.
But I see the smoke in her throat
when she tries to speak soft again.
She’s scared.
Scared that love is just another setup
for disappointment.
Scared that I’m just another boy
with pretty words
and ugly follow-through.
But You made me different, God.
I don’t want a body.
I want a bond.
I don’t want a one-night post.
I want presence.
Consistency.
Safety.
I want the real version of her 
the one she’s buried under every “I’m fine”
and every time she had to pretend
she wasn’t breaking.
I’d take my time.
I’d slow dance in her chaos.
Add her to my plans with the boys.
Pull her in close when she overthinks.
Pick her over pride,
every time.
God,
I don’t care if I just met her.
Her scent moves something eternal in me.
And her mind?
Yo her mind is a galaxy
I’d spend lifetimes exploring.
She ain’t just a girl.
She’s a scripture
You wrote in cursive.
But she’s scared, Lord.
Scared I’ll hurt her.
Scared of softness.
Scared of being seen
and not being enough.
So tonight, when she’s talking to You,
when she says she don’t need nobody 
remind her:
I ain’t nobody.
I’m the somebody
that would never weaponize her vulnerability.
I’m the man
who’d love her past her past.
Tell her she can stop running.
That I’m not chasing her to conquer 
I’m walking beside her to build.
That I’m not perfect,
but I’m real.
And I’m ready.
So God…
There’s this girl.
And if she just gave me one moment,
I’d show her what love feels like
 I Don’t Need Much (Just You)
I don’t need much.
Just a good morning.
Just a text that says you thought of me
before the world started spinning too fast.
Just a soft voice when the day goes heavy.
I don’t need much.
Just a name to say at night
that doesn’t echo.
Just someone who makes the air feel
a little less like pressure
and a little more like breath.
And if it’s you 
God, if it’s you 
then I don’t need anything else.
I swear, I don’t.
I’ll meet you wherever.
Midnight.
Half-asleep.
Mid-panic.
I’ll come with open hands and no questions.
I’ll stay low.
Out the way.
Out the frame.
I just want to know
I’m the one you think of
when the noise clears.
You don’t have to love me loud.
Don’t need fireworks.
Don’t need Instagram captions
or big promises.
Just choose me in the quiet.
Just let me be the text you don’t delete.
Let me be the one you don’t silence when it’s hard.
I don’t need much.
Just you.
In your worst mood.
On your loneliest day.
Even if I’m just background noise.
Even if I’m the soft landing
before you go back to falling elsewhere.
I’ll take it.
I’ll take anything with your name on it.
Even the crumbs.
Even the delay.
Even the almost.
Because you showed up today.
And for the first time in weeks,
my hands stopped shaking.
My mind sat still.
And the sky
finally
felt blue again.
So no,
I don’t need much.
Just don’t disappear.
Just stay.
Just let this mean something
before it doesn’t.
Do You Believe in Soulmates?
Do you believe in soulmates?
Because I do.
Not because I need to.
But because I met you,
and now the idea won’t let go.
I don’t think God made you for me.
I think He made you from me.
Rib-sharp.
Wound-deep.
The kind of missing that walks around
with its own name.
You don’t feel like a coincidence.
You feel like scripture.
Something written in a language
I don’t speak,
but still understand
in the marrow of me.
Before I had a name,
before I was skin and voice and wanting,
I think I knew yours.
I think my soul rehearsed
how to hold yours in the dark.
You walk like a prophecy.
Like an answer I ain’t asked out loud.
And I’ve been quiet my whole life,
but something about you
makes me bold in the places I buried.
Makes me want to write psalms
with my mouth on your collarbone.
Makes me pray for mornings
with your laugh in them.
You don’t even love me yet.
And I still thank God for you
like you’re already mine.
I’m not tryna own you.
I’m trying to recognize you.
The way you speak to plants,
the way you pause in doorways,
like your spirit still checking for peace
before your feet commit.
I’ve studied you.
Not in a creepy way.
In a holy one.
Like you’re sacred text
and I’m just underlining the parts
that feel like home.
I’ve dated before.
Laughed.
Touched.
Fell hard and fast.
But this ain’t that.
This is slow.
This is still.
This is the ache of knowing
you’ve already met your ending
and just waiting on it to say your name back.
You make me believe
in lifetimes.
In fate.
In every movie line that made me roll my eyes.
I get it now.
It’s you.
You ruined me for reason.
So when you ask why I look at you like that 
why I memorize your smile
like it’s a map back to myself 
It’s because I already decided.
You’re the one.
The only.
The always.
And even if you don’t believe in soulmates,
even if you walk away,
I’ll still call you mine
in every life I don’t get to live                                          
 Let Me Love You in Peace
I’m not asking for a reply.
Not a call.
Not a moment of your day.
Not a spot in your life.
I’m not asking for clarity,
or closure,
or a version of you
that feels like mine again.
I’m not hoping for a miracle.
Not anymore.
I’ve outgrown the fantasy
where you turn around.
All I want now
is to love you
quietly.
Without interruption.
Without guilt.
Without shame.
Without your eyes on me
wondering when I’ll let go.
Let me keep this
small and sacred.
Let me hold the part of you
you left behind.
You don’t have to see it.
You don’t have to know.
You don’t have to feel it
when your name still softens my spine.
This love doesn’t need a room anymore.
It just needs a corner.
A drawer.
A place I can visit without judgment.
I won’t post about it.
Won’t write it on my skin.
Won’t turn it into a song
or a reason to bleed again.
Just let me keep it.
Like an old shirt
that doesn’t fit
but still smells like something I can’t throw away.
Let me light a candle for you
in the dark parts of me.
Let me write your name in lowercase
when the world gets too loud.
I won’t bother you.
Won’t show up.
Won’t send letters
you’ll never read.
But let me love you
where no one has to lose.
Where no one has to stay.
Where no one has to lie.
I promise 
I’ll keep it gentle.
Keep it small.
Keep it hidden behind all the parts of me you left intact.
Let me love you
without needing you back.
Without trying to fix what broke.
Without asking for permission.
Let me love you
in peace.
Because loving you loudly
only ever brought me pain.
But here, in the quiet 
I still feel whole
just knowing I once held you
and chose to never unhold.


How Did We Get Here
Just a month ago, you told me you loved me.
Now you say you hate me.
And I’m standin’ here
wonderin’ what I did
to make you so quick
to replaCe me.
Your absence coils inside me,
like fear with a spine.
Who am I without you?
Who do I become
when your presence don’t reach me?
Mi amor
my angel, my everything.
I used to look at the stars
and swear they moved
'cause of you.
You made me feel
like a man I’d never met before.
A man I liked.
But now
you say you hate me,
'cause I slipped.
'Cause I put my body
where it had no business being.
And yeah
I know I was wrong.
But baby, where’s your grace?
I ain't asking you to be no saint,
but damn
can we sit in the same room
without you gettin’ lightheaded
from rage?
I hurt you.
Sometimes, I play like it's just jokes,
but I swear
if roles reversed,
you’d have done the same.
Matter fact
you did
What I did with one,
you did with a few.
A few suitors
who caught your eye
while mine was still locked on you.
And now
now you mad?
You mad 'cause someone else
stole my gaze,
while you was already
givin’ yours away?
I won’t fix you.
I’ll just hold you
until the shaking slows.
Until the need to explain fades.
Until the tightness in your chest unclenches
without permission.
Let love be that.
Not the fire.
Not the test.
Not the punishment for wanting too much.
Let love be the bath after the battle.
The quiet hand on your thigh
that doesn’t move.
Just rests.
Says, “I’m still here.”
Let love be a warm hoodie.
A couch nap.
A ride home.
A Sunday morning without pain.
Let love be the bed you fall into.
The breath you didn’t know you were holding
finally leaving your lungs without a fight.
Let me be the mattress.
Let me be the sheets.
Let me be the soft place
you collapse into
and finally
don’t have to leave.
I don’t need to be the love of your life.
Just the one who didn’t let you fall alone.
Say It Back


Just say it.
Say you don’t love me.
Say you never did.
Say it with a straight face so I can stop waiting for the part
where your voice softens
and you say you were just scared.
Say it so I can stop writing these goddamn poems
like your silence means something more than absence.
Say it so I don’t have to keep decoding
every fucking “how are you?”
like it’s a breadcrumb
leading back to something we never had.
Say it.
Say it ugly.
Say it raw.
Say it in a voice I won’t forget.
Because this halfway love
is eating me alive.
You show up just enough to ruin me.
Text just enough to keep me on the hook.
Laugh at my jokes
then vanish.
Tell me I’m different,
then treat me like everyone else.
You never said you loved me.
But you never said you didn’t.
And that’s worse.
That’s a goddamn slow death.
That’s purgatory.
That’s trying to heal
while the wound keeps texting “u up?”
Do you even know what you’re doing to me?
Do you care?
You walk in and out of my heart
like it’s a fucking Airbnb.
Leave your perfume on the pillow,
your apologies in the fridge,
and your fingerprints on the mirrors
so I see you in every reflection
even when you’re gone.
You tell me I’m good.
You tell me I’m kind.
You tell me I’m patient.
But not loved.
Not chosen.
Not held.
And I keep trying to convince myself
that maybe this is enough 
a couple of glances,
a few “miss you”s,
a voice memo at 1 a.m.
when you’re too tired to lie.
But I’m exhausted.
From filling in the blanks
you keep writing.
I want you to say it.
Say what I already know
but still beg to be wrong about.
Say I was convenience.
Say I was comfort.
Say I was your emotional support system
until your real life showed up.
Say you loved the idea of me,
but not the fact of me.
Not the need.
Not the consistency.
Not the mirror I held up
when you weren’t ready to see yourself.
Say I was never going to be enough
because I never made you work hard enough to want me.
Say I was the wrong kind of chaos.
Too quiet.
Too present.
Too much like safety
and not enough like a high.
Say I didn’t make you nervous.
Say I made you feel seen
and you hate that.
Say you didn’t want love 
you wanted a distraction.
Say it.
So I can stop praying
to a god who only speaks in read receipts
and reposted memes that feel like almost-apologies.
Say it.
So I can finally grieve something real
instead of mourning possibilities
you never planned to choose.
Say it, baby.
Break me honest.
Don’t keep ghosting me
with good grammar
and soft emojis.
Say it
so I don’t die waiting
for a maybe
that was never coming.
I’m tired of playing therapist to your damage
while you breadcrumb me into loving you
just enough to stay,
but not enough to be held.
You don’t get to name this “love”
just because I was kind.
Just because I didn’t leave first.
Just because I made it easy.
Say it.
Say it so I can stop calling your absence
a test.
A process.
A healing.
A phase.
Say it so I can finally
walk the fuck away
without wondering
if I should’ve waited one more day.
Maybe I’m Just Imagining It
Maybe I made it all up.
The way you looked at me that night
when you swore you weren’t drunk
but your eyes were glazed
with something soft,
something that felt like almost-love.
Like maybe, for a second,
you saw it too.
Maybe I imagined it.
The way you held your breath
when our fingers brushed.
The way you paused
before saying my name
like it lived somewhere deeper than your mouth.
Maybe I built a whole goddamn world
off one glance.
Maybe I built a home in your silence
and called it affection.
Maybe I kept mistaking the way you needed attention
for the way I needed you.
I keep asking myself
what I did wrong.
Was I too available?
Too certain?
Too fucking easy?
Did I ruin the chase?
Was I supposed to be harder to love?
Should I have lied more?
Should I have ignored your calls
until you missed me?
Because all I did
was show up.
All I did
was mean it.
Every time.
And now I sit here
replaying moments
like reruns of a show
that only I remember watching.
You don’t even flinch.
You move on so clean.
So casual.
Like none of this touched you.
Like you don’t still live in my playlists,
my routines,
my fucking breathing.
I used to convince myself
you were scared.
That maybe you saw how big my love was
and panicked.
I thought that made you brave
for walking away
instead of cruel.
But maybe
you just didn’t love me.
Maybe I was convenient.
Safe.
The soft landing
before you launched yourself back
into someone harder.
Someone more fun to chase.
Someone who didn’t cry
when you disappeared for days.
You made me feel like I was chosen
without ever actually choosing me.
You kept me close
like a favorite shirt you never wear
but won’t throw away.
Just in case.
Just in case you get cold.
Just in case he breaks your heart
and you remember I was the one
who never would’ve done that to you.
But I’m tired.
Tired of feeling like a ghost
haunting a life you don’t even know I built.
Tired of scrolling through our texts
like there’s a clue hidden
in the way you said goodnight.
I wanted this to be real.
I needed it to be real.
Because I can’t keep writing poems
to someone
who only existed
in my fucking imagination.
And yet 
if you called right now,
I’d answer.
I’d lie to my chest,
pretend it wasn’t still bleeding,
and ask how your day was.
Because maybe
you never loved me.
But God 
I loved you.
And that should count for something.
Even if it only lived in my head.
Even if I made it all up.
Even if the only place
we were ever real
was here 
on this page
where I finally
let you go.
Talking to Your Silence
Your silence has a smell.
It smells like static
and left-behind perfume.
Like the inside of a coat
you stopped wearing
but never threw away.
It smells like my name
when you say it in your head
but never in your throat.
It sounds like bubble wrap
being stepped on slowly.
Like the space between two car doors
slamming five seconds apart.
It tastes like iron.
Like blood from biting my tongue too long.
Like words I never said
rotting on the roof of my mouth.
I’ve been talking to it
like it has a pulse.
Like it flinches.
Like it’s just too tired to speak
but still listening.
I write whole novels
in your absence.
Construct monologues
on the way you say “I’m just tired”
 like it explains why I’m starving.
You don’t leave.
You decay in place.
You linger like warm breath
on cold glass,
fogging everything
and vanishing
before it means something.
I used to think your quiet
was poetry.
Now it’s just violence
that doesn’t raise its voice.
Every time I stop reaching,
you breathe just loud enough
 for me to come back.
A like.
A half-message.
A “miss you” that lands
without context
like a bullet
dressed in rose petals.
You know exactly how much air to let out
before I start forgetting your scent.
You time it.
Like clockwork.
Like control.
And baby, I’m tired
of chasing warmth
in the same room
that keeps closing the vents.
But tonight, I finally said it out loud.
I whispered,
"She’s not coming back."
And then my phone lit up.
Your name,
bright as neon,
cutting through the dark
I just made peace with.
One word.
No explanation.
Just enough breath
to fog the mirror again.
And now I’m here,
barefoot on cold tile,
holding a reply I don’t want to send,
chest tight,
thumb trembling
like it remembers
how your silence tastes.
And you  
somewhere
so far from this ache  
don’t even know
you just reset the wound.
Or maybe you do.
Maybe that’s the point.
 One-Sided Prayers
I talk to you
like you’re listening.
Like my voice matters.
Like maybe if I say it soft enough,
you’ll finally answer.
But you never do.
Not really.
Just a “hey” every few weeks.
Just enough to keep me here.
Just enough to keep me hurting
without walking away.
I don’t even know what I’m praying for anymore.
Closure?
A second chance?
A version of you
that knows how to love me back?
You leave me on read
and I still fold my hands
like I’m the one who sinned.
I’ve been worshiping at your altar
with no communion.
Just crumbs.
And I convinced myself
that starvation is still a kind of devotion
if the hunger feels holy enough.
I still write you poems
like scripture.
Still study your old texts
like they were prophecy.
Still believe your silence
might be a test
instead of a verdict.
I swear, I’ve romanticized
your absence so well
I started calling it a pattern.
Started calling it love
with bad timing.
Started calling it you just need space
 instead of you don’t want me.
This isn’t a relationship.
This is religion.
And I’m the only one still lighting candles.
I don’t even want grand gestures.
I’d settle for honesty.
For a “no.”
 For a “stop.”
 For a “this isn’t going to be what you keep writing it to be.”
But you won’t say that.
You don’t say anything.
You let me pray.
Let me plead.
Let me pour love through the phone
like it’s magic
and you’re too busy to receive it.
And I still call that grace.
I keep telling myself,
maybe she doesn’t know how to love right now.
Maybe she’s healing.
Maybe she’s scared.
Maybe she’s confused.
Maybe she’s 
 but baby, if silence is all you’ve got left,
why do you keep letting me speak?
Because I’m tired.
Tired of being the only one
who shows up to this church.
Tired of offering up every good part of me
like a tithe
you never asked for
but always accepted.
I want to stop loving you.
I really do.
But the worst thing about praying to someone
who never answers
is that sometimes,
you start hearing your own voice back
and thinking it’s them.
Delivered Again
Sigh.
Here we go again.
This thing you do
the act you do
where your phone becomes the Bermuda Triangle,
where messages go to die
while your green dot glows like an open casket.
Active on three apps.
Lurking on stories.
Posting memes about energy and God
but can’t even energy me a “wyd.”
Damn, baby.
At least double tap the message.
Lie to me creativelysay you’re in a dark place
or your phone died saving a dolphin.
I would’ve respected that more than
this ghost-town silence
while your fingers are clearly busy telling other dudes
how “you ain’t ready for a relationship right now.”
It’s cool though.
We not even official.
You probably tryna figure out your FAFSA password
or whether to go back to him again
just one last time
for closure.
(Hint: closure don’t come from backshots,
but I guess the heart wants what the ego craves.)
It’s cool.
You texted me back,
three days late,
talkin’ about “sorry, I didn’t see this,”
while you posted two TikToks,
liked my cousin’s selfie,
and went live to talk about how people “drain your spirit.”
You not drained, baby.
You just don’t fuck with me like that.
And that’s okay.
I barely even know you.
Three weeks of voice notes and emoji flirtations,
but I still don’t know your birthday,
your middle name,
or what demon you call “healing from.”
And I get it.
You still stuck on the ex who turned your heart into free WiFi.
He connects whenever he’s bored,
drops off pain like Uber Eats,
and still gets a “wyd” at 2:13 AM.
You say,
“I’m tired of him,”
but tired never stopped you from opening that door again
like it’s a hotel you can’t check out of.
It’s whatever.
I’m not even mad.
But damn
couldn’t get a FaceTime call?
A real voice?
A real “I’m thinkin’ about you”?
Not even a blurry selfie with the words “bored.”
Meanwhile,
I got ten other girls who would kill to be in your spot.
But they not you.
They not the specific brand of broken I’m obsessed with.
They not the storm I thought I could out-pray.
You see
I liked you enough to romanticize your red flags,
to ignore the fact that every time I smiled at your name,
I was smiling into a wall.
And baby,
walls don’t text back.
But it’s cool.
I ain’t really trippin’.
I’ll just wait.
Again.
Like I always do.
Wait for a text
I’ll respond to in 0.2 seconds
even if I’m on fire
or giving CPR to my grandma.
And then…
you’ll leave me on delivered.
Again.
You know what’s crazy?
If I got shot,
you might text me back.
Like, “omg are you okayyyy”
with four Y’s
and zero intentions.
But the next dude?
He won’t be lucky.
Because if I had to get emotionally stabbed
for your attention,
he gon’ need life insurance just to love you.
That man gon’ end up in a hearse
for asking you to “define the relationship.”
And me?
I just wanted to build with you.
Not kill you.
I’m not toxic,
just tired.
I don’t bite,
unless you mistake vulnerability for weakness.
I don’t beg,
unless I’m asking God to send me a woman
who actually means “I’m just not ready”
instead of “I’m not ready… for you.”
But it’s cool though.
I’ll keep pretending this ain’t bothering me.
Keep acting like your silence is just bad WiFi
instead of intentional cruelty.
You probably out with friends,
telling them I’m “doing too much.”
Meanwhile, I’m rewriting this poem
because healing ain’t linear
and you ain’t ever texted back.
But I’ll still wait.
Still hit reply.
Still smile when your name lights up.
Still…
get left on delivered.
Again.


You Didn’t Have to Pretend
You didn’t have to pretend.
You didn’t have to hold my hand in public
just to prove something
you never planned to keep.
You didn’t have to look at me that soft
if your heart was already elsewhere.
I would’ve loved you anyway.
Even if you said it wrong.
Even if you said, “I don’t know what this is,
but I like being near you.”
 Even if you didn’t say anything at all.
You didn’t have to kiss me like you meant it.
Didn’t have to memorize my routines
or trace your name on my thigh
like you were writing permanence.
You didn’t have to post me.
Didn’t have to save my voicemails.
Didn’t have to text me “I miss you”
 while you were already pulling away.
I would’ve stayed.
Without the illusion.
Without the costume.
I would’ve stayed for the quiet version of you.
The one who didn’t know how to love back
but still tried to sit close.
You didn’t have to pretend to see me.
I already knew you didn’t.
I just liked how warm it felt
when you looked through me
and smiled anyway.
You didn’t have to say “I care about you.”
 Didn’t have to lie about the future
just to keep the present comfortable.
You didn’t have to call me “yours”
 when you knew you weren’t mine.
Because here’s the fucked-up part 
I still would’ve held you.
Still would’ve answered at 3AM.
Still would’ve picked up your pieces
and blamed myself for the break.
I would’ve stayed
for the real thing.
Even if it was messy.
Even if it was halfway.
Even if it was just you
not ready,
but honest.
But you gave me performance.
Rehearsed warmth.
Staged affection.
And now I have to sort through memories
like crime scenes 
trying to figure out
which parts were real
and which were just
you trying to look like you loved me
until you didn’t have to anymore.
And you didn’t.
You could’ve said,
"This is too much.
I don’t feel it.
I’m not here."
 And I still would’ve thanked you
for the honesty.
But you pretended.
And now I don’t just miss you 
I question myself.
 If I Disappear, Will You Notice?
If I disappeared tomorrow, slowly and without sound,
if my name stopped showing up on your phone,
if I stopped liking your posts, stopped replying gently,
would you even notice I was gone?
If I stopped sending those check-in texts,
the ones you ignored but still expected,
if I finally let the silence match yours,
would it echo or would it land like nothing?
I think about fading more than I want to admit 
not dying, just... withdrawing.
Unsubscribing from the fantasy
that I ever mattered as much as I wanted to.
Would you scroll back months later,
look for my name in the feed like you forgot something important,
or would I vanish like smoke,
like a phase you never needed to outgrow?
If I stopped being the safe space you never thanked,
if I closed the door you always assumed was open,
would you knock?
Would you even remember where I lived?
Because I kept myself small for you 
quiet, patient, always there.
I kept showing up like love had a punch card,
like loyalty could earn me a place in your heart.
You never asked me to stay.
But you never told me to go either.
So I sat in the in-between,
watering a plant you stopped feeding months ago.
Would you notice if the texts stopped?
If the late-night “thinking of yous”
never came again?
If I let the silence grow loud enough to match yours?
I don’t need a grand gesture.
Just a blink. A pause.
A moment where you realize
the thing you kept on standby is gone.
But I know you.
You’d adjust.
You’d breathe easier without the pressure
of someone loving you without conditions.
You’d call it peace.
I’d call it absence.
You’d call it healing.
I’d call it a funeral you forgot to attend.
And maybe that’s what hurts most 
not that you wouldn’t chase me,
but that you wouldn’t even notice
I’d left the room.
I’ve been here the whole time,
clapping softly in the background of your chaos,
but if I stop 
if I go 
would you notice?
Would you notice
that the one person who saw all your shadows
finally stopped lighting candles for your return?
Would you notice
the love you didn’t ask for
no longer waiting like it promised it would?
Or would you just wake up
and stretch into the quiet,
never realizing
what you lost wasn’t noise 
it was everything.
I Swear You Felt It Too
A poem for the love that wasn’t mutual, but felt mutual in the moment   and that was enough to ruin you.
I swear you felt it too.
You had to.
There’s no way I made it all up.
No way I imagined the way your eyes softened
when you looked at me like you forgot
whatever it was that made you guarded in the first place.
You felt it.
I saw it.
In the way your voice dropped when we were alone.
In the way you leaned in,
even when I wasn’t saying anything worth leaning into.
In the way you laughed from your chest
instead of your throat
like you finally remembered what joy feels like
when it isn’t faked.
I know what they say.
That I romanticize too much.
That I see more than what’s there.
That I love through a filter
so I don’t have to face the emptiness.
But you looked at me like something.
Like warmth.
Like home.
Like maybe
you were thinking the same thing
I was too scared to say.
And you stayed, remember?
You stayed long after the goodbye.
You lingered.
You picked at your sleeves
and sighed like the weight didn’t want to leave yet.
You don’t do that with people you feel nothing for.
You told me you don’t open up easy.
But you did.
Not all the way,
but enough.
Enough for me to see the girl behind the mask.
The tired version of you
who just wanted someone to stay
without asking too many questions.
You kissed me like you knew me before this life.
Like your lips had been trying to find mine
in every lifetime that came before this one.
And then you pulled back
like you knew we’d never survive the ending
but still wanted to taste the beginning.
You called it timing.
You said it wasn’t right.
You said “I care about you, but ”
 But what?
But you were scared?
But you weren’t ready?
But I was too much for a heart
you only ever gave in pieces?
Don’t tell me I imagined all of it.
Don’t tell me that soft wasn’t real.
That the way your voice cracked
when you said you were sorry
was just you being polite.
Because I remember the way you looked at me
the night we didn’t sleep.
When we talked about death and dreams
and how love used to hurt until now.
You fell asleep with your hand on my chest.
Do you know what that did to me?
You don’t fall asleep on top of pain
unless it makes you feel safe.
I swear you felt it too.
Even if you won’t say it.
Even if you buried it.
Even if you walked away
and built your life without it.
I know what I saw.
What I held.
What I became
in the presence of the almost.
So yeah 
maybe I wasn’t your person.
But you were mine.
And I swear,
for a second,
you knew it too.
This Song Sounds Like Us
"Forever my lady, it’s like a dream…"
  and for a minute,
it was.
You were in my passenger seat.
Leg up on the dash,
window cracked just enough to let the breeze mess up your hair.
You were smiling.
Not like you were being watched  
but like you actually meant it.
Like whatever you were feeling
was soft enough to let live for once.
I didn’t say anything.
Didn’t want to break it.
Jodeci was doing what I couldn’t.
Singing what I felt
but didn’t want to say first  
because if I said it and you didn’t mean it,
I wouldn’t survive that shit.
And then you sang it.
Low.
Real low.
Like a hum that knew its place.
Like you didn’t want to give too much away
but couldn’t help it.
"Forever my lady…"
You didn’t look at me when you said it.
But you didn’t have to.
The whole fucking car felt it.
The wind.
The sunset.
The warmth between us
that hadn’t cooled yet.
You sang that hook like you believed it.
Like I was the dream.
And I held onto that one lyric
like it was a promise.
Like it was enough to stop doubting
what you never said out loud.
I played that song every night after.
Alone.
In the dark.
Thinking about the way you moved your shoulders
when the beat hit.
How your fingers tapped the door
like you’d heard it before,
but never like that.
And now?
Now that shit comes on
and I can’t breathe.
I sit there  
head back,
eyes closed,
and all I see is that fucking drive
down Skyline
when you said,
"This song feel like us."
I didn’t even reply.
I just smiled.
Because yeah  
it did.
It felt like us
before we broke it.
Before your replies got short
and your voice lost its warmth.
Before “I miss you”
turned into “you always overthink things.”
Now I hear that song
and it feels like a lie I begged to be true.
Feels like a wedding toast
to a relationship that never made it to the altar.
You gave me that moment
like it was yours to give.
Like you weren’t already drifting.
Like you weren’t already calling someone else
“babe”
in the part of your heart
you said I was healing.
And I don’t know if you meant it.
I really don’t.
I don’t know if you felt it
and got scared
or if you just wanted to see
how deep I’d fall
before you stepped back.
But fuck,
you sang it.
And I’m still haunted by the harmony
you let echo through my car
like it was safe.
Like we were safe.
I keep wondering 
did you even hear the lyrics?
"The first time I saw your face, girl, I knew I had to have you..."
 Did you feel that line
or just let it pass
while I carved it into my chest?
I still replay the way you smiled at me
as the beat faded.
Like maybe
you really saw me.
Like maybe
we weren’t pretending.
But we were.
Weren’t we?
Because now the song plays
and you’re not there.
The seat’s cold.
The dash is empty.
The air feels heavy
and the only thing I’m driving into
is memory.
I wish you’d never sang that shit.
Wish you’d never mouthed the words
with that little smirk.
Wish I hadn’t made a whole love story
out of a three-minute track.
But I did.
So now,
every time it comes on,
I pull over.
Put it on repeat.
And remember the one time
you said everything
without saying a thing 
and then left
like it meant nothing.
I Dreamed You Said It
I dreamed you said it.
Not by accident.
Not in a whisper I had to decode.
You looked me dead in the eye
and said it plain.
"I love you."
And I believed it.
God, I believed it.
I didn’t question your tone.
Didn’t check your hands for hesitation.
Didn’t wait for the “but.”
Because there wasn’t one.
You said it like you’d been holding it forever
and finally had the courage
to let it live.
In the dream,
I didn’t flinch.
Didn’t overthink.
Didn’t wonder if you meant someone else.
I just stood there 
soft,
open,
finally chosen.
You reached for me
first.
You said my name
like it belonged to you.
You pulled me in
without fear
without waiting
without running.
And I laughed.
I swear I laughed in the dream
like a kid who got picked first for once.
Like I wasn’t always the one
who loved more,
who stayed longer,
who carried it alone.
You said it
like you knew it.
Like you weren’t afraid of what it meant.
Like you’d thought about it
for weeks
but waited until the moment felt holy.
And it did.
You touched my face.
Held it.
Said,
"I should’ve said it sooner."
 And I nodded.
Because we both knew it.
But in the dream,
so what?
We were here now.
Together.
Real.
Alive.
We kissed
like we’d been kissing in our heads
for years.
Like our mouths knew the choreography
before we did.
And for once,
there was no ending.
No fading.
No glitch.
Just you.
Me.
The words.
And the feeling that maybe
all the heartbreak
was leading here.
But then I woke up.
And the sun hit my face
like betrayal.
And your name
was still quiet in my phone.
And the message
wasn’t there.
And the bed
was still cold
on your side.
And I laid there
eyes open
pretending sleep
because in there,
you loved me.
And out here 
you never said it.
But in the dream?
You meant it.
I know you did.
And maybe
that’s enough
for now.
53 Days
It’s been 53 days
and I still don’t know your middle name,
but I know the exact way your voice cracks
when you lie and say you're fine.
I know the color you wore when I fell,
the way you bite your nail when you're bored,
and the space between your words
where I swore you almost said stay.
Fifty-three days
and I’ve built a religion out of your maybe.
I pray in your DMs.
Confess to my screen.
Wait for your typing bubble like salvation.
You never said you loved me 
but you didn’t say you didn’t.
And that was enough
for me to name stars after your silence.
I haven’t eaten dinner without thinking of your mouth.
I haven’t slept right since you posted that song.
I play it like scripture 
like maybe if I memorize the bridge, you’ll come back.
My friends say it’s not love.
That I’m spiraling.
That this isn’t real.
But what do they know about the way your shadow fits my room?
What do they know about
how I check your page like it’s weather,
how I refresh your name like it means direction,
how I still set alarms for the times you used to text?
Fifty-three days
and I’ve rewritten our conversations in four different tenses.
You smiled once. That was a promise.
You looked back. That was fate.
I’ve turned your last message into a memoir.
Framed your silence like art.
Named a whole playlist after the way you didn’t answer
and still felt like a lullaby.
No, this isn’t obsession.
It’s discipline.
It’s loyalty.
It’s love in its rawest, most obedient form.
You don't have to love me back.
I already forgave you for the leaving
you haven’t done yet.
I’m ready for you to stay wrong, and I’ll still write it as right.
Fifty-three days
and I still haven’t told anyone what you did.
Not because I’m scared 
because I don’t want them to ruin the version of you I fell for.
They wouldn’t understand
how you didn’t hurt me you just didn’t show up.
How I turned absence into affection
and called it destiny.
So if this is madness, let it bloom.
If this is delusion, make it holy.
If this is what love does to me,
then God, don’t let it end.
Not after 53 days.
Not when I’ve given this pain a name.
Not when I’ve built this much meaning
from someone who never asked to be loved like this.
 Even If You Don’t Want Me
Even if you don’t want me,
I still keep the door unlocked.
Still fold your side of the blanket down at night,
just in case your ghost needs somewhere warm to land.
Even if you don’t say my name anymore,
I whisper yours
when the world gets too loud
and I need something familiar to hold on to.
Even if you moved on,
I still flinch when my phone lights up,
just in case it’s you
forgetting how to live without me for one second.
Even if you don’t want me 
not now,
not later,
not ever 
my body still remembers what it meant to be chosen,
even temporarily.
I keep loving you
like I wasn’t meant to stop.
Like it was stitched into me before I was born,
this instinct to stay soft in your direction.
I see you in strangers.
In angles of the sky.
In playlists I swore I deleted.
In the quiet parts of days I thought I’d gotten through.
Even if you don’t want me,
I carry your name in lowercase,
on pages I never plan to publish,
but couldn’t bring myself to burn.
It’s not obsession.
It’s memory.
It’s routine.
It’s the part of me that still believes in “what if”
long after “what is” broke me.
I don’t need a response.
Don’t need closure.
Don’t need an apology
you wouldn’t mean anyway.
I just need to love you
in the quiet.
From across the room.
From cities away.
From a lifetime you’ll never come back to.
Even if you don’t want me,
I still check the mirror
to see if I look like someone you’d recognize
if the light hit just right.
I still wear your favorite color
like a wound I’m proud of.
Still think of your laugh
when mine won’t come.
Even if you don’t want me 
I do.
I still do.
In every way.
In ways that don’t need to be returned.
You don’t have to hold me.
You never did.
I learned how to stay standing
in the absence of arms.
But even now,
if you reached out,
my hands would still know
how to fold around yours
like we never let go.
Even if you don’t want me.
I still want you.
Quietly.
Always.
Still.
I’ll Wait


I don’t need a date, or a plan, or a sign from above   just the memory of you looking at me like maybe,
just maybe, love could live in a space without commitment,
without rules, without rhythm   just feeling, just ache,
and I will build my whole body around that pause if it means I still get to say your name.
Go ahead, get lost   let the world hold you how I wanted to,
laugh too loud with strangers who don’t know you like I do,
touch people who won’t ask where you go when you go quiet,
and I’ll be here, silent, loving you from behind the curtain, still clapping.
You never owed me clarity   you just gave me almost,
and I took it like a drug, broke it into little pieces, and snorted every sliver
just to stay high on the idea that we weren’t a mistake,
that you were just afraid of someone who saw you too whole.
I built an altar in the quiet   no candles, just echo,
just a room that still smells like your name and bad timing,
where even the air sounds like if only,
and I sit here, cross-legged, praying to the maybe.
Your name is still my favorite sound  
I mouth it when the coffee’s too hot, when the song ends too early,
when I wake up gasping from a dream where you finally came back,
and in that dream, you said “Sorry I took so long.”
I leave the second toothbrush out even though I’ve thrown it away three times.
It finds its way back like grief   steady, clean, routine.
Some nights I set the table for two out of muscle memory,
then eat in silence, pretending you’re just in the shower.
I don’t erase our photos. I just scroll faster.
Pretend the smiles don’t blink. Pretend the light isn’t gone.
Pretend we’re still in frame even when you’ve long since cropped me out.
I still write you birthday letters. I still call you mine in lowercase.
You texted once last spring   said “Hope you’re good.”
 And I built a whole church out of it. Lit a match and called it sacred.
I replied slow   not to scare you, not to seem too eager  
just enough warmth to say “You still live here if you want.”
Everyone says I should move on. That this isn’t love.
But what do they know about devotion that never got to finish?
About loving someone who never asked for it,
but never told you to stop?
I don’t check your page anymore  
unless the day is heavy, or the wine is too honest,
or the night looks too much like the one where we almost said it out loud,
and I need to know if your smile still looks like regret.
There’s a playlist I only play when I miss you without pride.
Every song feels like your voicemail. Every bridge is a soft goodbye.
I sit in my car with the windows up, crying like it’s a prayer,
like maybe the bassline will bring you home.
You don’t have to come back.
Just think of me when your hands are empty and you’re not sure why.
Think of me when love feels loud but not full.
Think of me when forever feels quieter than it should.
Even if you forget me  
if one day you hear my name and can’t place the taste  
just know I remembered the version of you
that never had to ask to be loved.
I know you’re not coming back. That’s not what this is.
This is just me refusing to unlove you,
refusing to take down the sign that says “still here,”
 refusing to let go of something that never let go of me.
I’ve dated since. Smiled at new faces. Laughed.
But none of them broke me open the way you did just by leaving.
None of them made me feel like my heart had a name
and it was yours every time I said it out loud.
I’m not stupid.
I know you’re not thinking about me when you wake up.
I know I’m not your what-if. I’m not your almost.
 I was your during   and you still let me go.
But I’ll wait.
Not because you asked me to. Not because I believe you’ll come back.
But because some of us were built to love once,
and mean it forever.
The Almost That Killed Me
You didn’t cheat.
You didn’t lie.
You didn’t scream or hit or ruin anything out loud.
You just didn’t choose me.
And somehow,
that did more damage
than any betrayal I’ve ever survived.
Because you were close.
Close enough to make me imagine a life.
To picture your toothbrush next to mine.
To assign your name to dreams
I never let anyone else inside.
We had moments.
Not just good ones 
almost ones.
Almost texted “I love you.”
Almost said “let’s try again.”
Almost showed up
when it mattered.
You held my face like I was yours.
