Then walked away like I wasn’t.
And that split in the middle 
that almost 
that’s what broke me.
Not because I didn’t have you.
But because for a minute,
I really thought I did.
You gave me enough
to believe in the ending
without ever writing the middle.
I built us a home in my head.
I painted walls with your laugh.
Filled rooms with your moods.
And you never even came inside.
I don’t hate you.
That would be easier.
I don’t blame you.
Not fully.
I just...
ache.
All the time.
For something that was never real
outside of my chest.
You’re not my heartbreak.
You’re my haunt.
Because you didn’t leave me shattered 
you left me waiting.
Waiting for a version of you
that never showed up
but kept texting “I miss you.”
You didn’t break my heart.
You just never held it.
You just stood close enough
for me to hand it over
and far enough
to let it hit the ground.
And I’d rather be broken.
Fully.
Loudly.
With a clean cut.
But this?
This was the almost
that killed me.
FUCK YOU BITCH
Fuck you, bitch.
I hate the way you breathe.
Like nothing happened.
Like I ain’t died a thousand small deaths
just trying to get you to look me in the fucking eye.
I hate the way you switch up.
You was mine on Monday,
brand new on Thursday,
a stranger by the weekend.
You change like your conscience got a snooze button
and I was the dream you forgot to wake up from.
I hate your fucking mother for birthing you.
For pushing you out into this world
without teaching you how to love
something that loved you back.
You should’ve never made it out the womb
if you was just gon’ walk through hearts
like unlocked doors.
I hope they switch you down.
String you up.
Leave holes in your fucking body
like the ones you left in my soul.
Swiss cheese through the ribs.
Open like my chest was
every time you came back just to leave again.
I hope they line you up
in the same hallway you left me in.
Make you feel the same goddamn waiting
I felt
when you said “I’m just confused”
 while still letting me touch you
like that meant something.
I hate your face.
I hate your smile.
I hate that it worked on me.
I hate that I still think about it.
I hate that it still lives on my favorite hoodie
and I can’t even wear the shit without wanting to puke.
You didn’t break my heart.
You fucking pissed on it.
Stomped it out.
Lit it up
then danced in the smoke like it was holy.
And I watched.
Like a bitch.
Let you ruin me soft.
Called it “growth.”
Wrote you sonnets while you sent dry-ass “oki.”
Replied like you were gold
when you was dirt in designer.
I hate the way you blinked
when you lied to me.
Like I couldn’t see your pupils dilate
every time you said you “needed time.”
Bitch, you needed dick.
You needed validation.
You needed someone to suffer for your softness
without ever earning it.
You fed me air.
Told me it was dinner.
And I said thank you
with tears in my eyes
and hunger in my ribs.
I hate the way I let you.
I hate that I smiled through it.
I hate that I made your pain
a priority
while mine had to wait in line
behind your mood swings.
You don’t deserve closure.
You don’t deserve forgiveness.
You don’t deserve another fucking line.
But here I am,
writing this with your name bleeding out of my teeth
because you got my pain
in a chokehold
and I’m still gasping
like maybe this’ll make it stop.
I hope when you die,
they don’t find you pretty.
I hope your funeral is empty.
I hope your next life is filled with people
who love you like you loved me
late.
Wrong.
And only when it hurts.
And if I ever see you again,
I hope I don’t flinch.
I hope I don’t remember the soft.
I hope I don’t forgive shit.
I hope I look you dead in the eyes
and smile like I ain’t ever met you.
And I hope it kills you.
You Played Me
You played me.
Like a fucking app.
Downloaded my kindness
and deleted me
once you got the dopamine.
You played me like I ain’t read between the lines.
Like I ain’t hear the switch in your voice
when he texted back.
Like I ain’t see your heart clock out
when I started asking real questions.
You thought I didn’t notice?
I noticed everything.
The delay in your “I miss you.”
The sigh when I called.
The way you stopped saying my name
like it was yours too.
You thought I was soft.
You were right.
But softness don’t mean stupid.
I saw the way you kept the lie clean.
You smiled in 4K,
kissed me like an exit,
and told me it was healing.
You said you needed time.
Nah.
You needed space to fuck around
without feeling guilty.
You made me into a dummy
just so you could sleep at night.
Played the game like I wasn’t watching.
Like I wasn’t clocking every pattern.
Every fake “goodnight.”
Every time you forgot the version of me
you told your friends you loved.
You weaponized my patience.
Used my loyalty like it came with a mute button.
Tested how far I’d stretch
before snapping.
And bitch, I almost snapped.
But now?
Now I know.
You never loved me.
You loved being loved.
You loved how I poured into you
without ever asking where the cup came from.
You loved how I believed your stories
even when the plot was see-through.
You thought I was in love.
I was.
But I was also tired.
Tired of playing therapist
while you auditioned for chaos.
You played me.
And I let you.
Because part of me wanted to believe
you weren’t like the rest.
Turns out you worse.
They stabbed me and ran.
You stabbed me and stayed
just long enough to watch the bleeding.
You watched me rot
and called it romance.
Watched me beg
and called it devotion.
I hope it was worth it.
I hope whatever high you got
off watching me collapse
keeps you warm when you’re 43
and alone in a condo with a fake plant
and a therapist who calls your bluff.
Because I remember everything.
The way you blinked when you lied.
The way you kissed with one foot out the door.
The way you looked at me
like I was both your safety
and your fucking inconvenience.
And now?
Now I look at myself
like I finally got out of prison.
You played me.
Cool.
But the best part?
You lost the one person
who would’ve taken a bullet
and never asked who pulled the trigger.
Time I’ll Never Get Back
All those nights I spent waiting by the phone,
ignoring calls, missing practices, always alone,
canceling plans, telling my friends “maybe next time,”
just to watch you waste more of what was mine.
I missed games, skipped drills, fell behind for you,
paused my dreams, put my whole grind aside for you,
ignored advice, warnings from teammates and coaches,
while you toyed with my patience like pests with roaches.
You weren’t worth a single rep that I lost,
not a single practice session did you offset the cost,
every bench I warmed because I skipped that day,
I blame myself but I blame you too, in every way.
You watched me slip behind while you played dumb,
promising love that would never come,
I missed my moment, lost my position,
traded my ambition for your indecision.
You never respected my dreams, my drive,
saw me drowning and still kept the lie alive,
late-night texts, empty promises, dragging me down,
and I let you, lost in your bullshit, letting myself drown.
I’m backtracking now, catching up to what I missed,
thinking of every game, every practice dismissed,
every rep, every hour, every drop of my sweat,
and the respect I lost in myself, biggest regret.
I wish I could rewind time, erase your name,
train twice as hard, undo all this shame,
tell my younger self not to answer your call,
and remind him love that’s real won’t ask him to fall.
You were never worth the effort, the sacrifices made,
you were never the truth, just lies perfectly played,
you weren’t special, just another lesson learned hard,
another scar, another trust forever left marred.
You stole time I’ll never regain wasted potential,
cost me my spot, cost me credentials,
and I swear to God, if you text me again,
I’ll leave you unread won’t fall for pretend.
So fuck you, your excuses, your wasted affection,
fuck your distraction, your careless deception,
you’re the bench player in a life where I’m starting,
I’m leaving your lies behind this is me departing.
My time, my grind, my sweat they’re sacred,
no longer giving to someone complacent,
I’m back on the court, back on the climb,
fuck you, bitch you were never worth my time.
You Wasn’t Even My Baddest Bitch
You wasn’t even my baddest bitch,
but I acted like you were it.
Told the world you were a dream,
but deep down I knew that shit was a skit.
I said I’d marry you. Lied.
Told you I wanted kids. Lied again.

when forever was never even in my plan.
You was never the one I pictured in white,
never the name I’d want my child to say.
But I said it anyway, said all that sweet shit,
just to keep your ass from walking away.
Truth is, I settled. That’s on me.
Went backwards when I knew better.
Held you like royalty in public,
when you barely passed the loyalty check ever.
Why the fuck am I crying over you?
I’ve been with better, laughed harder, felt realer.
But something about you had me in a chokehold,
maybe ‘cause you needed saving, and I’m a healer.
You had nothing I ain’t seen done right before,
and everything I should’ve walked away from.
Still, I let you post me, claim me, waste me
treated you like a ten when you wasn’t even one.
Your looks ain’t the reason I stayed,

thinking I was built to hold what others spotted.
I gave you poems, peace, protection, pride
you gave me confusion and half-assed replies.

You wasn’t even good in bed like that,

You didn’t move me, not even close,
but I hyped you like you were a queen.
And what’s worse is I meant the lies

Lied with love, like maybe if I said it enough
you’d become the person I needed you to be.
You ain’t cook. You ain’t clean.
You ain’t call just to check how I’m feeling.
You thought existing was enough to be loved,
and I played into it, made it appealing.
You wasn’t even funny, girl,
but I laughed ‘cause I liked your smile.
Watched you butcher my favorite jokes,
and still let you think you had style.
I introduced you to people I respected
and pretended you were more than you are.
Watched you talk like you had substance,
and kept quiet while you lowered the bar.
I passed on real ones to keep your drama.
Ignored women with hearts and peace.
Chose you and chaos over kindness,
now I sit here grieving my own decrease.
You ain’t love me. You loved control.
Loved what I gave, but not who I was.
You liked being adored and lifted up,
but never showed up, not even just because.
And you got the nerve to act like I’m pressed,
like I’m lucky I ever got to hold you close.
But let’s be realyou got your status from me,
without me you’d still be unknown, at most.
You never prayed for me once.
Never pushed me to be more than I am.
I gave vision, you gave excuses,
I gave heart, you gave scam.
I lied when I said I could see forever,
lied harder when I pictured the ring.
Now I’m just sitting here, pissed and poetic,
grieving over someone who ain't worth a thing.
My mama ain’t even like you,
and she sees through people the first day.
She told me, “Son, this ain’t your match,”
and I said, “Ma, love don’t work that way.”
But she was right. They all were.
I just couldn’t admit I chose wrong.
I wanted to prove I could make it work,
now I’m just here writing breakup songs.
You ain’t never held me when I was low.
You watched me drown and turned away.
Called me dramatic for feeling too deep,
then cried wolf when I gave you space.
You wasn’t even my baddest bitch
you just showed up when I was tired.
And I mistook your presence for purpose,
let my loneliness call you fire.
But nah, you wasn’t fire.
You were smoke and noise and shade.
A spark I chased in the dark
‘cause I was too scared to see what I made.
I made you the main character in a role you ain’t earn,
gave you flowers while I burned.
Gave you chapters in a book that was mine,
while you barely read the lines in return.
You wasn’t even the best I’ve had,
and that’s the sickest part of this whole thing.
You were just loud, and there, and pretty enough
for me to ignore what real love could bring.
I’ve had women who saw me clear,
who held me down without the mess.
I traded queens for a crown made of cardboard,
and now I’m left with this stress.
But I’m done. You ain’t getting another line,
another rhyme, another sip of my shine.
I gave too much for too little in return,
and now all that’s left is time I’ll never earn.
So here's the last thing I’ll say,
before I pack this page and throw the key
you wasn’t even my baddest bitch…
you was just the one who got to me.

I could’ve done better than your half-assed affection,

But it’s clear now you were never the prize,

My mistake was thinking you were worth the chase,

So fuck you for wasting my patience and care,

 I Should’ve Let You Drown
I should’ve let you drown.
I should’ve stood there,
watching the water rise around your pride
and said nothing.
Let the waves take you.
Let the cold fill your lungs.
Let the silence
finally match the noise you buried in my chest.
But I didn’t.
I fucking dove in.
I held my breath,
gave you mine.
And you still looked me in the face
like I was the reason you couldn’t breathe.
You say you were “tired.”
That you “tried.”
You didn’t try.
You took.
 You took everything.
And when I was running on empty,
you said I was “too much.”
I should’ve known when you stopped trying to fight for anything
but your own exit.
I watched you go limp.
Stop answering.
Stop asking.
Stop showing up to the relationship
like it was already over
and you were just waiting for me to say it first
so you wouldn’t feel guilty.
Do you remember the night I stayed up ‘til 4am
talking you out of quitting your job,
your life,
yourself?
You said,
"I don’t know who I am anymore."
 I told you,
"I do. I still see her."
 I should’ve shut the fuck up
and let you drown.
Do you remember when I made breakfast
after we didn’t speak for two days
and you ate in silence
like I was a stranger in your house?
You didn’t thank me.
You didn’t say a word.
Just swallowed the love and kept walking.
You had me reading your moods
like I was learning a language
you didn’t want to teach.
Like if I could just decode the cold shoulder,
you’d come back.
But you were already gone.
You stopped planning.
Stopped saying “we.”
You said “I just need time.”
 Then took mine.
All of it.
You let me carry this relationship
like a body
we were supposed to bury together.
But you dropped your shovel
and asked me why I was sweating.
I stayed.
I stayed through the distance.
The attitude.
The sex that felt like obligation.
The late nights where your phone was face down
but lit up every ten minutes.
The passive aggression.
The one-word texts.
The dry kisses.
The long silences after I said “I love you.”
I stayed because I believed in you
more than you did.
I saw the softness under all that numb.
I thought if I just held on long enough,
you’d see it too.
But no.
You wanted comfort,
not change.
You wanted love,
but not effort.
You wanted someone to sit in the dark with you
and not ask if you were okay.
You stopped trying
and called it “healing.”
Stopped apologizing
and called it “growth.”
You gave up
and called it "I didn’t want to hurt you anymore."
Don’t fucking lie.
You didn’t stay to protect me.
You stayed long enough to make sure
I’d miss you when you left.
And now?
Now I’m the one in therapy
trying to explain
how someone can be next to you
every day
and still make you feel abandoned.
I should’ve let you drown.
I should’ve let the silence pull you under.
Let the weight of your own choices
drag you to the bottom.
Instead, I threw you my arms,
my lungs,
my body,
and you used them
to swim to someone else.
And you had the nerve
to call me controlling.
To say I “expected too much.”
I expected effort.
Honesty.
Presence.
The basics.
You wanted to be loved without responsibility.
To be worshipped
but never witnessed.
I gave you grace.
You called it weakness.
I gave you forgiveness.
You used it to reload.
I should’ve let you fucking drown.
Because now
I’m the one gasping.
Still shaking.
Still trying to unlearn the way you loved me
like I was a task.
You gave up.
On us.
On yourself.
And left me
to feel like the failure.
So I’m saying it now 
out loud 
with my full chest:
Next time?
I won’t save you.
I’ll watch you sink.
And I won’t feel bad about it.
You Made Me Soft Just to Break Me
I was never soft,
not like that.
Not until you.
Not until the way you laughed at the way I poured the coffee.
Not until your voice cracked when you said,
"I feel safest with you."
That’s what killed me.
You said shit like that
and then left me in pieces
trying to rebuild a man I only became
because I thought you saw him.
I wasn’t open.
I wasn’t tender.
I didn’t hand out my truths like candy.
But you?
You got all of it.
Every wound.
Every lesson I hadn’t healed from yet.
And I gave it willingly
because you made it feel safe.
You said I could trust you.
So I did.
And you used what I gave you
as a blueprint
for how to ruin me.
You made me soft 
held my fears like they were precious
until you got bored
and threw them back at me
mid-argument.
You studied me.
Took notes.
Memorized what made me flinch
so when it was time to leave,
you’d know exactly where to aim.
You didn’t just leave.
You unraveled me.
You walked away
with the most human parts of me still in your teeth.
And now I flinch when love looks kind.
I question compliments.
I second-guess quiet mornings
because that’s when you started pulling away.
Softness doesn’t feel safe anymore.
It feels like setup.
Like betrayal dressed in slow kisses
and “goodnight, I love you.”
You made me soft
just to break me.
Taught me how to cry
then punished me for leaking.
Told me to let go
then blamed me for falling.
I should’ve kept the armor on.
Should’ve stayed cold.
Should’ve stayed that version of me
that didn’t ask for anything,
that didn’t believe in us.
Because what the fuck did I gain?
A better way to bleed?
You had me thinking
that growth meant pain,
that love meant disappearing acts,
that softness was weakness
if it wasn’t yours first.
And you get to walk around now
telling people you “tried.”
That “it just didn’t work.”
But I know better.
I know the truth:
You made me soft
just so you could say,
"See? Even the strong ones fall apart."


 I Don’t Write About You No More
I don’t write about you no more.
Not because I’m healed 
because I’m fucking tired.
Tired of folding you into stanzas

Tired of turning your cruelty into something

Tired of making your distance

Scrolled to the bottom.

Then acted surprised

And that’s on me.

and I gave you a home.
You came with confusion
and I gave you answers.
You came with attitude
and I gave you patience.
You came with nothing.
And I made it feel like enough
so I wouldn’t feel stupid
for choosing you.
But you were never enough.
Not for me.
Not even for yourself.
And I bent backwards
just to make you feel whole
while you stood there
watching me snap.
I don’t write about you no more.
Because every poem I gave you
was a lie I told myself.
Every verse

that you were selfish.
That you were reckless.
That you hurt me,
and then blamed me for bleeding.

when it mattered.
You loved my attention,
but never my presence.
Loved being wanted,
but not being known.
Loved being in control,
but never being accountable.
And I kept writing you
like you were something worth remembering.
You're not.
You’re a bitch.
A ghost with a phone.
A habit I should’ve broken
after the third time
you said “sorry”
and didn’t mean it.
I gave you poems

when I should’ve walked the fuck away.
And now?
Now I keep that same energy.
No more open letters.

No more verses
for someone who couldn't even text back like they gave a fuck.

You don’t get to be immortal
just because I wrote you pretty.

Cool.
Stay gone.
You’ll find your name
in no new pages.
No hidden meanings.

You were never a muse.
You were a warning.
And now?
You’re irrelevant.
I don’t write about you no more.
Not because I forgot.

who the fuck I am

Was I Ever Enough?
Be honest.
Not with me.
With yourself.
Was I ever enough?
Or was I just there?
Easier than being alone.
Better than answering hard questions.
Soft enough to fall on
when your ego took a hit.
Was I enough
when I showed up
after your silence?
When I forgave you
before you even said anything?
Was I enough
when I remembered your favorite takeout,
your bad days,
your tells,
your trauma
 shit you forgot you ever said?
Was I enough
when you were tired of the game
but not ready for the truth?
Or did I just fit
until you felt strong enough
to leave?
I loved you with both hands.
No filter.
No fallback.
Just me.
Open.
Here.
Yours.
You held that love
like it was breakable.
Then you dropped it anyway.
Then you blamed gravity.
You kept me close
but never let me in.
You liked the way I looked on paper.
Liked how I made you feel.
Liked knowing someone would answer
even if you didn’t call.
But did you ever think,
even once,
that I needed something too?
That maybe being enough
shouldn’t feel like bleeding quietly
just to keep you from leaving loudly.
I adjusted everything 
my tone,
my timing,
my tenderness 
trying to be
exactly what you needed
before you even asked.
And still,
you left like I was nothing.
So just say it.
Say I was a phase.
Say I was easy.
Say I was convenient
until it got real.
But don’t lie.
Don’t pretend I was everything
if you always knew
you’d leave anyway.
Was I ever enough?
Or was I just nearby
when you needed something to hold
that didn’t ask for too much?
Was I ever enough?
Because I swore
you were everything
and now I’m standing here
wondering
if I was anything.
I Can Love Us Both
I’ve been carrying this thing
like it was mine to carry.
Both hands.
No help.
No thanks.
No weight shared.
Just me, dragging this body of a maybe
down every hallway of my life
like it wasn’t already dead.
I loved for both of us.
Don’t let the silence fool you.
There was noise in here.
I just kept it quiet
so I wouldn’t scare you off.
I made room for your absence.
Set places for it at the table.
Learned how to cook my own heart
to feed the space
you never filled.
You didn’t call.
So I made excuses.
You didn’t come.
So I said you were busy.
You didn’t try.
So I told myself I was hard to reach.
I turned your neglect into nuance.
Told myself this was a slow burn.
Told myself this kind of love
just takes time.
But no one takes this long
to care.
No one forgets you this easily
if they ever really had you.
And the sickest part is 
you didn’t have to try.
I fell so fast,
you could’ve left your hands in your pockets
and still caught me.
You never said you loved me.
But you never said you didn’t.
And I think that was the point.
You kept the door cracked
so I’d stay in the hallway,
cold and hoping.
You liked the attention.
Liked the way I wrote you
so much softer than you ever were.
Liked how I painted you
as tender
just because I needed to believe
someone like you
could love someone like me.
I kissed versions of you
you never gave me.
I held hands with your potential.
I planned a future
with your shadow.
And still 
you never said,
“This isn’t that.”
 You never said,
“Go.”
 You let me rot in the waiting room
and still asked for quiet.
You never showed up.
But I waited
like devotion was its own reward.
Like bleeding out quietly
meant I deserved the love in the next life.
But this isn’t heaven.
This is grief.
This is me,
standing in the wreckage
of a love story I wrote alone.
And you?
You’re out somewhere,
laughing.
Calling someone else by the name
I begged you to remember.
You’re out there,
breathing easy,
while I’ve been
spitting up the pieces of myself
I had to digest
just to stay lovable.
I was your rest stop.
You parked,
refueled,
and left.
And I stood there, smiling,
thinking that meant something.
But I get it now.
You never wanted me.
You just liked the way I made you feel
when you didn’t want yourself.
And me?
I called that love.
I called it patience.
I called it loyalty
and God
and maybe.
But it was just pain.
Wrapped in hope.
Swallowed whole
so I didn’t have to face the fact
that love shouldn’t
feel like begging.
So this is it.
This is the burial.
This is the last thing I’ll write
with your name pulsing
underneath the lines.
This is me finally
closing the door
you never walked through
but always kept unlocked
just in case you wanted to watch me suffer.
You don’t have to mourn.
You never built this.
But I did.
And I died in it
a thousand times
before I realized
you never cared
who was bleeding.
I Don’t Need You to Choose Me
I don’t need you to choose me.

I don’t need your love

Not fully.
Not enough.
And it’s fine.

I’ve been doing it for a while.
I’ve learned how to quiet the ache

How to sleep without dreaming of your shoulder.

Not in the way

You don’t have to love me.
You don’t have to look back.
You don’t have to feel guilty
for not showing up when I did.
I’ve let go of the version of me
who thought your attention was oxygen.
Now I breathe without it.
Just slower. Just quieter.
I don’t need you to choose me.
I just needed to say that
before I finally
meant it.
 Stay As Long As You Need
Stay as long as you need.
There’s no clock here.
No guilt.
No rules tucked beneath the pillows.
You don’t have to earn tomorrow.
I won’t ask when you’re leaving.
I won’t ask if you’re staying.
I’ll just keep setting the table
like I’m expecting you for dinner
every night.
Even if you’re gone.
Even if it’s been weeks.
Even if I’ve learned how to make your favorite meal
from memory
and muscle.
I folded a towel for you
even though you said you weren’t spending the night.
Even though you came in with your keys still between your fingers
like you didn’t know whether this house was a blessing
or a mistake.
I noticed the way you scanned the room.
Looking for exits
before your eyes ever found the warmth.
That’s okay.
I know what it means to survive in advance.
You don’t owe me soft.
You don’t owe me whole.
Just give me the version of you that made it
through the day.
Take the good blanket.
The one with the weight that feels like arms.
Take the left side of the bed
or all of it.
I sleep easy knowing your breathing fills the space.
Stay as long as you need.
No curfew.
No quiet shame when you exhale too loud.
No lectures when your phone lights up
and your face goes still.   
You don’t have to tell me who it was.
You don’t have to lie
and say it was nobody.
I’ve loved people with ghosts still in their mouths.
I don’t flinch easy.
Let your mascara stain my shirt.
I won’t wash it out.
Some stories are better worn
than erased.
And if you wake up crying,
I’ll act like I didn’t hear.
But I’ll be closer.
And the tea will already be steeping
before your feet hit the floor.
I don’t need a title.
I don’t need a label.
I just need the sound of your footsteps
still echoing down my hall
when the world forgets to be kind to you.
Stay as long as you need.
Stay until you stop apologizing for taking up space.
Stay until your body stops bracing for the blow.
You can leave when you're ready.
But not because you feel like you're too much.
Not because you think I need saving.
I don't want a project.
I want your presence.
I want your pacing heart and quiet hurt
and loud joy when it finally returns.
You can go.
Whenever.
Truly.
But if no one's ever told you before
you’re allowed to be held.
Without owing. 
So stay.
Just until the shaking stops.
 


I Was There When You Fell Apart


I was there
when you fell apart.
Not just near.
Not just watching.
There.
 Floor-sitting, late-night-texting,
hand-holding,
"Breathe, baby, I got you"
 there.
I was the one
who stayed on the phone
when your voice cracked
and you couldn’t explain why.
The one who told you
your anxiety didn’t make you hard to love.
The one who said you didn’t have to be strong
just to deserve space.
You let your mask slip with me.
Let me see the version of you
that shakes.
The version that hides from her mother,
from her friends,
from the world that calls you confident
but never sees the girl crying in the kitchen.
I was there.
Not for the highlight reel.
Not for the party version.
But for the crash.
The nights you couldn’t sleep.
The mornings you couldn’t get up.
The messages you sent at 2:41 a.m.
saying,
"I don’t think I can do this anymore."
I was the one who reminded you
you already were.
The one who made sure you ate.
The one who listened
without trying to fix.
The one who loved you
even when you didn’t love yourself.
And you?
You left.
You healed just enough
to forget how you got through it.
Just enough to romanticize someone else
who never saw the blood.
Someone who never stayed through the dark
but showed up when the light came back on.
You let me carry your grief
like it was mine.
Let me watch you fall apart
then treated me
like a phase.
Like I was just the soft place
you landed in
before you walked again.
And maybe that’s what I was.
The bandage.
Not the body.
The medicine.
Not the memory.
But God 
I was there.
Every second.
Every silence.
Every "I can’t do this"
that I turned into
“Yes, you can.”
And now you post pictures
with someone who doesn’t know
the way your breath hitches before you cry.
Someone who never saw your hands shake
while you poured coffee.
Someone who didn’t sit through
the version of you
that made me fall deeper
because I saw what others would’ve run from
and said,
"Stay."
But you didn’t.
You let me see it all.
And still decided
I wasn’t worth bringing
into the healed version of you.
 This Isn’t Your Fault
This isn’t your fault.
You never promised anything you didn’t give.
You never said you’d stay forever  
I just heard it in the way you breathed near me.
You never called me yours,
but I carved your name into every part of me
like love was something that could be claimed quietly,
without permission, without return.
You didn’t lie to me.
Not out loud.
I lied to myself
every time you showed up half-hearted and I called it devotion.
I created the fantasy.
I built the warmth.
You just stood still
while I wrapped you in a love you never asked for.
You never asked me to wait.
I chose to.
Waited for you to change,
waited for you to notice,
waited for you to love me like I loved you.
But that’s not on you.
That’s on me.
For thinking softness was a strategy,
for thinking if I stayed long enough, you’d meet me halfway.
You don’t owe me anything.
Not closure.
Not clarity.
Not a second chance.
You don’t have to explain why it faded.
I saw it.
In the way your hands stopped reaching,
in the way your eyes stopped settling when they looked at mine.
 Loving You Isn’t a Mistake
Loving you
isn’t a mistake.
Not the way
they say it is.
Not when I
still remember
the way you
sank into me.
Not when you
touched my face
like it held
your future.
Not when silence
from you
felt louder
than storms.
I chose you
with both hands,
before reason,
before logic.
I kept you
in soft places
when you only
brought sharp ones.
You never asked
for my devotion,
but you never
refused it either.
I filled
your silences
with warmth
you didn’t return.
I held
your bad days
without asking
for good ones.
You called
me kind
like it was
a curse.
You smiled
when I gave
everything,
but gave nothing.
And still
I don’t regret
what I did
for you.
Even now
when you’re gone
and colder
than ever.
Even now
when everyone says
you never
deserved me.
Even now
when I know
you never
looked back.
I loved you
too deeply
to ever
take it back.
And if
this was wrong,
I’d still
do it again.
Because loving you
was mine.
And it made
me real.
I’d Still Pick You
A poem for when you know better, but love still lives in your chest like a tenant who won’t move out.
Even after the silence,
after the unanswered calls,
after the slow fade,
after the version of me you left waiting with the lights on 
I’d still pick you.
Even knowing what I know now 
that your love came in half-measures,
that your promises didn’t stretch far enough to meet my needs,
that you only showed up when it served you 
I’d still pick you.
I’ve read the books.
Heard the advice.
Sat across from friends who loved me enough
to say, “You deserve more.”
They’re right.
But love isn’t always smart.
It’s not always safe.
Sometimes it just is 
loud, raw, loyal.
And mine still looks like you.
I’ve met people since.
Good people.
People who answer.
People who stay.
But they don’t crack me open
the way your absence still does.
They don’t make my chest ache
like your memory does at 3AM.
And that ache?
That’s how I know
I’m still yours
in all the places I swore I cleared out.
I’d still pick you
in a room full of choices,
in a world where everyone else chooses me first,
I’d still point to you and say, “That one. I want that one.”
Even if you don’t want me back.
Even if you’ve moved on.
Even if you’re someone else’s now 
I’d still want to be yours.
Not because you’re better.
Not because you earned it.
But because love doesn’t need a reason
when it’s this deep in the bones.
If you knocked right now,
I’d open the door like it never closed.
I’d smile like I didn’t write poems about the silence.
I’d say “Hey” like I didn’t die waiting for it.
I wish it weren’t true.
I wish I could say I’m past it.
That I wouldn’t make the same mistake twice.
But if love is a mistake then God, let me choose wrong again.
Let me choose you again.
Because in a world that made me question myself,
your name still feels like the only answer
that makes my heart sit still.
So yeah 
after everything,
even now 
I’d still pick you.
My Hopeless Romantic Homegirl
She got a playlist for every boy she meets.
Swears this time it’s different.
Said that about the last one,
the one before that,
and the one who stole her AirPods
and her peace of mind.
My hopeless romantic homegirl
beautiful chaos in a sundress,
lip gloss poppin’,
trauma locked under that Fenty highlighter.
She be falling in love like it’s on sale.
BOGO heartbreaks.
Buy one red flag, get two more for free.
She got a thing for emotionally unavailable men
with vague jobs
and full sleeves.
Tells me,
“He got potential.”
I’m like,
“Baby… so does nuclear war.”
She sends me screenshots of “good morning” texts
like they diamonds.
Writes his name in cursive in her Notes app.
Already got the baby names picked.
The wedding pinned.
The heartbreak preloaded.
She be loyal to delusion.
A soldier for situationships.
Gives her whole soul to men
who couldn’t spell “intimacy”
with Siri’s help.
And yet,
she’s the best kind of dangerous.
Loving like it’s her last breath,
dreaming like no man before ever lied,
texting paragraphs to someone
who responds with
“that’s crazy.”
I tried to warn her.
Said,
“Stop loving boys like you tryna fix your father.”
She said,
“I’m not.”
But her tears said different.
Her pattern said different.
Her therapist would’ve said the same
if she ever actually booked the session.
But I get it.
‘Cause sometimes,
she feels like the girl no one stays for.
Like she gotta perform softness
to be worthy of safety.
So she makes herself easy to love
for people who only want her easy.
Never her heavy.
Never her truth.
Never her shadow side.
But God, she got heart.
Too much heart.
So much that she lends it out like it’s a rental.
And when it comes back dented,
she just paints it pink and tries again.
Like loving hard is a religion.
And she’s the last believer.
Sometimes,
I think she’s the strongest one I know.
Other times,
I just wanna grab her face and say:
“Baby girl,
you don’t have to earn love by surviving it.”
But she wouldn’t listen.
She’d laugh,
pour another glass,
and talk about how he’s just “been through a lot.”
Like she ain’t been through more
and still showed up soft.
That’s the thing about my hopeless romantic homegirl:
She still believes.
Even after the late replies.
The lies.
The “I’m not ready for a relationship”
from a man who’s been ready to cheat since birth.
She still holds onto hope
like it’s the last balloon at the party.
And I love her for that.
Even if I hate how it breaks her.
Even if she swears this one’s the real thing
and next week she’ll be crying
on my shoulder again
to a Summer Walker soundtrack.
Still,
I’ll be there.
For the texts,
the rants,
the heartbreak dress in the cart.
Because behind all that pain
and performative poetry,
she’s the kind of woman
who deserves the very love
she keeps trying to give away.
And maybe, one day,
she’ll find it.
Or maybe,
she’ll finally realize
that all this time
she was the dream
she kept praying for in him
If You Called, I’d Still Answer
If you called,
I’d still answer.
I know.
I hate that about me too.
I could be mid-laugh,
mid-move-on,
mid-“I’m finally done,”
and still 
if your name showed up on my screen,
I'd pause the whole fucking world
to hear you say "Hey."
I’d pick up soft.
Like nothing happened.
Like you didn’t break me
into someone I had to learn to survive inside of.
I’d pick up like I wasn’t just talking to my friends
about how I finally let you go.
Like I didn’t just write a poem
about how I’d never
do this again.
If you called,
I’d pretend I wasn’t waiting.
Wouldn’t tell you that your name
still lives in my favorites
because I couldn’t bring myself
to drag you to the blocked list.
I wouldn’t ask why.
Wouldn’t say, “Why now?”
 Wouldn’t say, “What changed?”
 Because I know nothing did.
But my voice?
It would soften.
Drop an octave.
Like it remembers
how to speak to you
in a language I swore I’d forgotten.
If you called,
I’d tell myself,
“Just this once. Just to hear what they need. Just to be kind.”
But it’s never just.
Not with you.
It’s always everything.
If you called,
I’d remember your laugh
before I remembered the cold.
I’d remember your hands
before I remembered the way they let go.
If you called,
every door I boarded up
would creak open
like they never really meant it.
And I’d hate it.
Hate how easy it is.
Hate how familiar you still sound.
Hate that your voice
could still find the version of me
that made space for you
even in the ruins.
So no
I’m not proud.
I’m not healed in the way
that makes me unreachable.
But I’m real.
And the real truth?
If you called,
I’d still answer.
Even if I knew
you’d hang up
before I got the chance
to ask why you left
in the first place.
You Looked Back Once
You looked back once.
That’s all it took.
I was already breaking.
Already watching your back
get smaller
in a hallway that felt like goodbye.
But then 
you paused.
Not long.
Not with tears.
Just enough for me to think
maybe
this isn’t how it ends.
You looked back once.
And I built a whole future from it.
You blinked,
and I saw a maybe.
You hesitated,
and I heard an apology
you never gave.
I told my friends,
"She looked back."
 As if that meant something.
As if that wasn’t just your last scan
to make sure you left nothing behind.
You looked     mmmmnnbv like you were checking
to see if I was still standing.
And I was.
God, I was.
Standing there like a fool
who thought love could live
in a glance.
And maybe you don’t even remember it.
Maybe it meant nothing to you.
Maybe you were just checking traffic
on your way out.
But me?
I made it into a chapter.
You looked back once.
And I held on
for months.
One Day We’ll Laugh About This
One day,
we’ll laugh about this.
I’ll bump into you at some grocery store
you never used to shop at
and you’ll say my name
like it never left your mouth
all these years.
I’ll pretend I didn’t check your Instagram last night.
You’ll pretend you didn’t think about me
when that one song played.
We’ll smile.
It’ll be soft.
Like we’re both trying to make peace
with how much time we lost
not talking.
You’ll ask how I’ve been
and I’ll lie.
Say I’m good.
Say I’m happy.
Say I sleep fine.
And maybe I will be.
But not because I stopped loving you.
Because I stopped waiting
for you to say it back.
We’ll stand there,
cart handles between us,
and you’ll say something dumb like,
"Remember that time we fought for three hours
about nothing?"
And I’ll laugh.
But in my chest,
I’ll still remember every second.
What I wore.
What I said that cracked.
How I begged with my silence
for you to just choose me.
But I’ll laugh.
Because maybe now
you finally would.
Maybe in this version of the timeline,
we’re grown.
We’re steady.
We know how to stay.
Maybe this time
you don’t flinch when I ask for more.
Maybe this time
you hold my hand in public
and mean it.
One day,
we’ll look back
and talk about “the old days”
like they weren’t the thing
that almost ended us.
I’ll tell you how I almost broke.
You’ll tell me how you were scared.
And we’ll laugh
because it’s easier than crying
over something we already survived.
We’ll sit in some corner booth.
You’ll order something sweet.
I’ll pretend I forgot your favorite drink
just to see if you remember mine.
You will.
Of course you will.
We’ll talk like time never happened.
Like healing didn’t take years.
Like I didn’t write a hundred poems
trying to get you out of my bloodstream.
And you’ll say,
"I always loved you, you know."
And I’ll pretend
I didn’t wait my whole fucking life
to hear it.
And then we’ll laugh.
Because what else is left to do
with all this pain
except rewrite the ending?
Hopeless Romantic
another way to say “I loved harder than I should have.”
Why do I even love? I hate being the hopeless romantic,
the one who sees “maybe” and calls it magic,
who watches people walk away without a fight,
and still thinks about what he could’ve said that night.
I hate how I get my hopes up over nothing at all,
how one reply makes me forget every ignored call,
how I give people the benefit of the doubt
long after they’ve shown me what they’re about.
I hate that I fall for people who leave things unclear,
who speak in circles and disappear,
who act like I’m a secret they’re afraid to hold,
but still keep me warm enough to keep me cold.
I hate how I always try to understand their side,
how I explain away disrespect and still stay tied,
how I think patience will earn me more than regret,
how I lose sleep over texts they forget to send or never meant.
I hate that I replay conversations that weren’t even deep,
that I make meaning from moments they won’t keep,
that I hold space for them in the middle of my chest,
and call it love when it’s really just unrest.
I hate that I remember everything they said in passing,
while they probably forgot I even asked them,
that I carry their name like I was told to,
that I treat small kindness like a breakthrough.
I hate how I’m still loyal to what could’ve been,
how I go quiet instead of speaking what’s within,
how I shrink so they don’t feel boxed in,
how I make myself simple just to be let in.
I hate how they call me sweet and then leave,
how I get thanked for loving right before they cleave,
how I’m always a good man, just not the one,
how I’m almost loved, but never done.
But I love how I don’t stop.
How I still love, even when it’s not returned on top.
How I show up even when they don’t ask,
how I tell the truth, even behind a mask.
I love that I care without making them earn it,
that I trust until they burn it,
that I give without keeping score,
that I open my heart even when I’m unsure.
I love that I still believe people mean well,
that I still hope when it’s easier to dwell,
that I don’t let pain change my core,
that I walk back into the fire knowing what’s in store.
I love that I say how I feel, even if it’s too much,
that I don’t play games, that I stay in touch,
that I don’t make people chase me to feel wanted,
that I keep my word even when I feel haunted.
I love that I don’t need to be loved back to love hard,
that I give fully even when they disregard,
that I don’t perform, that I don’t pretend,
that my heart shows up   beginning to end.
I love that I still answer messages with grace,
that I hold kindness even in empty space,
that I’m still warm even when they go cold,
that I never let bitterness take hold.
I love that I can be alone without hardening,
that I don’t hate women because one left me hurting,
that I still wish them well when they move on,
that I don’t need revenge to feel strong.
I love that I don’t ghost, that I say goodbye,
that I don’t leave people wondering why,
that I give closure even when I don’t get it,
that I walk away without making them regret it.
I love that I’d rather be soft than right,
that I don’t weaponize silence or spite,
that I stay the same even after goodbye,
that I don’t fake peace   I cry when I cry.
I love that I don’t fall for the games,
that I don’t run from people when it gets strange,
that I talk things through, that I listen deep,
that I try, and I keep trying, even when it cuts me steep.
I love that I don’t take people for granted,
that I mean what I say and stand planted,
that I don’t flinch when they get distant,
that I show up, even if it’s inconsistent.
I love that I loved you  
even if you didn’t love me too.
That I did it clean, did it raw, did it true,
and never made you feel small for walking through.
I love that I keep my word even in heartbreak,
that I still love even when it’s a mistake,
that I still look at people and believe,
even when there’s no reason to grieve.
I love that I leave with dignity and depth,
that I say “thank you” even under my breath,
that I carry every version of us in peace,
that I let go without breaking piece by piece.
I love that I’m not jaded, just bruised,
not angry, just a little used,
still full of wonder even in the hurt,
still choosing love, even when it turns to dirt.
So yeah. I’m the hopeless romantic.
And yeah, sometimes it’s tragic.
But I’d rather be this
than someone who forgot what love is.
Author’s Note
I didn’t plan for these poems to become a book. They started as raw moments, messy truths scribbled when the quiet got too heavy and love got too loud. At first, they were private reflections, snapshots of emotions I didn’t know how else to handle
  written late at night, early in the morning, or during those long afternoons when silence became deafening. They’re honest, chaotic, and far from neat  because love, at least the kind I know, has never been neat or predictable.
If you're reading this, maybe you understand exactly what I'm talking about. Maybe you've experienced the sting of holding onto someone who slipped away anyway, felt the ache of waiting for messages that never come, or navigated the quiet heartbreak of being overlooked. 
Maybe you've been the one left standing in the aftermath, questioning yourself, your worth, and why love feels more like chaos than peace. This collection is for those moments  the longing, the frustration, the stubborn hope that refuses to fade, and the courage required to keep your heart open even after it's been bruised.
These poems aren't instructions or neatly packaged lessons, and they certainly aren’t solutions. They're simply fragments of my truth  pieces of myself laid bare. I wrote them to make sense of what I was feeling, to navigate the confusion, pain, and sometimes the unexpected joy hidden beneath it all.
 But now they belong to you as well. If these words resonate with you, know you're not alone in what you're feeling. Your emotions aren't exaggerated, and your heart isn't foolish for desiring something authentic, even when authenticity comes with risk.
Thank you for being here, for sharing this vulnerability with me, for feeling this journey alongside me. It means more than you know.
  Kiminou












Acknowledgments
To the ones who inspired these poems some blurred into composites, others painfully clear thank you for the honesty, even when it hurt. Especially when it hurt. To my family and friends who held space for me, quietly believing I’d find my way even when I was busy losing myself your patience was a safety net I pretended not to need until the fall became too real. To the voices that pushed me to go deeper, to bleed onto the page without apology your insistence on truth forced me to confront myself, and this collection is better for it.
To anyone reading who sees themselves reflected here whether you're caught in the tenderness of early hope or the ache of later heartbreak I see you. I was you. In many ways, I'm still you. We're all just searching, stumbling, and learning to love in a world that makes it easier to hide.
And finally, to myself the version I was, the person I am now, and the one I'm still growing into grace. Always grace. Keep going. The story isn’t over yet.
 Kiminou








                                 About the Author




Kiminou Knox is a writer, poet, athlete, and creative force from East Palo Alto, California. At just 18, his work already explores deep and powerful themes of identity, love, legacy, spirituality, and the emotional realities that shape young Black experiences. Known for his sharp yet introspective voice, Kiminou debuted with Poems from a Black Boy, a collection praised for its raw honesty, emotional vulnerability, and unfiltered reflection of Black youth and life. His sophomore release, Spirit of Solomon, further establishes him as a thoughtful storyteller, delving into complex narratives of resilience, masculinity, emotional honesty, and the complicated interplay between love and self-discovery.
Beyond poetry, Kiminou balances his artistic pursuits with the discipline of a multi-sport athlete and the entrepreneurial vision behind creative ventures such as his clothing brand, The TeeShirtTeens. Committed to using his voice and platform for authentic storytelling, he consistently pushes boundaries and challenges norms, aiming to resonate deeply with readers and inspire honest conversations.
As Kiminou continues toward a future in literature and sports journalism, his creative mission remains clear: to leave an impact, speak truths others shy away from, and craft narratives that demand to be heard, felt, and remembered.
